# VibeHammer Development Plan

Goal for V1: a Windows `.exe` where two people play Intercessor Squad (5) vs Termagants (10) hotseat on a
24"x24" board, through the full turn sequence (Command, Movement, Shooting, Charge, Fight, End of Turn),
following the 11th edition core rules. The rules come from `docs/rules/` and the units from `data/`.

Architecture, conventions and rules-lookup policy are in `CLAUDE.md`. This file is the order of work.

## How to run each milestone

1. `/clear`, then set the effort level shown for the milestone (e.g. `/effort high`).
2. If the milestone says **Plan mode**, enter plan mode (Shift+Tab) and paste the prompt. Review the design before approving.
3. Paste the prompt. Claude reads the listed rule files first.
4. The milestone is done when every acceptance check passes and `ctest` is green. Then ask Claude to commit.

### Effort levels

| Level | Use for |
|---|---|
| `medium` | Scaffolding, wiring, UI layout, data plumbing: well-specified work with few rule subtleties. |
| `high` | Ordinary rules implementation: one rule area with clear text and good test coverage. |
| `xhigh` | Rule areas with many interacting clauses, "if possible" constraints, or alternating-player flow. |
| `max` | Core architecture decisions that everything else builds on. Use it sparingly. |

If your Claude Code version doesn't have `/effort`, put "think hard" (for high) or "ultrathink" (for xhigh/max) in the prompt.

## M1: Build skeleton ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `medium` · **Plan mode:** no

> Read CLAUDE.md. Create the CMake project: `CMakeLists.txt`, `CMakePresets.json` (configure preset `default` using the "Visual Studio 18 2026" generator, x64; build/test presets `debug` and `release`), and the three targets `wh40k_core` (static lib), `wh40k_client_2d` (exe), `wh40k_tests` (GoogleTest, registered with CTest via `gtest_discover_tests`). Fetch raylib, Dear ImGui, rlImGui, nlohmann_json and GoogleTest with FetchContent, pinned to exact release tags. Core gets `/W4 /WX /permissive-`. Add a placeholder core function with one test, and a client that opens an 1280x800 raylib window with an ImGui demo panel. Add a CMake check that fails the build if any file under `src/core` includes raylib or imgui headers.

**Acceptance**
- `cmake --preset default && cmake --build --preset debug && ctest --preset debug` succeeds from a clean clone.
- The client window opens and shows an ImGui panel.
- Adding `#include <raylib.h>` to a core file breaks the build.

## M2: Core types, data loading, serialization ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** yes
**Rules:** 01.01–01.03, 02.01–02.07

> Read CLAUDE.md and rules 01 and 02. Design the core data model: `Side`, strong IDs, `Characteristics` (M, T, Sv, InSv, W, Ld, OC; nullable where the datasheet shows '-'), `WeaponProfile` (range or melee, A as fixed or dice expression like D3, BS/WS nullable for Torrent, S, AP, D, parsed abilities such as `RapidFire{1}`, `Assault`, `Heavy`, `CloseQuarters`, `Blast{1}`, `TwinLinked`), `Weapon` (one or more profiles; each model picks its own profile, see D-001 in `docs/decisions.md`; random characteristics are parsed into a `DiceExpr` but never rolled, see D-002), `Model` (position, base, wounds remaining, weapons), `Unit` (side, keywords, models, per-turn flags), `GameState`. Write loaders for `data/units/*.json` and `data/scenarios/v1_skirmish.json` that produce an initial `GameState` with both units deployed using the scenario's `formation` (D-008) inside their deployment zones. The loader must accept the data quirks as they are (don't hand-edit the unit files): skill as an integer, `null` or the string `"N/A"` (Torrent weapons); A as an integer or a dice string (`"D3"`, `"D6"`); `"Pistol"` loads as Close-quarters (24.27); `"Blast"` with no number is `Blast{1}` (24.05), `"Blast 1"` likewise; `"Rapid Fire 1"` is `RapidFire{1}`. Give every type JSON round-trip support and equality. Don't add game flow yet.

**Acceptance**
- Loading the scenario gives 5 Intercessors (32 mm bases, M6 T5 Sv3+ W2 Ld6+ OC2) and 10 Termagants (28.5 mm, M6 T3 Sv5+ W1 Ld8+ OC2) with the right weapons.
- Bolt Rifle loads with both profiles (Focused Fire: Heavy, Rapid Fire 1; Saturation: Assault, Rapid Fire 1).
- `GameState` → JSON → `GameState` round-trips equal. Tests cover unknown weapon abilities, which should load as `Unknown{text}` and not throw.
- The loaded formation matches D-008: Intercessors in one line of 5, Termagants in two ranks of 5, 1" gaps, front ranks touching their zone fronts (front lines exactly 12" apart). Both units are in coherency and wholly within their zones. A test formation that breaks coherency (e.g. 10 Termagants in 1 rank) is rejected with an exception.
- Strangleweb (skill `"N/A"`, A `"D6"`) and Termagant spinefist (`"Pistol"`) load without error.

## M3: Geometry ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** no
**Rules:** 01.04, 03.01–03.04

> Read CLAUDE.md (Geometry section) and rules 01.04 and 03. Implement `src/core/geometry/`: base-to-base distance, the tolerance helpers (`within`, `more_than`, `in_base_contact`), coherency check (03.03, which returns the offending models), engagement checks (03.04, model and unit level), path length for segment lists, path validation (cannot cross enemy bases, cannot leave the battlefield, can pass through friendly bases; the end position must not overlap any base), and "closest model / closest distance between units" ("Closest Model/Unit" and "Within/Wholly Within" are app-only rules; their wording is recorded in D-009 and D-010). Write tests in inches with explicit coordinates, including edge cases at exactly 2", 2"+ε and 9".

> Note: M2 already added `geometry/units.hpp`, `base_distance`, `within`/`more_than`, `wholly_within` (D-009) and a basic `check_coherency` for the loader. Extend and test them here; don't duplicate them.

**Acceptance**
- Coherency handles the 2"-of-one and 9"-of-all clauses independently (test a line of 6 Intercessors, 32 mm bases, with a 1.9" edge-to-edge gap between neighbours: the 2" clause passes, and the 9" clause fails because the end models are about 14.5" apart edge-to-edge. Also test the reverse: models within 9" of every other model but one model more than 2" from all the others).
- A path that clips an enemy base by 0.5 mm is rejected. A path through a friendly base is accepted, but ending on it is rejected.

## M4: Dice and the attack sequence (pure functions) ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** no
**Rules:** 01.05–01.07, 04.01–04.03, 05.01–05.04, 06.02, 06.03, 24.04, 24.07, 24.16, 24.27, 24.30, 24.37

> Read CLAUDE.md, D-006 in `docs/decisions.md`, and rules 01.05–01.07, 04, 05, 06 and the listed 24.xx abilities. Batch identical attacks per D-006 (compare attack-time abilities only). Implement leadership and battle-shock rolls (01.06, 01.07) as pure functions too; M7 (Desperate Escape) needs them before M11 wires up the Command phase. Implement `DiceSource` (seeded PRNG with state in GameState) and `ScriptedDice`. Implement the attack sequence as pure functions over a snapshot: gather attack dice with identical-attack batching (04.03), Rapid Fire within half range, hit rolls (unmodified 1 fails, unmodified 6 critical, Heavy +1 when eligible; "Modifying Dice Rolls" is an app-only rule that isn't in docs/rules, so ask me how to cap modifiers before implementing it), wound rolls against the S vs T table, allocation groups and allocation order (05.03), save rolls resolved lowest to highest against the current group, prioritising wounded models (05.04), damage, destruction, mortal wounds (06.02) and hazard rolls (06.03). Where the opposing side must choose (allocation order, which model in a group), expose that choice as an input parameter for now. M5 turns it into a PendingDecision.

**Acceptance**
- With scripted dice, 5 Intercessors firing Saturation at 10 Termagants within 12" roll 15 attack dice (A2 + Rapid Fire 1 within half range = 3 per model, 24.30). Hits, wounds (S5 vs T3 → 3+) and saves (5+ with AP-1 → needs 6) match a hand-calculated expected result.
- Termagant Fleshborer vs Intercessor: S5 vs T5 → 4+. Damage carries correctly onto a wounded model first.
- Excess damage on a destroyed model is lost, not spilled (05.04). There's a test for this.
- Per D-006, Bolt pistol attacks and Bolt Rifle Saturation attacks from different models at the same target are gathered as one batch. Focused Fire attacks are a separate batch, and Focused Fire with and without the Heavy bonus are separate batches.
- Whatever modifier-cap behaviour I confirm is recorded in `docs/decisions.md` and tested.

## M5: Game flow, PendingDecision and the action engine ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `max` · **Plan mode:** yes
**Rules:** 01.03, 07.01–07.03, 08.01–08.05 (structure only), 09.01–09.03, 10.01–10.03, 11.01–11.03, 12.01–12.04 (structure only)

> Read CLAUDE.md (Engine API) and rules 07 to 12, focusing on the sequence structure of each phase. Design and implement the state machine: battle round → turns → phases → steps, with `PendingDecision` as the single source of "who must act and how". Define the `GameAction` and `GameEvent` variants, `legal_actions`, `validate_action` and `apply_action`, the active-side switching from 01.03 (`legal_actions` returns full actions for discrete decisions and action templates for continuous ones, per D-004 in `docs/decisions.md`), an event log, and replay (initial state + action list → final state). Phases may be stubs that only let units "remain stationary / not shoot / not charge / pass", but the full turn loop for 5 battle rounds must run. Hook the M4 attack functions through PendingDecisions for the opposing side's allocation choices. In the plan, decide how to keep 05.04 "Select Model" from prompting once per failed save (10 Termagants can mean 10 prompts per volley): for example skip the prompt when only one model is legal, and/or accept a full casualty order in one action. Also include the D-007 game end (immediate end on wipe-out). Apply D-012 (attacks against a target that is no longer eligible are lost, never re-targeted) when attack batches are resolved through the engine.

**Acceptance** (amended 2026-11-02: M5 phases are stubs, so a game is ~30 forced actions with no templates until M7-M10 add decisions)
- A test plays 5 battle rounds of all-pass actions and reaches `GameOver` (a draw, D-007).
- D-004 property: random play over many seeds, to GameOver or 200 actions (whichever comes first). Every fully specified action from `legal_actions` is accepted by `validate_action`; template fills (the D-013 casualty order, from attack-flow states) get a deterministic, rule-cited result. The check gets richer as later milestones add decisions.
- Every random game, replayed from its initial state, reproduces an identical final `GameState` JSON, and a replay file under `tests/replays/` passes.
- Submitting an action for the wrong side, or out of phase, is rejected with a rule-cited reason.
- 05.03/05.04 choices reach the opposing side as PendingDecisions, with at most one casualty prompt per attack batch (D-013). D-012 and D-007 are tested.

## M7: Movement phase ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** no
**Rules:** 03.01–03.03, 09.01–09.07, 06.03

> Read rules 03 and 09, and D-005 in `docs/decisions.md`. Implement Remain Stationary, Normal Move, Advance Move (D6 roll as an event), and Fall-back Move with both Ordered Retreat and Desperate Escape (hazard rolls before moving, can move through enemy models, battle-shock roll after). Units move model by model (`ProposeModelMove` with a segment path), then `CommitUnitMove`, which validates distance, path, coherency, engagement and the 'After Moving' conditions, or reverts every model (03.01). A reverted unit has not been selected to make that move and can be selected again (03 sidebar, "If you cannot set up a unit"), but dice already rolled stand (D-005). Desperate Escape's battle-shock roll needs "Multiple Battle-shock Rolls", an app-only rule: ask me for the wording if it comes up. Track per-turn flags (advanced, fell back, moved more than 3" for Heavy). The active player must select every unit (09.02 and its "Selecting units to move" sidebar).

**Acceptance**
- Tests cover each move type's eligibility, the distance limit (M, or M + D6 for an advance), ending engaged on a normal move being rejected, and incoherent ends reverting all models.
- Desperate Escape through an enemy base is legal, and Ordered Retreat through one is not.
- D-005: after a reverted Advance, selecting Advance again reuses the same roll (no new dice consumed). After a reverted Desperate Escape, its mortal wounds stand and no new hazard rolls are made.

## M8: Shooting phase ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** no
**Rules:** 06.01, 10.01–10.06, 04.01–04.02, 24.04, 24.07, 24.16, 24.27, 24.30

> Read rules 04, 06.01 and 10. Implement shooting type selection (Normal, Assault, Close-quarters; Indirect is out of scope), weapon and profile selection per model (Bolt Rifle mode, chosen independently per model per D-001, Close-quarters weapons excluding other ranged weapons for non-MONSTER/VEHICLE models), target eligibility (visible, in range, unengaged for non-close-quarters), and resolution through the M4 attack sequence and the M5 decision flow (the Shoot action calls `begin_attacks` in `engine/attack_flow.hpp`). Visibility is model-to-model line of sight with a 1 mm-wide line, blocked only by models of other units (there's no terrain in V1).

**Acceptance**
- A unit that advanced can only use Assault shooting, with [ASSAULT] weapons only. [ASSAULT] is checked per profile: after an Advance a Bolt Rifle may fire Saturation but not Focused Fire. A unit that fell back cannot shoot.
- Engaged Intercessors can use Close-quarters shooting with Bolt pistols at the unit they're engaged with. Engaged Termagants (no [CLOSE-QUARTERS] weapons) can't shoot. In Normal shooting each Intercessor fires either its Bolt pistol or its Bolt Rifle, not both (24.07).
- Heavy applies only when no model moved more than 3" and the unit is unengaged.
- The Termagants can shoot from 18" but not from 18.1".

## M9: Charge phase ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** no
**Rules:** 11.01–11.04, 24.13

> Read rule 11. Implement charge eligibility (unengaged, within 12" of an enemy, didn't advance or fall back), target selection, the 2D6 charge roll, and the charge move with its constraints: end closer to a target, end engaged with all selected targets and no other enemy units, coherency, distance ≤ roll. Mark the "within 1" if possible / engaged if possible" clauses with `TODO(if-possible)` per CLAUDE.md. A successful charge grants Fights First until end of turn. A failed charge leaves the unit where it was.

**Acceptance**
- Charge targets must be within 12" **and within the charge roll** (11.04 Before Moving), measured edge-to-edge. That is a stricter test than whether the unit can reach engagement, which needs only (distance − 2"). Tests:
  - An enemy at 11.9" with a roll of 11: no charge target can be selected, so the charge fails and the models don't move.
  - An enemy at 9.9" with a roll of 9: the target can't be selected either, even though a 9" move would reach within 2". This guards against the 10th-edition reach check.
  - An enemy at 9.9" with a roll of 10: the target is selectable and the charge succeeds.
  - A roll of 2 never succeeds (11 sidebar "Failed charges").
- A charge that would end engaged with a non-target enemy unit is rejected (add a second Termagant unit in the test scenario for this).

---

## M10: Fight phase ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** yes
**Rules:** 12.01–12.09, 04.02 (splitting melee attacks), Appendix "Passing in the Fight Phase"

> Read rule 12 and the Rules Appendix. Plan the alternation flow first. Implement pile-in (3", models in base contact can't move, end closer to the closest pile-in target, end engaged), the Fight step with the Fights First Combats and Remaining Combats sub-steps alternating as written, Normal Fight vs Overrun Fight (extra 3" pile-in), melee weapon selection (one per model, splitting attacks across engaged targets; resolved through `begin_attacks`), and consolidation with the three priority modes (Ongoing, Engaging, Objective; V1 has no objectives). A unit that meets no mode's conditions can't consolidate (09 sidebar "Selecting modes"). An Engaging Consolidation that ends engaged with enemy units that haven't been selected to fight forces the opponent to select each of them to fight (12.08 After Moving). The flow must support fights after the Fight step has ended. Implement fight-phase passing from the appendix ("Eligible to fight, but unable to fight"). A player may pass only if all of their eligible units are more than 5" from all enemy units. The Fight step ends when both players pass in succession, or when one passes and the opponent has no eligible units. Per the appendix FAQ, an Overrun Fight requires the unit to still be eligible to fight.

**Acceptance**
- After a successful Intercessor charge, the Intercessors fight first. The alternation then continues per 12.04, which a test asserts step by step.
- A unit that is no longer engaged when selected makes an Overrun Fight.
- Consolidation picks the mandatory mode by priority, and a test covers each reachable mode.
- An Engaging Consolidation into an enemy unit that hasn't fought makes that unit fight (12.08), and a test asserts the forced selection.
- Passing is rejected while an eligible unit is within 5" of an enemy, and accepted otherwise.

---

## M11: Command phase, battle-shock, end of turn, game end ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** no
**Rules:** 08.01–08.05, 03.03 (Regaining Coherency), Appendix (Starting Strength, Half-strength); uses the 01.06/01.07 roll functions from M4

> Read rules 01.06–01.07, 08 and 03.03, and the appendix entries on starting strength and half-strength. Reuse the M4 leadership/battle-shock roll functions. Implement CP gain (track CP; no stratagems yet), the battle-shock step for units below half-strength or already battle-shocked, battle-shock effects (OC becomes '-', blocks Ordered Retreat), the End of Turn coherency removal (the owning side picks models via PendingDecision, no on-death triggers), and the game end and result per D-007 (an immediate win on wipe-out; otherwise a draw after battle round 5), emitted as a `GameOver` event.

**Acceptance**
- 10 Termagants reduced to 5 at the start of their Command phase must take a battle-shock test (5 is at half), and 6 don't.
- 5 Intercessors (an odd starting strength, so they can never be *at* half-strength): with 3 left there's no test, and with 2 left (below half) they test.
- A battle-shocked unit tests every Command phase until it passes, and passing clears battle-shock (08.03). Both players gain 1 CP in every Command phase (08.02).
- D-007: wiping out a side ends the game immediately with a win. Reaching the end of round 5 with both sides alive gives a draw.
- An incoherent unit at End of Turn has models removed until it's coherent.

## M12: Interactive client ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** yes

> Read CLAUDE.md. Make the M6 viewer playable via the engine API only. Clicking a model selects its unit, and clicking a model in the selected unit selects that model. Model drag builds a segment path, with a ghost marker at the start position and a live inch ruler. Add a "Move unit" handle that translates the whole unit and becomes per-model paths. Overlays (toggleable): 2"/9" coherency rings for the selected unit, red 2" engagement rings around enemies, weapon range rings, and charge distance after the roll. Add an ImGui decision panel generated from `PendingDecision` and `legal_actions` (no hard-coded phase buttons), Shooting and fight selections default to one weapon profile and target for the whole unit, which the client expands into per-model choices; selecting a single model overrides just that model (D-001). Commit/Reset Move, validation failures shown with their rule-cited reasons, and a dice log with per-roll results.

**Acceptance**
- A full V1 game can be played hotseat from battle round 1 (scenario formation, D-003) to GameOver using only the mouse.
- In one shooting activation, the unit-wide default plus a single-model override produce a `GameAction` with mixed Bolt Rifle profiles, and the engine accepts it.
- The client source contains no rules logic: every legality question goes through `validate_action` or `legal_actions`.

## M14: Close formation and no-overlap model moves ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `medium` · **Plan mode:** yes (agreed 2026-11-05)

> Start both squads in base contact (D-008: `gap_in` 0 in `v1_skirmish.json`) and reject a single model's proposed move that would end on another model (03.01 Ending a Move, checked per proposal: D-027). The "Move unit" handle orders its proposals so a touching unit can still be translated in any direction. Regenerate the replays for the new start positions.

**Acceptance**

## M15: Client UI improvements ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `medium` · **Plan mode:** yes (agreed 2026-11-05)

> Make the client easier to read and use (D-028): scale the UI to the monitor's DPI with a UI scale slider and open maximized; replace the movable windows with a fixed sidebar (Decision, Game, Log, Files) and a phase bar across the top of the board (round, whose turn, the phase track, the current step and who is being waited for); draw each ring overlay only when it is relevant, with a colour legend; replace the wound number with pips on damaged models. Client only: no engine changes.

**Acceptance**
- At the start of a game no rings are drawn. Engagement and coherency rings appear while models are being moved, weapon ranges while choosing shots, the charge distance after a charge roll; each can be switched off.
- The phase bar shows the battle round, the turn side, the current phase highlighted in the Command → Fight track and the current step; it says who is being waited for when that is not the turn side.
- The panels cannot be moved or resized; the UI scale slider resizes text and widgets.
- Models at full wounds carry no number; damaged models show pips.
- Overlapping rings of one kind draw as one border; a dragged model's rings follow the cursor, with a line from where it started; hovering a ring explains it.

## M16: Click-on-the-board interaction
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** yes (agreed 2026-11-05)

> Pick units on the board instead of with sidebar buttons (D-029): click the unit to move and choose its move type from a menu; click the shooter, then the target, then a weapon; click to declare a charge and then its target; click to pile in, consolidate or fight. Units that can't be clicked now are drawn slightly grey. The sidebar keeps the step-level buttons (End shooting, Pass, Commit move, ...) and a hint. Client only: every choice is one of `legal_actions`.

**Acceptance**
- At the start only the Intercessors are clickable (the Termagants are dark grey); clicking them offers Normal and Advance. Remain stationary is the "End Movement phase" button, which keeps every remaining unit stationary.
- Grey appears only while the board waits for a click; with no unit left to act, the panel shows only the end-phase button.
- After picking a shooter, the panel asks for a target and a line follows the cursor from the shooter.
- Clicking the shooter then the target offers each weapon choice that can reach it; picking one fires, and the engine accepts it.
- A full game can be played to GameOver with board clicks, menus, step buttons, drags and the melee/casualty editors only (logic-level test).

## M20: Dice roll pop-ups ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `medium` · **Plan mode:** yes (agreed 2026-11-05)

Presentation only: the engine has already rolled when the action is applied, and the cards come from its events (Option 1; Option 2 is under Later).

**Acceptance**
- Each hit, wound, save, advance, charge, hazard and battle-shock roll an action makes gets a card, in order, in the colour of the side rolling (saves: the defender).
- A card's dice and pass/fail match the engine's events exactly (logic test).
- While a card is open, the board, bars and log stay at the moment of the roll and the action bar shows who is rolling, in the pop-up's colour; the board waits. The "Show dice pop-ups" setting in the sidebar turns them off.
- No cards for replays, loads, undo or rolls made before the session started.

## M31: Skip pile-ins and consolidations too small to matter ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `medium` · **Plan mode:** yes (agreed 2026-11-06)

> "Skip steps with nothing to do" (D-036) also covers the Pile In and Consolidate steps (12.02, 12.03, 12.06, 12.07, 12.08) when no unit's move is worth making (D-047): every model is in base contact or can end at most 2 mm from where it started, and no model could end engaged with an enemy unit it isn't engaged with now. The client then ends the step (or skips the overrun pile-in) itself, as for D-036. Moving is the player's choice (12.02 / 12.07: "units they choose to move"), so no rule changes. A read-only core query previews the move's start so `move_reach` (D-041) gives each model's area.

**Acceptance**
- `preview_fight_move` returns the state with the pile-in or consolidation in progress, nullopt for an invalid start, and leaves the input unchanged (core tests).
- Every model in base contact, or boxed in to 2 mm or less: the step ends by itself; with room for 3 mm it doesn't (logic tests).
- A move under 2 mm that could bring a model into engagement with a new enemy unit is not skipped; nor is an Engaging Consolidation that can reach its target (logic tests).
- A pointless overrun pile-in is skipped with `SkipPileIn`; with the setting off nothing is skipped; one Undo removes the skip with the player's last action (logic tests).
- No new replay: a skip is an ordinary `EndPileIns` / `EndConsolidations` / `SkipPileIn` step, which the existing replays already cover; they stay byte-identical. (Logic test: the history with the skip rebuilds to the same state.)

## M32: Near base contact counts for step skipping ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `low` · **Plan mode:** yes (agreed 2026-11-06)

> House rule (D-048, to review later), client only: for "Skip steps with nothing to do", a model within 0.05" of an enemy model in a pile-in (12.03) or Ongoing Consolidation (12.08) counts as in base contact, so as not moving, because a mouse drag can't reach the exact contact you get by pushing models together on a table. The engine keeps exact base contact, so with skipping off such a model may still move closer. D-048 also records an open question for later: "closer to the closest unit" lets a model slide round to a different enemy model.

**Acceptance**

- A pile-in whose only movable model is 1 mm from an enemy is skipped (logic test).
- Existing tests and replays pass unchanged.

## M33: Skip moves that can only get marginally closer ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `low` · **Plan mode:** yes (agreed 2026-11-06)

> "Skip steps with nothing to do" judges a pile-in or consolidation by how much closer a model can get to the closest target unit, not how far it can travel (D-049, extends D-047). A model whose starting gap to that unit is 0.1" or less can't gain more than that, so it doesn't count. The engine is unchanged.

**Acceptance**
- A model 1.8 mm from the unit with room to slide ~12 mm is skipped; at just over 0.1" it isn't (logic tests).
- The usual pile-in after a charge, the 3 mm pocket and the new-contact case are still offered; replays unchanged.

## M34: Auto move for pile-ins and consolidations ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** yes (agreed 2026-11-06)

> An **Auto move** button next to Commit / Reset places a pile-in or consolidation (12.03, 12.08) for the player to review and commit (D-050). It is a greedy placement: each model goes to the reachable spot closest to the target, with ties within 1 mm going to more friends within 2". Coherency (03.03) is kept after every placement, both model orders are tried and the better legal one is kept. It is client-only: ordinary `ProposeModelMove`s submitted as one Undo group, each validated by the engine.

**Acceptance**
- After a charge, the pile-in is legal and puts at least 3 Intercessors in base contact; an Ongoing Consolidation is legal and gains contact; an Engaging Consolidation ends engaged with its target (logic tests).
- A model 1.8 mm away goes straight into contact (D-049), not round to another Termagant; a pocketed model with 3 mm room ends in contact; a unit all in base contact gets "no model can get closer" (logic tests).
- Coherency holds after every proposal, and a rear model moving first lets the front one reach contact; among equally close spots, the one within 2" of two friends wins (logic tests).
- Offered only for pile-ins and consolidations, before any drag; one Undo group; the history replays; the same input gives the same output (logic tests). Replays unchanged.

## M35: Pick casualties on the board ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** yes (agreed 2026-11-06)

> With "Allocate damage automatically" off, the defender clicks the models that take the damage, one at a time (D-051). Only legal models can be picked; each click shows what it does. A picked model keeps taking the damage forced onto it (05.04 / 06.02), and no model takes damage without a click, even a forced one. Then "Confirm casualties" (or "Undo pick", or "Use default order"). The engine now always asks for casualties when damage is inflicted (amends D-013); the answer is still one `OrderCasualties`.

**Acceptance**
- A forced casualty is still asked for; all saves passed means no decision (core tests).
- `preview_casualties`: the options are the current group (wounded models first) and a forced model still needs its click. A picked model keeps its forced damage (W2, and W3 with another wounded model). Illegal or surplus picks give nothing, later allocation groups wait, and excess attacks are counted. Mortal wounds work the same. The preview equals the real resolution (core tests).
- The board handles the decision: models that can't be picked are grey and can't be clicked; marks show each click's damage; Undo pick; Confirm only when done, giving the previewed casualties; "Use default order" is the unit order; automatic allocation still answers at once (logic tests).
- Replays regenerated on purpose: byte-identical.

## M36: Fix illegal moves ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** yes (agreed 2026-11-06)

> A new setting, **Fix illegal moves** (on by default, D-052), client only. A drag the engine would refuse goes to the nearest spot it can go, shown while dragging:
> - too far: stops at the maximum, along the drag;
> - off the battlefield: stops at the edge;
> - on another model: stops touching it;
> - a charge, pile-in or consolidation that doesn't end closer: goes to the nearest spot that does.
>
> A line into or through an enemy model stops along the drag, before that model (base contact), and is never routed round it. The fix only moves a model by up to 1" sideways. Anything further goes back along the drag instead.
>
> Engagement Range is never fixed: a model may move through it, and ending in it is left for Commit to check, as now (user's choice). The same goes for coherency and the other rules about the whole unit. Each fixed drop is an ordinary `ProposeModelMove`, checked by the engine.

**Acceptance**
- Engagement Range is left alone: a drop within 2" of an enemy, a drag through it, a charge within 2" of a non-target, and a pile-in that leaves it are all untouched. A Fall Back dragged too far stops at its maximum (logic tests).
- Along the drag: too far ends exactly at the maximum, and a second line uses only the distance left. A charge or Normal drag onto an enemy base ends in base contact, on the line. A gap too narrow for the base stops the model before it. A drop on a friendly model's centre stops on the near side (logic tests).
- Nearest spot: off the edge stays at the edge; a glancing drop on a friend touches it beside the drop; a pile-in that isn't closer goes to the nearest closer spot, and onto the target gives base contact (logic tests).
- A legal drop is untouched; no legal spot gives no fix; a whole-unit drag too far or off the edge is accepted for every model (logic tests).
- Over grids of drops (Normal, charge, pile-in), every fix is accepted by the engine and is within 1" of the drop or on the drag line, and the same input gives the same output (logic tests). Replays unchanged.

---

**Effort:** `medium` · **Plan mode:** yes (agreed 2026-11-06)

## Start screen and bot opponent (M38–M45)

**Run note (agreed with the user 2026-11-06):** M38–M45 are pre-approved as one batch and run back to back
without `/clear` and without per-milestone sign-off. This overrides "one milestone per session" and "agree each
milestone first" for these milestones only. Each milestone is implemented by a sub-agent at the effort shown,
then verified (build, `ctest`, `git diff tests/replays` empty), marked ✅, committed and pushed.

Every bot milestone follows **D-054** (the bot is a player, not the rules; it never peeks at dice; it is
deterministic). Strength targets are measured with the Release arena tool (`wh40k_bot_arena`) and the numbers are
recorded under the milestone. A missed target is recorded as an open item, never lowered silently.

Bot levels: **Easy** = baseline (M39), **Normal** = M41–M43 decisions, **Hard** = M44 evaluator and lookahead.
Every earlier level stays selectable as a regression opponent.

---

## M38: Start screen and saved settings ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `medium` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-06)

> The exe opens on a start screen instead of a game. It has these buttons:
> - **Play hotseat**;
> - **Play vs bot**, with "You play: Space Marines / Tyranids" (default Space Marines; the bot takes the other side);
> - **Watch bot vs bot**;
> - **Continue saved game** (only if `saves/game.json` exists);
> - **Watch a replay** (combo of `tests/replays/*.json`);
> - **Quit**.
>
> Under the buttons are the options:
> - scenario (combo of `data/scenarios/*.json`); seed (random, or fixed with a value);
> - bot level (Easy / Normal / Hard; levels not built yet fall back to the best one available);
> - bot speed (Slow / Normal / Fast / Instant); "Bot rolls its own dice automatically";
> - the existing toggles: show dice pop-ups, skip steps with nothing to do, fix illegal moves, allocate damage
>   automatically, warn before ending shooting with shots left; UI scale.
>
> **Settings:**
> - They are an `AppSettings` struct in `src/client_2d/logic/settings.{hpp,cpp}`.
> - They are saved as JSON to `vibehammer_settings.json` next to the exe.
> - Missing, unknown or corrupt keys fall back to the defaults.
> - The sidebar toggles stay where they are and write back to the settings.
>
> **In-game:** the sidebar toolbar and the victory pop-up get a **Main menu** button.
>
> **CLI:**
> - Any CLI flag skips the start screen, so `package.ps1`'s smoke test (`--screenshot --steps 3 --seed 1`)
>   still renders a game.
> - `--menu 1` shows the start screen (for screenshots).
>
> **Bot sides:** the game records which sides are bots (`std::array<bool,2>` in the client, not in `GameState`).
> The bot itself plays from M39; until then a bot side is played by hand.

**Acceptance**
- Settings round-trip through JSON. A missing file, a corrupt file and an unknown or wrong-typed key all give the
  defaults for what they affect (logic tests `M38_Settings`).
- The scenario list finds `v1_skirmish.json`; the replay list finds the `tests/replays` files (logic tests).
- `--menu 1 --screenshot` renders the start screen. The existing smoke flags still render a game (screenshot).
- Replays unchanged.

---

## M39: Bot framework, driver, baseline bot, arena ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-06)

> **The `wh40k_ai` library:**
> - A new headless library in `src/ai/` (`wh40k_ai`, namespace `wh40k::ai`).
> - It links `wh40k_client_logic`, so the dependencies run ai → logic → core with no cycle.
> - It is include-checked with `cmake/CheckCoreIncludes.cmake`, with `/W4 /WX`.
> - Tests go in `tests/ai/` → `wh40k_ai_tests`, named `M39_...`.
>
> **The files:**
> - `bot.hpp`:
>   - `enum class BotLevel { Easy, Normal, Hard }`, `struct BotConfig`;
>   - `struct BotChoice { core::GameAction action; std::string reason; }`;
>   - `class Bot { BotChoice decide(const core::GameState&, core::Side); }`. It is a stateless policy plus an
>     optional plan memo (D-054).
> - `state_hash.*`: FNV-1a over unit positions, wounds, flags, flow and pending. It excludes `state.dice`. It is used
>   for the bot's own RNG seed and the plan memo.
> - `simulator.*`: a bare `GameState` copy stepped with the 4-argument `apply_action` and a bot-owned `SeededDice`.
>   It is the **only** ai file that may call `apply_action(`.
> - `baseline_bot.*`: the M12 `choose()` policy (`tests/client/client_logic_test.cpp`, "Normal move towards the
>   enemy when more than 8" away", translate 2" or the charge roll minus 1", charge once, editor defaults). It is
>   moved in unchanged as level Easy; the old test keeps passing, through the moved code if convenient.
> - `fallback.*`: a legal answer for every one of the 15 decision kinds, and the loop guard (the same state hash and
>   decision three times → fallback; fallback rejected → the bot stops and reports the reasons).
> - `bot_driver.*`: raylib-free pacing.
>   - `tick(double now)` returns an action when it's due.
>   - Thinking pauses per action kind, scaled by speed. Normal speed: 0.8 s before a unit's activation, 0.12 s
>     between model placements, 0.4 s before a commit, 0.6 s for reactive allocation and casualty answers.
>   - Slow ×2 (×3 since M48, D-059), Fast ×0.4, Instant 0.
>   - It runs `decide` on a worker `std::thread` with a `GameState` copy. A generation counter drops results that
>     went stale after undo, a new game or a load.
> - `arena.*` and `arena_main.cpp` (exe `wh40k_bot_arena`, run in Release):
>   - flags `--games N --a <level> --b <level> --scenario <file> --seed <first>`;
>   - prints win, draw and loss counts, the mean end round, mean surviving models, rejected actions,
>     `UnitMoveReverted` count and p50/p95 decision time.
>
> **Client changes:**
> - Auto actions: `AutoSettings` gets the bot sides, so auto-allocate never answers a bot's decision.
> - Undo: `UndoGroups` tags each group with its owner; Undo pops any trailing bot groups plus the human's last group.
> - Input gating: every board click, drag and panel button checks that a human controls the deciding side.
> - Panel: while the bot decides it shows "Bot (Tyranids) is thinking… — <reason>", with **Pause** and **Step**.
> - Dice: a bot's own dice cards auto-roll and auto-continue after a dwell (about 1.2 s at Normal speed; a click
>   skips it), when "Bot rolls its own dice automatically" is on. The bot never submits while a pop-up is open.
> - The bot also answers its reactive decisions (allocation order, casualties) during the human's turn.
> - Saves: the save JSON gets an optional `"players": {"A": "human", "B": "bot", "bot_level": ...}` block. Old saves
>   load as hotseat. `GameState` is unchanged.
> - CLI: `--bot A|B|both|none`, `--bot-speed slow|normal|fast|instant`, `--bot-level easy|normal|hard`, and
>   `--bot-steps n` (runs n bot decisions instantly before the first frame, for screenshots).

**Acceptance**
- 40 seeded Easy-vs-Easy games finish (`state().result` set) with zero rejected actions and zero `UnitMoveReverted`
  events (ai tests).
- Changing only `state.dice` never changes a decision, over the states of several games (ai tests).
- Guard test: `apply_action(` only appears in `simulator.cpp`; no raylib, ImGui or OS headers (ai tests and the
  include check).
- Driver: delays follow the speed (Instant is 0); it never acts while dice are showing; a stale worker result is
  dropped (ai tests).
- Undo against a bot returns to the human's last input; auto-allocate leaves the bot's decisions to the bot; a save
  with a `"players"` block reloads and an old save loads as hotseat (logic tests).
- `--bot B --bot-steps 20 --screenshot` renders (screenshot). Replays unchanged.

**Results (2026-11-06):**
- Release arena, `wh40k_bot_arena --games 200 --a easy --b easy` (V1 skirmish, seeds 1–200): Space Marines win 200,
  Tyranids 0, draws 0, unfinished 0; mean end round 1.94; mean surviving models A 4.92, B 0.00; rejected actions 0,
  `UnitMoveReverted` 0; decision time p50 0.008 ms, p95 0.289 ms.
- The baseline is lopsided: Easy is the M12 test policy, whose Termagants walk 2" at a time into Bolt Rifle fire. That
  is the yardstick the later levels have to beat, not a balance problem to fix here.
- ctest: 40 seeded Easy-vs-Easy games in `M39_Arena` (about 2.5 s in Debug).

## M40: Odds and threat analysis ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-06)

> `src/ai/analysis/`. All numbers come from core's own functions (D-054); thresholds are never re-typed.
>
> **`odds.*`:**
> - Per-die hit, wound and save probabilities, made by driving `make_hit_rolls` / `make_wound_rolls` with
>   `ScriptedDice` faces 1–6, and from `check_save_roll` / `required_wound_roll`.
> - Weapon abilities come from `make_attack_profile` / `ability_effects` / `gather_attack_dice`: Heavy, Torrent,
>   Rapid Fire (half range), Assault, Blast, criticals.
> - Expected damage per weapon profile against a target unit, and a damage distribution sampled by
>   `resolve_attack_batch` on a scratch `Unit` with the bot's dice (it handles W2 spillover and wounded models
>   first).
> - Weapons the engine rejects (D-002, D-026) are excluded via `unimplemented_ability_problem`.
>
> **`charge_odds.*`:**
> - Exact P(charge succeeds): for each 2D6 total, a scratch state with that roll (`ScriptedDice`) and a check that
>   a legal charge move exists. A quick distance bound is used first, and a placement check from M42 once it exists.
> - The same for the six advance results.
> - The distance to the (expected kills + 1)-th closest enemy model after our shooting.
>
> **`threat.*`:**
> - Threat ranges: M, M + advance, weapon range, M + charge distribution.
> - Unit role: ranged vs melee expected value against the actual enemy unit.
> - Matchup value per range band: engaged, within half range, in range, out of range.

**Acceptance**
- For every V1 weapon profile, with and without Heavy and within or outside Rapid Fire range, analytic expected
  damage is within 2% of the mean of 20,000 sampled engine batches (ai tests, fixed seed).
- Charge P(success) equals a brute-force enumeration over all 36 dice pairs, for several distances (ai tests).
- Against each other, the Termagants rate as ranged-leaning and the Intercessors as strong at both (ai tests).

**Results (2026-11-06):**
- Expected damage: all 66 cases (every accepted V1 profile, with and without [HEAVY], in and out of [RAPID FIRE]
  half range, against Intercessors, Termagants and a wounded Intercessor squad) are within 2% of 20,000 engine
  batches each, in `M40_ExpectedDamage.DISABLED_FullGrid...` (Release 18 s, Debug about 6 min; run on purpose).
  ctest runs an 8-case subset at 20,000 batches each (about 23 s in Debug); the M40 tests add about 27 s in Debug.
- Charge odds equal the 36-pair brute force at 3", 6", 9", 11.5" and 13" (`M40_ChargeOdds`).
- Roles (`M40_Role`): Termagants vs Intercessors ranged 0.42 / melee 0.28 model equivalents (Ranged lean);
  Intercessors vs Termagants ranged 5.79 / melee 4.44 (strong at both). Details in D-054, M40 notes.

## M41: Shooting, defensive and fight decisions (level Normal) ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-06)

> Every decision except movement and charging now uses the M40 analysis. Movement and charges stay on the baseline
> policy until M42/M43.
>
> **Shooting:**
> - Shooting type, and each model's weapon, profile and target, maximizing expected value with overkill avoidance.
>   E.g. Saturation vs Focused Fire falls out of the numbers (Heavy after ≤ 3" of movement, Rapid Fire within 12").
> - `SelectAttackBatch`: D2 batches before D1 against W2 targets.
>
> **Defense:**
> - Allocation order and casualty priority:
>   - keep coherency;
>   - prefer to remove the models closest to an enemy that may charge, or the engaged models to break a combat,
>     whichever is worth more;
>   - keep models with better weapons.
> - Also covered: `RestoreCoherency` removal, and Desperate Escape hazard casualties.
>
> **Fight:**
> - Fight type (Normal / Overrun), `PassFight`, `ForcedFight` order, weapon and attack split (`MeleePlan`
>   options), `OverrunPileIn` (pile in or skip).
> - Pile-ins and consolidations use `auto_fight_move` (D-050) with target choice; skipping is always a candidate.
> - An Engaging Consolidation is weighed against the free fight it gives an enemy unit that hasn't fought (12.08).

**Acceptance**
- Fixtures (ai tests):
  - the profile choice changes correctly with range and with how far the unit moved;
  - casualty order keeps coherency where possible (no `RestoreCoherency` losses where avoidable);
  - the front models are removed when an enemy charge threatens;
  - a pointless consolidation is skipped.
- Arena: 20 seeds per seat in ctest, 200 in the Release arena (recorded here). Normal does better than Easy in both
  seats, with zero rejections and zero reverts.

**"Better" (defined before the arena runs; compared with Easy vs Easy on the same seeds 1–200):**
- As Space Marines (Normal A vs Easy B): no fewer A wins, and an earlier mean end round or more mean A surviving
  models.
- As Tyranids (Easy A vs Normal B): more mean wounds dealt to the Marines (A's mean wounds lost), a later mean end
  round or more mean B surviving models; and no fewer B wins + draws.
- In both seats: zero rejected actions, `UnitMoveReverted`, fallbacks, and no more models removed for coherency
  than Easy. (The arena now also prints mean wounds lost per side, models removed for coherency and fallbacks, and
  `--trace <seed>` prints one game's bot decisions with their reasons.)

**Results (2026-11-06):**
- Release arena, V1 skirmish, seeds 1–200:

  | Run | A wins | B wins | Draws | Mean end round | Mean survivors A / B | Mean wounds lost A / B | Coherency removals | p95 decision |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `--a easy --b easy` | 200 | 0 | 0 | 1.94 | 4.92 / 0.00 | 0.44 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 0.118 ms |
  | `--a normal --b easy` | 200 | 0 | 0 | 1.92 | 4.91 / 0.00 | 0.47 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 0.339 ms |
  | `--a easy --b normal` | 200 | 0 | 0 | 1.94 | 4.92 / 0.00 | 0.44 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 0.393 ms |
  | `--a normal --b normal` | 200 | 0 | 0 | 1.92 | 4.91 / 0.00 | 0.47 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 0.345 ms |

  Every run: 0 rejected actions, 0 `UnitMoveReverted`, 0 fallbacks, 0 unfinished.
- **As Space Marines: met.** Same wins (200), earlier mean end round (1.92 vs 1.94; over seeds 1–1000, 1.89 vs
  1.91). Normal's Intercessors pile in and consolidate into more Termagants, so they also take slightly more wounds
  back (0.47 vs 0.44) and keep 0.01 fewer models on average.
- **As Tyranids: not met (open item, for M42/M43; M43: they now play differently, see M43's results).** Normal's Termagants play exactly as well as Easy's: identical
  to two decimals over seeds 1–200 and 1–1000 (A wounds lost 0.36 vs 0.36, mean end round 1.91 vs 1.91 over 1000).
  Their outcome is decided by the decisions M41 leaves to the baseline: Easy's movement walks them 2" at a time into
  Rapid Fire range and its charge rule throws them into combat. Of the decisions M41 owns, the Termagants' only gun
  is the Fleshborer, their default casualty order is already front rank first, and they lose the melee exchange, so
  they end pile-ins and consolidations as Easy does; the few fights they still win something in (a pile-in that adds
  Termagant attackers, keeping engaged models while they still fight) don't move the numbers. No coherency losses
  occur in either level's games (one did, in a Normal game, before the casualty order chose the whole set of models
  to lose instead of one pick at a time; fixed).
- ctest: `M41_Arena` plays 20 seeds per seat and asserts only the robust direction (no fewer wins or wins + draws
  than Easy, no more coherency removals, every game clean). The M41 tests add about 11 s in Debug.

## M42: Charge planner ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-06)
**Rules:** 11.02, 11.04, 03.01, 03.03, 03.04

> **Auto charge moves:**
> - `auto_fight_move` (D-050) is generalized to charge moves (11.04): every model ends closer to a charge target; it
>   aims for base contact, else within 1", else engaged; it never ends engaged with a non-target; the whole unit
>   ends engaged with every target.
> - The client's **Auto move** button also works for charge moves.
> - The 11.04 "if possible" clauses are still not enforced by the engine (TODO(if-possible)); the planner just tries
>   to satisfy them.
>
> **The bot's charge decision:**
> - Declare only when P(success) × value (Fights First, 11.04; what the enemy's shooting and fighting can do to us
>   next turn) beats both a failed charge and not charging.
> - Choose the charge targets.
> - `SkipChargeMove` when no legal placement exists for the roll.

**Acceptance**
- Over a grid of positions and rolls, every planned charge move passes `move_end_problems` and commits without a
  revert, and it engages at least as many models as the M39 baseline translation (ai and logic tests).
- Charge declaration fixtures: it charges when the odds and value are good, and holds when the odds are poor and
  shooting is worth more (ai tests).
- Human Auto move for charges: offered only before any drag, one Undo group, deterministic (logic tests
  `M42_...`).
- Arena: zero reverted charge moves. Replays unchanged.

**Results (2026-11-06):**
- **Placements (D-056):** the logic grid (`M42_AutoChargeMove.*Grid*`: gaps 2.5"-11.5", sideways offsets 0", 3", 7",
  two formations per charger, every roll that makes the target eligible) has 186 cases for each charger. Every one
  gets a legal arrangement that commits without a revert. The M39 baseline translation stands in 173 (Intercessors)
  and 152 (Termagants) of them; the auto move engages at least as many models every time, and more in 121 and
  152 of them (for the Termagants the back rank is brought in, not left 2.1" behind). The ai grid
  (`M42_ChargeMove.AcrossAGrid...`: 88 cases, both chargers) replays the bot's planned steps through the engine:
  every step valid at the hash it expects, `move_end_problems` empty, no revert, at least the baseline's engaged
  models in all 88 and more in 61.
- **Declarations (ai tests `M42_Declare`):** Intercessors 4" from Termagants declare (92% odds, a charge move
  +0.94 on average against +0.45 for holding). Termagants 11.5" from Intercessors hold ("3% charge odds; the
  Intercessor Squad would win the fight"), and so do Termagants 10" from Intercessors that are locked in combat
  with other Termagants (nothing threatens them, a charge only joins a losing fight). Termagants 10" from free
  Intercessors are a near tie (−0.944 for a charge against −0.962 for holding): the Intercessors would shoot and
  then charge them next turn anyway, so striking first is very slightly better, and the bot declares.
- **Auto move for charges (logic tests `M42_AutoChargeMove`):** offered only for a charge move before any model is
  moved, submitted as one Undo group, deterministic, legal; it never ends engaged with a unit that isn't a target,
  engages both of two targets, and says why when no arrangement exists.
- Release arena, V1 skirmish, seeds 1–200:

  | Run | A wins | B wins | Draws | Mean end round | Mean survivors A / B | Mean wounds lost A / B | Coherency removals | p95 decision |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `--a easy --b easy` | 200 | 0 | 0 | 1.94 | 4.92 / 0.00 | 0.44 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 0.119 ms |
  | `--a normal --b easy` | 200 | 0 | 0 | 1.92 | 4.91 / 0.00 | 0.47 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 0.675 ms |
  | `--a easy --b normal` | 200 | 0 | 0 | 1.93 | 4.91 / 0.00 | 0.47 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 0.443 ms |
  | `--a normal --b normal` | 200 | 0 | 0 | 1.92 | 4.91 / 0.00 | 0.47 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 0.969 ms |

  Every run: 0 rejected actions, 0 `UnitMoveReverted`, 0 fallbacks, 0 unfinished.
- **The M41 Tyranid open item: not improved (still open, for M43).** Over seeds 1–200 Easy-vs-Normal reads 0.47 A
  wounds lost against Easy-vs-Easy's 0.44, but over seeds 1–1000 the two are identical (A wounds lost 0.36 / 0.36,
  mean end round 1.91 / 1.91, A survivors 4.93 / 4.94). The baseline movement walks the Termagants into Rapid Fire
  range, the first Bolt Rifle volley leaves 4–6 of them, and their charges (at about 8"–10", which both levels
  declare) are made with a single rank that the baseline translation also engages in full. In 60 games each level
  declared 61 Tyranid charges and made 12 charge moves. The difference has to come from movement (M43).
- ctest: the M42 logic tests take about 6.5 s and the ai tests about 7.5 s in Debug; the whole suite went from about
  77 s to 85 s.

## M43: Movement planner and whole-turn plan ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-06)
**Rules:** 03.01–03.04, 09.02–09.07, 10.02–10.06, 11.02

> **Candidates:**
> - Move types: Remain Stationary, Normal, Advance, Fall Back (Ordered Retreat / Desperate Escape).
> - Destinations:
>   - the unit translated along 16 fixed directions (a constant unit-vector table, no `sin`/`cos`) × distance
>     steps;
>   - line-abreast and wedge formations facing the enemy.
> - Formation changes assign models to slots in a collision-free order, with two-segment paths to break cycles.
> - Each spot is snapped with `move_reach` / `fix_drop`, then the whole proposal is checked with
>   `move_end_problems` before Commit (D-054).
>
> **Whole-turn scoring (M40 analysis):**
> - Our shooting value this turn: Heavy if we move ≤ 3", Assault weapons only after an Advance, none after a Fall
>   Back, Rapid Fire within half range.
> - Plus charge odds × melee value (no charge after an Advance or Fall Back).
> - Minus the enemy's expected reply next turn: their shooting from where they can get to, and their charge odds ×
>   melee.
> - Plus terms for board edges, coherency slack, and engaged shooting (engaged Termagants can't shoot; Intercessors
>   can fire Bolt pistols).
>
> **What should come out of the scoring:**
> - A shooting unit stays out of the enemy's likely charge range while it can still shoot. If it will be caught
>   anyway, it closes and charges first.
> - A melee unit closes, and only Advances when it can't charge this turn anyway.
> - An engaged unit Falls Back when staying in combat is worth less than shooting next turn.

**Acceptance**
- Fixtures (ai tests):
  - holds still for Heavy when that's best;
  - closes to Rapid Fire range plus charge range when that's better;
  - stays out of the enemy's likely charge range when kiting is better;
  - never ends a move in Engagement Range unless it is charging;
  - no reverts.
- Arena (Release, 200 seeds, recorded here):
  - as Intercessors vs Easy: ≥ 95% wins, with an earlier mean winning round than Easy vs Easy;
  - as Termagants vs Easy: more draws and wins, and more surviving models, than Easy vs Easy.
- Zero rejections and zero reverts.

**Results (2026-11-06):**
- **Fixtures (ai tests `M43_*`, all pass):**
  - holds still for Heavy: `M43_Heavy.InTheLastTurnFarFromAnotherIntercessorSquadItRemainsStationaryAndFiresHeavyFocusedFire`
    (19" from a second Intercessor Squad in the game's last turn: Remain Stationary, then Heavy Focused Fire) and
    `M43_Heavy.FarFromAnotherIntercessorSquadItMovesNoMoreThanThreeInchesAndKeepsHeavy` (20" in round 1: Remain
    Stationary or a move of no more than 3", Heavy kept). Against the Termagants holding still never wins: a 6" move
    loses only 4% of the shooting (Saturation 5.55 against Focused Fire 5.79) and buys a charge.
  - closes to Rapid Fire and charge range: `M43_RapidFire.IntercessorsSixteenInchesFromTermagantsCloseToHalfRangeAndChargeRange`
    (16" -> about 10": every Intercessor within 12", a charge can be declared, no Advance);
  - stays out of the likely charge range: `M43_Kiting.TermagantsTenInchesFromIntercessorsKeepOutOfRapidFireAndChargeRangeAndStillShoot`
    (10" in mid-board -> more than 14", P(charge after a move) under 25%, still within the Fleshborers' 18");
  - never ends a move in Engagement Range unless charging, no reverts: `M43_Legal.AcrossAGridNoPlannedMoveEndsEngagedOrIsRevertedAndAFreshBotGivesTheSamePlan`
    (both units, gaps 3"-16", two offsets, and both units starting engaged: no rejection, revert, reset or engaged end);
  - Advance decided from its odds before the roll: `M43_Advance.DecidedFromTheOddsBeforeTheRollThenEachRollsMoveStandsAndKeepsTheAdvance`
    (the same choice whatever `state.dice`; each of the six rolls gets its own legal plan, never reset, still an
    Advance), and `M43_NeverPeeksAtDice.MovementDecisionsAreTheSameWhateverTheDiceAndFromAnyStateOfThePlan`;
  - also `M43_FallBack.EngagedTermagantsFallBackAndEngagedIntercessorsStayInTheFight` and `M43_Arena` (12 seeds per
    seat: every game won as Space Marines and sooner than Easy, later games as Tyranids, all clean).
- Release arena, V1 skirmish, seeds 1–200:

  | Run | A wins | B wins | Draws | Mean end round | Mean survivors A / B | Mean wounds lost A / B | Coherency removals | p95 / max decision |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `--a easy --b easy` | 200 | 0 | 0 | 1.94 | 4.92 / 0.00 | 0.44 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 0.13 / 0.36 ms |
  | `--a normal --b easy` | 200 | 0 | 0 | 1.41 | 4.93 / 0.00 | 0.35 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 8.7 / 23.3 ms |
  | `--a easy --b normal` | 200 | 0 | 0 | 2.44 | 4.92 / 0.00 | 0.44 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 2.4 / 25.2 ms |
  | `--a normal --b normal` | 200 | 0 | 0 | 1.51 | 4.95 / 0.00 | 0.30 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 7.9 / 17.0 ms |

  Every run: 0 rejected actions, 0 `UnitMoveReverted`, 0 fallbacks, 0 unfinished. Over seeds 1–1000: Easy vs Easy
  1.91 rounds, A wounds lost 0.36; Easy vs Normal 2.38 rounds, 0.40; Normal vs Easy 1.42 rounds (1000 wins). (The
  arena now also prints the slowest decision and which seeds lost models to coherency.)
- **As Intercessors vs Easy: met.** 200 wins of 200, mean winning round 1.41 against Easy vs Easy's 1.94 (1.42
  against 1.91 over 1000 seeds). They move 6" straight at the Termagants (Saturation at half range, 5.55 expected),
  charge at 42-92%, and usually end the game in round 1 or 2.
- **As Termagants vs Easy: more draws + wins and more surviving models: not met (open item, for M44).** 0 draws and
  0.00 surviving models, as Easy. It is out of reach in this matchup: Space Marines go first, so the first Bolt
  Rifle volley (5.8 expected kills at 10", before the Termagants move at all) is unavoidable; the 24" board leaves at
  most about 17" between the units and the Bolt Rifle reaches 24", so the Termagants can never get out of range or
  sight (no terrain). Beyond 12" Easy's Focused Fire still kills 2.9 a turn, so a draw needs ten Termagants to
  survive five volleys whose expected kills add up to about 17; no seed in 1000 does. Traces (e.g. `--trace 14 --a easy
  --b normal`): R1 the 4-6 survivors Advance back to 15-16" (outside half range, the Intercessors' charge odds 17%)
  and shoot; R2 the Intercessors walk 2" and shoot outside half range; the 1-2 left are caught in R2 or R3.
  **The secondary signs improve clearly:** a later mean end round (2.44 against 1.94 over seeds 1–200, 2.38 against
  1.91 over 1000) and more wounds dealt to the Marines (0.40 against 0.36 over 1000 seeds, +11%; equal over seeds
  1–200, 0.44 and 0.44, where the difference is within the noise).
- **The M41 open item (Normal Termagants play exactly as Easy's): resolved for play, open for results.** They now
  play differently and better (kiting, Advance and shoot, Fall Back from losing combats, a last charge when doomed),
  but they still never draw (above).
- Performance (Release): a Movement decision takes 12-16 ms (the move's plan 3-6 ms, an Advance valued over its six
  rolls, Remain Stationary with its charge prospect); the arena's slowest decision is 30 ms and p95 under 9 ms.
- ctest: the M43 tests take about 21 s in Debug; the planner also plays Normal's moves in the M41 and M42 game tests
  (about +15 s); the whole suite went from about 85 s to 127 s.

## M44: Game-aware evaluator, lookahead, tuning (level Hard) ✅ (done, with open items)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-06)

> **Evaluator:**
> - A small Markov chain over (models left on each side, battle round, range band) for the remaining half-turns.
> - It gives V = P(win) − P(loss), with a draw worth 0 (D-007: wipe-out wins, round 5 is a draw).
> - The winning side pushes to wipe the enemy out, and a losing side plays for the draw (kiting, staying out of
>   charge range).
> - Movement, casualty order, coherency removal and consolidation all use V.
>
> **Lookahead:** the top 3 move plans are checked by an engine-backed Monte Carlo of the rest of our turn and the
> enemy's reply. It uses the simulator with the bot's own dice, a cheap rollout policy and a fixed work budget
> (simulated actions, never wall-clock time).
>
> **Tuning:**
> - Weights live in a `BotWeights` struct, tuned with arena runs; the final values and results are recorded in
>   D-054.
> - New scenarios so the tuning isn't done on one matchup: `data/scenarios/bot_termagants_first.json` and
>   `data/scenarios/bot_20_termagants.json`.

**Acceptance**
- Hard beats Normal in both seats over 200 Release arena seeds on every scenario (recorded here). A small seeded
  subset runs in ctest.
- p95 decision time under 500 ms in Release (reported by the arena, not asserted in ctest).
- Zero rejections and zero reverts. Replays unchanged.

**"Beats" (defined before the final arena runs):** each Hard seat (`--a hard --b normal`, `--a normal --b hard`) is
compared with `--a normal --b normal` on the same scenario and seeds 1–200.
- **Primary:** the mean game result per game from the seat's point of view (win +1, draw 0, loss −1). Hard beats
  Normal in the seat when its mean is higher.
- **When both runs sit at the same ceiling or floor in the seat** (the seat wins every game, or loses every game),
  pre-declared tie-breaks, in order:
  - as the side that always wins: an earlier mean end round, then fewer mean wounds lost;
  - as the side that always loses: more draws, then a later mean end round, then more mean wounds dealt.
- Equal on every measure is not a win; a tied primary result off the ceiling or floor is not a win either.

**Results (2026-11-06):**
- **What Hard is** (D-054, M44 notes): Normal's modules plus a game-aware evaluator (a Markov chain over each unit's
  wounds left, the half-turn and the range band, with each unit's room to back away; V = P(win) − P(loss), a draw
  worth 0, plus a time preference and material as tie-breaks) and a lookahead (6 Monte Carlo rollouts per move plan
  of the rest of our turn and the enemy's reply, on the bot's own dice, at most 6000 simulated actions). Movement,
  charges, casualty order, coherency removal and consolidation use the evaluator. Every Hard decision starts from
  Normal's answer and plays something else only when the evaluator values it more than 0.01 higher
  (`BotWeights::margin`).
- Release arena, seeds 1–200 (Hard's weights `BotWeights{}`; the sweeps are in D-054, M44 notes):

  | Scenario | Run | A wins | B wins | Draws | Mean result A | Mean end round | Mean survivors A / B | Mean wounds lost A / B | Coherency removals | p95 / max decision |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | V1 skirmish | `--a normal --b normal` | 200 | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | 1.51 | 4.95 / 0.00 | 0.30 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 10.0 / 22.7 ms |
  | V1 skirmish | `--a hard --b normal` | 200 | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | 1.51 | 4.95 / 0.00 | 0.30 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 19.6 / 134.9 ms |
  | V1 skirmish | `--a normal --b hard` | 200 | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | 1.67 | 4.96 / 0.00 | 0.27 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 14.8 / 132.6 ms |
  | V1 skirmish | `--a hard --b hard` | 200 | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | 1.67 | 4.96 / 0.00 | 0.27 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 50.3 / 132.4 ms |
  | Termagants first | `--a normal --b normal` | 200 | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | 2.05 | 4.61 / 0.00 | 1.25 / 10.00 | 0 / 0 | 10.6 / 22.4 ms |

  | 20 Termagants | `--a hard --b hard` | 194 | 2 | 4 | +0.960 | 2.73 | 4.38 / 0.12 | 1.76 / 19.89 | 8 / 0 | 35.5 / 512.1 ms |

  Every run: 0 rejected actions, 0 `UnitMoveReverted`, 0 fallbacks, 0 unfinished. Normal vs Normal reproduces the
  numbers measured before any M44 code (and M43's V1 row), so Normal's play is unchanged. (The arena now also
  prints the mean result per game and the seeds with a `UnitMoveReverted`, and `--trace` prints each decision's
  time; `--weights` overrides Hard's weights for tuning.)
- **As Tyranids (Hard B against Normal A): beats Normal on every scenario.**
  - V1: both always lose, with no draws either way; a later mean end round, 1.67 against 1.51. Hard falls back from
    a losing combat instead of staying to be finished off, and keeps away.
  - Termagants first: both always lose, no draws; a later mean end round, 2.06 against 2.05. The margin is a few
    games in 200 and varies with the weights (2.04–2.06 across the sweeps): within the noise, recorded as measured.
  - 20 Termagants: mean result −0.960 against −0.970 (2 wins and 4 draws against 1 and 4).
- **As Space Marines (Hard A against Normal B): ties on every scenario: not met (open item).**
  - V1 and Termagants first: both win every game, with the same mean end round (1.51; 2.05) and the same wounds lost
    (0.30; 1.25). Hard keeps Normal's answers in nearly every Space Marine decision: the evaluator puts P(win) at
    100% for both and their tempo values within the margin.
  - 20 Termagants: the same mean result, +0.970 (195 wins, 1 loss, 4 draws), with a mean end round of 2.60 against
    2.59. The non-wins are long melees with the big Termagant unit (for Normal, seeds 39, 97, 119, 131 and 190; the
    Intercessors end with 0–2 models).
  - Evidence that it is out of reach for this design: with the margin at 0 (or Normal's answer only one candidate
    among others) Hard's own choices as the Marines do slightly worse: V1 1.52 rounds, Termagants first 2.06, 20
    Termagants 194 wins and 6 draws (the same mean result). Only 2 of the first 120 V1 seeds change, both a round
    later (seeds 33 and 59). There Normal consolidates into the last Termagants, and Normal's Termagants then stay
    in the combat and die in their own turn. The chain assumes the enemy plays its best (falling back), so it
    values not consolidating a hair higher. Normal's Marines are already at the ceiling in V1 and Termagants first:
    the end round is decided by the first charge roll (42–72% after the first volley) and the fight after it. A
    stronger opponent model (the enemy's actual policy in the rollouts) or a deeper search would be the next step.
- **Performance: met.** p95 at most 55 ms (hard vs hard, Termagants first); the slowest decision took 512 ms (hard
  vs hard, 20 Termagants: a Movement decision of the 20-model unit with its Advance and Fall Back options). A chain
  table costs 14 ms (V1) or 27 ms (20 Termagants) in Release and is built about once per game per bot. A whole Hard
  vs Hard game takes 0.2 s (V1) to 0.5 s (20 Termagants).
- **Zero rejections, reverts and fallbacks: met** in every run above, and in every sweep after `escape_stands`
  (D-054). Replays unchanged; no core change.
- **Tests (ai tests `M44_*`, all pass):**
  - the evaluator: V within [−1, 1] and zero-sum across a grid, obvious positions ordered, a draw worth 0, terminal
    states exact (a hypothetical wipe-out too), more wounds never worse, a cached table equal to a fresh one;
  - the Monte Carlo: the same result for the same state and when only `state.dice` changes, and the budget respected;
  - Hard never peeks at dice, and a fresh bot gives the same answer (16 Hard decisions of two games, 6+ kinds);
  - play for the draw: four Termagants 9" from a whole Intercessor Squad in battle round 5 back away out of half
    range, shoot and declare no charge;
  - the new scenarios load, deploy legally and play clean games. The 20 Termagants stand in three ranks of 7, 7 and
    6 (two ranks of 10 would break the 9" clause of 03.03). A small V1 arena (2 seeds per seat) is clean, wins every
    game as the Marines and lasts no shorter as the Tyranids;
  - `M39_Levels.HardPlaysAsNormalUntilItIsBuilt` (written for this milestone) became
    `M39_Levels.EveryLevelIsBuiltAndHardIsItsOwnLevel`: `effective_level(Hard) == Hard`, and Hard's MoveUnits reason
    comes from the evaluator ("Hard: win ...") while Normal's doesn't.
- ctest: the M44 tests take about 22 s in Debug; the whole suite went from about 127 s to about 150 s.

## M46: Bots pile in and consolidate when it costs nothing ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** yes (approved 2026-11-06) · **Rules:** 12.02, 12.03, 12.06, 12.07, 12.08 · **Decision:** D-057

> In play the bots hardly ever piled in or consolidated, which looked passive. After a charge the models are
> usually already engaged, so closing into base contact changes nothing in the melee exchange, and Normal and Hard
> ended the step on a tie. Easy ended every pile-in and consolidation except an Overrun's.
> - **Normal and Hard: move unless it hurts.** A tie goes to the tidier move (more engaged, more in base contact,
>   closer, tighter coherency, as Auto move places models). A move that costs them is still refused.
> - **Easy: always Auto move.** Every offered pile-in and consolidation worth making (D-047, D-049), if it stands.
> - No rule or core change; the bot only picks among legal actions (D-054). The arena counts the Pile In /
>   Consolidate steps the bots answer and the moves they make.

**Acceptance**
- Clean build; all of ctest passes. New ai tests `M46_*` and client tests `M46_FightMoveShape.*`.
- Release arena (200 seeds; V1 and both bot scenarios; Easy/Easy, Normal/Easy, Easy/Normal, Hard/Normal,
  Normal/Hard): the bots make a clearly larger share of the pile-ins and consolidations offered to them; no seat's
  wins + draws drop by more than ~3 percentage points; no rejected actions, reverted moves or fallbacks.
- The existing replays are byte-identical; the new replay `tests/replays/m46_bot_fight_moves.json` is byte-stable.

**Results (2026-11-06):** all acceptance items met. Arena before (code at `95cb5e4` with the new counters) and
after, `wh40k_bot_arena --games 200`:
- **Fight moves made / Pile In and Consolidate steps the bot answered** (fewer steps reach the bot after, because
  models in base contact can't move and the client skips the step, D-047):

  | Scenario, A vs B | Side A before → after | Side B before → after |
  |---|---|---|
  | V1, Normal vs Easy | 60 / 183 → 138 / 140 | 0 / 136 → 94 / 96 |
  | V1, Hard vs Normal | 34 / 127 → 125 / 125 | 1 / 63 → 43 / 43 |
  | V1, Normal vs Hard | 30 / 91 → 91 / 91 | 1 / 56 → 41 / 41 |
  | 20 Termagants, Easy vs Easy | 0 / 810 → 376 / 376 | 56 / 808 → 436 / 439 |
  | 20 Termagants, Hard vs Normal | 109 / 649 → 346 / 351 | 50 / 984 → 522 / 530 |
  | 20 Termagants, Normal vs Hard | 97 / 558 → 317 / 322 | 56 / 927 → 483 / 491 |
- **Results** (Space Marines wins / Tyranid wins / draws): V1 and Termagants-first are unchanged in every pairing
  (200/0/0, and Easy vs Normal on Termagants-first 199/0/1 both times). 20 Termagants: Easy/Easy 198/0/2 →
  196/1/3; Normal/Easy 200/0/0 both; Easy/Normal 186/1/13 both; Hard/Normal 195/1/4 → 194/0/6; Normal/Hard 194/2/4
  → 196/3/1. The largest change is Hard as Tyranids, wins + draws 6 → 4 (1 point in 100).
- Clean everywhere: 0 rejected actions, 0 `UnitMoveReverted`, 0 fallbacks.
- **Seen, not fixed (open):** Easy's Termagants, which aren't weighed, now lose models to coherency after
  casualties (Auto move strings a big unit round the enemy). 20 Termagants: Easy/Easy 0 → 37 models, Normal/Easy
  0 → 128; Termagants-first Normal/Easy 0 → 10. Normal's and Hard's Termagants are not affected (Hard/Normal
  3 → 1); their Intercessors lose slightly more (5 → 9, 8 → 9). **Addressed by D-058 (M47):** the default
  casualty order keeps Easy's Termagants in coherency (37 → 2, 128 → 5, 10 → 2).
- **Decision time:** p95 at most 46 ms (budget 500 ms). Hard's slowest single decision on 20 Termagants went from
  0.20 s to 1.46 s.
- Replays: existing files unchanged; `m46_bot_fight_moves.json` (Normal vs Normal, 20 Termagants, seed 1; both
  sides make fight moves) is identical across two regenerations and replays with core only.

## User-testing feedback (M47–M50)

**Run note (agreed with the user 2026-11-07):** M47–M50 come from user testing and are pre-approved as one batch.
They run back to back without `/clear` and without per-milestone sign-off. This overrides "one milestone per
session" and "agree each milestone first" for these milestones only. Each milestone is implemented by a sub-agent
at the effort shown, then verified (clean build, all of `ctest`, `git diff tests/replays` showing only the planned
changes), marked ✅, committed and pushed. A missed acceptance number is recorded as an open item, never lowered.

**Agreed with the user:**
- Slow bot speed = 3× Normal.
- Pause moves to the sidebar as a checkbox, not saved between games. Step and the bot speed stay in the bar
  above the board.
- The new casualty order is the default everywhere; Hard also compares it as an extra candidate; Normal is
  unchanged; the Hard vs Hard bot replay may be regenerated on purpose.
- A unit's odd-one-out model (the Sergeant) is kept until last, unless removing it is the only way to keep
  coherency.
