## M47: Default casualty order: furthest from the action first, keeping coherency ✅ (done, with an open item)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-07) · **Rules:** 03.03, 05.04, 06.02 · **Decision:** D-058

> User feedback: "when choosing which units to kill, the auto mode should prefer models furthest away from the
> 'action' ... so that could be models at the back, or models at risk of being split off and losing coherency. so
> the least coherent ones I guess. this is important". Today the default (`CasualtyPlan`) is plain unit order,
> and V1 lists the front rank first (D-008), so it removes the Sergeant and the front rank first.
>
> **New `src/client_2d/logic/casualty_order.{hpp,cpp}`** (read-only core queries and geometry; the engine still
> decides 05.04, 06.02 and 03.03):
> - `coherency_losses(models)`: moved verbatim from `normal::coherency_losses`, on a pairwise near/far table with
>   the same `within` predicates as `check_coherency`.
> - `default_casualty_order(state, target, picks = {})`: every model once, `picks` first.
> - `default_coherency_removal(state, unit)`: the 03.03 Regaining Coherency pick.
>
> **Keys**, for each candidate m of the remaining set S; the first difference decides:
> 1. Fewest extra 03.03 losses after removing m. Models already out of coherency therefore go first.
> 2. The odd-one-out model last: the only model of its `type_name` in a unit with more than one type, fixed at the
>    decision. Key 1 comes first, so it goes earlier only when that is the only way to keep coherency.
> 3. At risk first: not engaged, and within 2" of at most one other model of S (a leaf, a straggler, a pair, or
>    already isolated). Engaged models are the action and never count as at risk.
> 4. Furthest from the closest enemy model first (edge to edge, any enemy unit on the battlefield, compared with
>    `more_than`; skipped with no enemies).
> 5. Later in unit order first (the back rank, D-008); the first-listed model is kept longest.
>
> **Phase A**, only while `OrderCasualties` for this target is pending: step through
> `preview_casualties(state, priority)` and push the best of its options, so forced wounded models, a picked W2
> model taking the damage, allocation groups and the 06.02 tiers are right. **Phase B:** the best of S,
> repeatedly, so every prefix is a good removal set.
>
> **Where it applies:**
> - `CasualtyPlan`'s default: "Allocate damage automatically", "Use default order", Easy's casualties and the
>   all-level fallback.
> - `CasualtyPicks` fills the rest after manual picks with it. On the board, "Use default order" finishes from the
>   current picks ("Default order for the rest" when there are picks).
> - Easy and the fallback answer RestoreCoherency with `default_coherency_removal`. A human's pick stays a click
>   (D-042).
> - Normal: unchanged (it uses the moved `coherency_losses`).
> - Hard: the default order is a 4th candidate in `decide_casualties`, after Normal's order, front-first and
>   rear-first; Normal's order is still kept unless beaten by the margin. Hard's rollouts use Easy's answers, so
>   they see the new order too.
>
> **UI text:** "use the default order" explains the order in a tooltip; the auto-allocate tooltip says it too
> (and moves back onto its own checkbox: with a bot in the game it was attached to "Bot rolls its own dice").

**Acceptance**
- Clean build; all of ctest passes. New client tests `M47_*` (inch coordinates: back rank first, straggler first,
  a line keeps coherency, an isolated model first, the Sergeant last, a wounded W2 model, melee, 06.02, picks plus
  the rest, auto-allocate, the coherency pick) and ai tests `M47_*` (Easy, fallback, Hard's candidate). Existing
  tests that used `CasualtyPlan` as "unit order" use an explicit unit-order priority instead, with every assertion
  kept.
- `m46_bot_fight_moves.json` (Normal vs Normal) regenerates byte-identical. `m45_bot_game.json` (Hard vs Hard) may
  change: regenerated on purpose, the first differing step recorded. The core replays are unchanged. New replay
  `tests/replays/m47_default_casualties.json` (Easy vs Easy, 20 Termagants) is byte-stable and replays with core
  only.
- Release arena (`--games 200`; V1, Termagants first and 20 Termagants; Easy/Easy, Normal/Easy, Easy/Normal,
  Hard/Normal, Normal/Hard), before and after:
  - 0 rejected actions, 0 `UnitMoveReverted`, 0 unfinished games, 0 fallbacks.
  - Easy's Termagant coherency removals at most half of before (D-057's open item: 37 / 128 / 10).
  - Normal's and Hard's coherency removals at most before + max(3, 20%).
  - No seat's wins + draws drops by more than 3 points; Hard seats at most 2 games lower.
  - p95 decision time ≤ 500 ms; Hard's slowest decision on 20 Termagants ≤ 1.2 × before.

**Results (2026-11-07):** every acceptance item met except one coherency bound (open item below). Arena before (code
at `ec71730`, measured before any M47 edit) and after, `wh40k_bot_arena --games 200`, seeds 1-200:

| Scenario, A vs B | Space Marines wins / Tyranid wins / draws | Removed for coherency, A (Space Marines) | B (Tyranids) |
|---|---|---|---|
| V1, Easy vs Easy | 200/0/0 both | 0 → 0 | 0 → 1 |
| V1, Normal vs Easy | 200/0/0 both | 0 → 0 | 2 → 0 |
| V1, Easy vs Normal | 200/0/0 both | 0 → 0 | 0 → 0 |
| V1, Hard vs Normal | 200/0/0 both | 0 → 0 | 0 → 0 |
| V1, Normal vs Hard | 200/0/0 both | 0 → 0 | 0 → 0 |
| Termagants first, Easy vs Easy | 200/0/0 both | 0 → 0 | 2 → 2 |
| Termagants first, Normal vs Easy | 200/0/0 both | 0 → 0 | **10 → 2** |
| Termagants first, Easy vs Normal | 199/0/1 both | 0 → 0 | 0 → 0 |
| Termagants first, Hard vs Normal | 200/0/0 both | 0 → 0 | 0 → 0 |
| Termagants first, Normal vs Hard | 200/0/0 both | 0 → 0 | 0 → 0 |
| 20 Termagants, Easy vs Easy | 196/1/3 → 197/1/2 | 1 → 0 | **37 → 2** |
| 20 Termagants, Normal vs Easy | 200/0/0 both | **1 → 15 (missed)** | **128 → 5** |
| 20 Termagants, Easy vs Normal | 186/1/13 → 188/2/10 | 0 → 0 | 0 → 0 |
| 20 Termagants, Hard vs Normal | 194/0/6 both | 9 → 9 | 1 → 1 |
| 20 Termagants, Normal vs Hard | 196/3/1 → 195/3/2 | 9 → 10 | 1 → 1 |

- **Easy's Termagants keep coherency:** D-057's open item 37 / 128 / 10 → 2 / 5 / 2 (bound: half). The few left are
  thin lines wrapped round the Intercessors with three or four models left, where the greedy order ends with two
  pairs and the next casualty must isolate one (D-058, accepted).
- **Wins + draws:** the largest drop is 1 point (20 Termagants, Easy vs Normal, the Tyranids 14 → 12). Hard's seats:
  Hard vs Normal unchanged, Normal vs Hard Tyranids 4 → 5. Hard never chose its new candidate in the 240 games
  checked (seeds 1-40 of each Hard pairing): Normal's order is kept unless beaten by the margin, so Hard's games
  change only through its rollouts (Easy's answers) and its opponents.
- Clean everywhere: 0 rejected actions, 0 `UnitMoveReverted`, 0 unfinished games, 0 fallbacks.
- **Decision time:** p95 at most 27.9 ms (budget 500 ms). Hard's slowest decision on 20 Termagants 0.92 s → 0.82 s
  (Hard vs Normal) and 0.97 s → 0.83 s (Normal vs Hard), both runs three arenas at a time (bound 1.2 ×).
- **Replays:** the core replays and `m46_bot_fight_moves.json` (Normal vs Normal) regenerate byte-identical.
  `m45_bot_game.json` (Hard vs Hard, seed 9) regenerates byte-identical too: no step differs (the default order is
  never Hard's pick there; only Hard's printed values move by a few hundredths, through its rollouts, which use
  Easy's answers). New `tests/replays/m47_default_casualties.json`: Easy vs Easy, 20 Termagants, seed 121, 99 steps,
  Space Marines win in battle round 2; 6 casualty orders (1 by the Space Marines) and 1 Regaining Coherency removal,
  each the default at its step; identical over two regenerations; replays with core only.
- **Open (missed bound):** 20 Termagants, Normal vs Easy: Normal's Intercessors lose 15 models to coherency (before
  1; bound 4). Normal's code is unchanged (m46 is byte-identical). Easy's Termagants now keep their engaged models, so
  they fight back more (557 → 608 Termagant fights over the 200 games), and Normal's casualty order wounds a model
  without weighing that 05.04 then forces that model to take the next damage: when it is the link of a line the
  charge strung out (the wounded Sergeant in seeds 16, 20, 27), its death isolates a neighbour. Normal's casualty
  decisions that can't keep coherency: 4 → 18 of about 230. Fixing it means changing Normal's casualty order, which
  this batch keeps unchanged; left for a later milestone.
- Also seen: V1 Easy vs Easy 0 → 1 and Termagants first Easy vs Easy 2 → 2 Termagants removed for coherency (the
  same thin-line case; not D-057's numbers).

## M48: Bot pacing and Pause in the sidebar ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-07) · **Decision:** D-059

> User feedback: slow bots should be slower; when the bots move quickly Pause can't be clicked; the Pause tooltip
> covers the bar's text saying what the bot is doing; the Step button stays where it is.
> - **Slow = 3× Normal** (`ai::speed_factor`): 2.4 s before a unit's activation, 0.36 s per placement, about 5 s
>   per bot dice card.
> - **Pause in the sidebar:** in a game with a bot, the "Game" section has a bot block under the unit list:
>   "Pause the bots (P)" and "Bot rolls its own dice automatically". Client state only, reset by a new game or a
>   load. The bar loses its Pause / Resume button and keeps Step first (so it doesn't shift), then the bot speed,
>   then the status. Against one bot the Step / speed row also shows while a bot's dice card is open.
> - **Clicks work while dice show:** the dice pop-up takes focus only when a card first appears and not while an
>   item is active (it took focus every frame, which cancelled every press elsewhere). Only a click on the pop-up
>   or on the bare board skips a bot card's wait. Step and key N only reveal or close a bot's card, never a
>   human's.
> - **Tooltips don't cover the bot's status:** the bar's tooltips wait for a longer rest and open below the bar.

**Acceptance**
- Clean build; all of ctest passes (speed factors updated in the M39 / M45 tests; new client tests `M48_*` for
  `set_paused`, Step on a human's card and the dice run). Replays unchanged.
- Screenshots: the sidebar bot block in a game against the bot, and the watch bar in bot vs bot with Step first
  and no Pause button. The package README says where Pause is.
- Manual check on Windows: Pause, Step and the bot speed respond while bot dice show at Fast.

**Results (2026-11-07):**
- **Slow = ×3:** `ai::speed_factor(Slow)` is 3.0 (was 2.0): 2.4 s before an activation, 0.36 s per placement,
  1.05 s before a bot dice roll and 3.6 s on show. Normal, Fast and Instant are unchanged.
- **Pause in the sidebar:** in a game with a bot, the "Game" section has the bot block right under the unit list:
  "Pause the bots (P)" (bound to `WatchControls::set_paused`; key P still toggles it) and "Bot rolls its own dice
  automatically" (moved up from the toggles below). Pause stays client state (`WatchControls::reset` on a new game,
  a load or the start screen; never in `AppSettings`). The bar lost Pause / Resume: Step comes first, then the bot
  speed, then (watch bar) the "Watching bot vs bot (<level>)" chip and the status ("Paused" stays there and as "is
  paused" in the thinking bar). Against one bot, the Step / speed row also shows in the rolling bar while a bot's
  card is open, on the same row as in the thinking bar.
- **Clicks work while dice show:** `DicePopup::draw` takes the focus once per card, when it first appears and no
  item is active (it called `SetNextWindowFocus` every frame, which cleared every other window's active item, so
  a press never reached its release). A paced bot card's wait is skipped only by a left click on the pop-up (not
  on its Roll / Continue button, which acts itself) or on the bare board (`!io.WantCaptureMouse`); the decision is
  `click_skips_bot_dice_wait` in `watch_controls`. `step_closes_popup` takes a `ShownCard` (None / Bots /
  Humans): Step or key N on a human's card is dropped and leaves the card alone.
- **Tooltips:** Step, the bot speed and the reason line's full reason use `bar_tooltip`: `Stationary |
  DelayNormal | NoSharedDelay`, placed at the item's x just below the action bar's bottom edge.
- **Tests:** client `M48_Watch.SetPausedAndTheToggleAgree`, `StepIgnoresAHumansCard`,
  `TheBotDiceRunFollowsThePauseCheckbox`, `OnlyAClickOnThePopUpOrTheBareBoardSkipsTheWait`; ai
  `M48_Pacing.SlowIsThreeTimesNormal`, `ASlowBotHandsOverItsActivationAfter2Point4Seconds`. Updated:
  `M39_Driver.PausesFollowTheActionKindAndTheSpeed` and `TheActionIsHandedOverOnceItsPauseHasPassed` (Slow 3.0,
  delivery at 3× Normal), the comment in `M45_Watch.SpeedChangesLiveAndStepHandsOverOneAction`, and
  `M45_Watch.AStepWithDiceShowingGoesToThePopUp` (same checks, now with `ShownCard`). All of ctest passes; replays
  unchanged (`git diff tests/replays` empty).
- **Screenshots** (`xvfb-run`, seed 1): the sidebar bot block against the bot (`--bot B`); the watch bar in bot vs
  bot (`--bot both --bot-steps 3`) with Step first and no Pause button; a bot's dice card (`--bot A --bot-steps 10
  --dice 1`) with the Step / speed row in the rolling bar.
- **Manual check still needed on Windows** (mouse input can't be scripted): at Fast, while the bot's dice show,
  ticking "Pause the bots", clicking Step and changing the bot speed all respond on the first click, and clicking
  them doesn't also skip the dice card; a click on the board or the pop-up still skips it; the bar's tooltips
  appear after a short rest, below the bar.

## M49: The Roll button only when the bot's dice wait for a click ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `medium` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-07) · **Decision:** D-060

> User feedback: only show the button to roll the bot's dice if the bot is paused.
> - A bot's card that rolls by itself (the bot's card, "Bot rolls its own dice" on, not paused) has no Roll and
>   no Continue / Done button. A same-height dim line takes their place: "The bot rolls its own dice. Pause the
>   bots to roll them yourself." (after the reveal, "Continuing..."). Enter, Space and a click on the pop-up or the
>   board still skip the wait.
> - Paused, or with the setting off, the card waits with Roll and Continue as a human's does. Pausing mid-card
>   shows them at once.

**Acceptance**
- Clean build; all of ctest passes; new client tests `M49_DiceButtons` cover every combination. Replays
  unchanged.

the bots to roll them yourself.", then "Continuing..."); Enter / Space and a click on the pop-up or the board still
skip. The rolling bar's hint reads "The bot rolls its own dice." on such a card. New client tests
`M49_DiceButtons` (7); all 723 ctest tests pass; replays unchanged. Screenshots (`--seed 1 --bot A --bot-steps 10
--dice 1` / `--dice 2`) show the bot's Hit-rolls card with the placeholder line before and after the reveal. No flag
pauses the bots, so the paused card (Roll back at once) is covered by the logic tests; a manual Windows check
remains: pause mid-card and see Roll / Continue appear, click on the dim line skips. D-060; D-033 / D-054 amended.

## M52: Release workflow, first GitHub release v0.1.0 ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `medium` · **Plan mode:** approved (2026-11-07) · **Decision:** D-063

> User request: "Could you make a release on GitHub for me?" The zip only builds on Windows, so a GitHub Actions
> workflow (`.github/workflows/release.yml`, `windows-latest`) builds it and publishes the release.
> - Triggers: a pushed tag `vX.Y.Z`, or a manual run with a `tag` input (builds the commit it runs on and creates
>   the tag there with the release).
> - Checks the tag against the version in `CMakeLists.txt` and `src/core/version.cpp`, and that
>   `docs/releases/<tag>.md` (the release notes) exists. Picks the VS generator with vswhere (2026 or 2022).
> - Builds everything in Release, runs all of ctest, then `tools/package.ps1 -Generator ... -SkipSmokeTests`
>   (the runner has no OpenGL 3.3, so the exe isn't launched). Uploads `VibeHammer-<tag>-windows-x64.zip`,
>   after checking it holds `VibeHammer.exe` and nothing from `docs/` and no PDF.
> - `package.ps1` with no arguments behaves exactly as before.

**Acceptance**
- The workflow run is green; release v0.1.0 exists with the zip attached and the notes.
- Linux build + ctest pass; replays unchanged.
- Manual check on Windows: download the zip, unzip, run VibeHammer.exe.

**Results (2026-11-07):** workflow run 42367178346 (manual run, tag v0.1.0) is green on a runner with VS 2026
("Visual Studio 18 2026"): Release build, 727 of 727 tests passed (13 disabled diagnostics and regenerators not
run), the zip packaged with 22 entries and nothing from `docs/`, and the release published at
https://github.com/Joeb1603/VibeHammer/releases/tag/v0.1.0 with `VibeHammer-v0.1.0-windows-x64.zip` (1.8 MB).
Decision D-063. Still open: the manual check on Windows (download, unzip, run VibeHammer.exe).

## Full-size battlefield with several units per side (M53–M58)

**Run note (agreed with the user 2026-11-07):** M53–M58 are pre-approved as one batch and run back to back
without `/clear` and without per-milestone sign-off, on branch `strike-force-44x60`. This overrides "one
milestone per session" and "agree each milestone first" for these milestones only. Each milestone is implemented
by a sub-agent at the effort shown, then verified (clean Debug and Release builds, all of `ctest`,
`git diff tests/replays` showing only the planned changes, arena numbers and screenshots where the milestone asks),
marked ✅, committed and pushed. A missed acceptance number is recorded as an open item, never lowered silently.
Ambiguities get the conservative choice, recorded in `docs/decisions.md`. At the end the branch goes to `main` by
a rebase-merged pull request.

**Agreed with the user:**
- Board 60" wide × 44" deep (the core rules give no size; this is the usual Incursion/Strike Force table), with
  12"-deep deployment zones along the long north and south edges, so 20" between the zones.
- Armies: 3 × 5 Intercessors (Space Marines) vs 4 × 10 Termagants (Tyranids). Only the two existing datasheets;
  more unit types are the next task ("Later", item 5).
- Winning stays D-007 (wipe-out wins, otherwise a draw after round 5). No terrain and no objectives in this batch.
- Deployment stays fixed by the scenario file (D-003), with per-unit positions.
- Bots: Easy and Normal learn to play several units; Hard is generalised from the 1v1 duel to the whole army.
- Client: each unit gets its own tint and a number on the board.

## M54: Speed for big games, with no change in play ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-07) · **Decision:** D-068

> Pure optimisation of core's read-only queries and of `src/ai`. **Every decision and every roll stays identical.**
> - Measure first (Release arena `--trace` on the new scenario), then fix the worst paths. Candidates:
>   `target_problem` checks range and engagement before line of sight and builds its blockers once per query
>   (`core/rules/shooting.cpp`); `legal_actions` at a Shoot decision only needs to know whether any shot exists
>   (`core/engine/shooting.cpp`); fewer `GameState`/`Unit` copies in Normal (`model_worth`, `move_score`,
>   `plan_shooting`); cheaper charge prospects; `has_line_of_sight` only if profiling says so.
> - If Instant bot speed still freezes the window on a slow decision, Instant also runs on the worker thread.

**Acceptance**
- Arena per-game output identical before and after on all four scenarios (Easy, Normal and Hard pairs, fixed seeds,
  compared with a Release build of the pre-change commit). All replays byte-identical. ctest green.
- New scenario, Release: Normal p95 decision time ≤ 100 ms and max ≤ 1 s; Hard (which plays as Normal there until
  M57) the same. Before and after numbers recorded.

**Results (2026-11-07):** all acceptance items met (D-068).
- **Identical play:** the arena's new `--per-game 1` report (per game: result, end round, steps, survivors, wounds,
  coherency removals, fight-move counts, rejections, reverts, fallbacks, and FNV-1a hashes of the accepted actions,
  the final state and the decision trace with every reason) was compared with a Release build of `7536893` (M53)
  carrying the same arena patch: 4 scenarios × 6 pairings (easy/easy, normal/normal, hard/hard, hard/normal,
  normal/hard, normal/easy) × seeds 1–200 = 4800 games, every line identical (the 16 required pairings twice).
  Every replay regenerates byte-identical (`Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays`, `M45_`/`M46_`/`M47_Replay`);
  `git diff tests/replays` is empty.
- **Profile first** (callgrind, `-O2 -g`, same games before and after; instructions): Hard vs Hard on 20 Termagants
  (3 games) 33.9 G → 11.8 G; on V1 (6 games) 8.4 G → 5.3 G; Normal vs Normal on the strike scenario (4 games)
  7.4 G → 4.5 G; Easy vs Easy there (10 games) 3.1 G → 0.95 G. Before, 72% of the Hard 20-Termagant games went into
  the rollouts' Easy pile-ins and consolidations (`auto_fight_move`: `in_region` with `hypotf` 33%,
  `check_coherency` 26%), and the strike games into `shot_options` (19%, `unit_visible_to_model` 15%) and
  `GameState` copies (17%, mostly `apply_action`'s rollback copy). After: `auto_fight_move` 43% (of a third of the
  work), the Hard chain table build 18–24% (unchanged, see below), `GameState` copies 3–6%, `shot_options` 4%.
- **Release arena, base → new (200 games each, run back to back on the same machine):**

  with room to spare. Hard's slowest 1v1 decision (20 Termagants) 0.82 s → 0.34 s. Wall times include the arena's
  own live game (its `Match` keeps a reused rollback copy too); single-run maxima vary by ±20% between runs.
- **What changed** (D-068): inline geometry; allocation-free engagement and distance queries; shooting facts shared
  within one `shot_problems` / `shot_options` call, range before line of sight for the yes/no, `has_shot_option` for
  `legal_actions`; an `apply_action` overload with a reused rollback copy (the simulator, `Match`);
  `Simulator::reset`; in the auto moves `trimmed_region`, `hypot_less`, `RayRefusals`, `CoherencyWithOneMoved`,
  sines and cosines once, the target gap by base group. Instant bot speed now decides on the worker thread too (at
  most 15 ms of waiting per frame), so a slow decision never freezes the window; `--bot-steps` stays synchronous.
- **Tests (new):** core `R03_03_Coherency.OneModelMovedAgreesWithTheFullCheck`, `M54_ShotOptions.*` (2),
  `M54_ShotProblems.SharedFactsGiveEveryDeclarationItsOwnReason`, `M54_Queries.*`, `M54_ApplyAction.*` (2),
  `M54_Match.ACopiedMatchPlaysOnTheSameAsTheOriginal`; client `M54_ReachRegion.*` (4: `hypot_less` and
  `point_segment_closer_than` against `std::hypot` with edge and non-finite values, `trimmed_region` and
  `RayRefusals` against `in_region` over the reach areas of random play); ai
  `M54_Simulator.ResetIsTheSameAsANewSimulator`, `M54_Driver.InstantOnTheWorkerHandsOverTheSynchronousBotsActions`,
  `M54_Arena.ThePerGameReportIsAFunctionOfTheGames`.
  `M39_NoRulesInTheBot` now looks for the simulator's 5-argument `apply_action` call. ctest (Debug, serial): 742 →
  757 tests, all pass, 55.4 s → 48.4 s.
- **Open (for M57):** the Hard chain table build (`ChainTable::build_half_turn`) is now the largest single cost of a
  1v1 Hard game (18–24%); it was left alone because its values depend on the order of its sums. `has_line_of_sight`
  itself and Normal's move scoring are unchanged.

## M56: Easy and Normal across several units ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-07) · **Rules:** 01.04, 09.02, 10.02, 11.02, 12.02–12.08 · **Decision:** D-066

> - **Easy:** aims at the closest enemy unit (D-010) instead of the first one, for moves and charge moves. It walks
>   its full Move while the closest enemy is far away (a threshold the V1 board never reaches), then the old 2"
>   steps. V1 play, and so `m47_default_casualties.json`, stays identical.
> - **Normal:**
>   - Move scoring weighs our best shot over every enemy unit, and the reply (shooting and charge threat) of every
>     enemy unit, not only the closest one.
>   - Activation order: which unit moves, shoots or charges next is chosen by a stated heuristic, not unit id order.
>   - Charge planner: no double counting across several targets; holding value and expected losses use the units
>     actually involved.
>   - Casualty worth, "front" and consolidation are measured against the attacker or the relevant enemies.
>   - With one unit per side every change reduces to today's maths, so `m45_bot_game.json` and
>     `m46_bot_fight_moves.json` stay byte-identical. If a 1v1 change proves unavoidable, D-066 records why, the
>     replay is regenerated on purpose, and V1 strength is shown to be no worse.

**Acceptance** (Release arena, 200 seeds, both seat assignments)
- New scenario: Normal beats Easy in at least 75% of games; the new Normal beats the M55 Normal (built from that
  commit in a scratch worktree) in at least 55% of games in both seats.
- V1 and bot scenarios: Normal vs Easy no worse than recorded in M41. Zero rejected actions and reverts.
- A seeded ctest subset on the new scenario (`M56_...`). Decision times within M54's targets. Replays as above.

**Results (2026-11-07):** design in D-066. Met: V1 and both bot scenarios play exactly as before, replays
byte-identical, the M55 regression switch reproduces M55, the Marine-seat targets, zero rejections / reverts /
fallbacks, decision times. **Open:** both Tyranid-seat targets (no Tyranid side has won a game on any scenario at any
level; Normal's Termagants now draw almost every game against Easy, and play level with M55 against Normal).
- **What changed** (bot only, D-066): Easy aims at the closest enemy unit, walks its full Move while it is more than
  12" + M away (then the old 2" steps), and charges the closest eligible target, its charge move aimed at that
  target. Normal: the move score adds every other enemy unit as a shooting target and its next-turn reply
  (shooting and charge threat; a winning side counts only units that can reach no other of its units, a losing
  side every unit it would be the closest target of); the unit closest to the enemy moves first; a charge with
  several targets shares the charger's strength (no double counting), holding and charging add the other units'
  threat, and our shooting's kills come off the unit actually shot; casualty worth against the attacker, the
  "front" against the units engaged with it or that may charge it, consolidation against every enemy unit.
  Regression switch: `BotConfig::generation` = `Generation::M55`, arena levels `easy-m55` / `normal-m55` (arena and
  tests only).
- **1v1 unchanged:** the arena's `--per-game 1` report of a Release build of `d76e51b` (M55) and of this build, on
  V1, Termagants first and 20 Termagants, seeds 1–200, pairings easy/easy, normal/normal, hard/hard, hard/normal,
  normal/easy, easy/normal, easy/hard and hard/easy (24 runs, 4800 games): every line identical (result, counts,
  hashes of the actions, the final state and the decision trace with its reasons). So Normal vs Easy there is
  exactly as recorded in M41 (V1: 200 wins, mean end round 1.92). The four replay regenerations
  (`Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays`, `M45_` / `M46_` / `M47_Replay`) leave `tests/replays` unchanged.
- **The switch reproduces M55:** on the Strike scenario, `normal-m55`/`normal-m55`, `normal-m55`/`easy-m55`,
  `easy-m55`/`easy-m55` and `easy-m55`/`normal-m55` (seeds 1–200) give the same per-game lines as the M55 build's
  normal/normal, normal/easy, easy/easy and easy/normal (800 games, trace hashes included).
- **Strike 44x60, Release, seeds 1–200** (every run clean: 0 rejected, 0 `UnitMoveReverted`, 0 fallbacks, 0
  unfinished; "-m55" = the M55 level through the switch):

  | A (Marines) vs B (Tyranids) | A wins | B wins | Draws | Mean result A | End round | Survivors A / B | Wounds lost A / B | p95 / max ms |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | easy vs easy | 188 | 0 | 12 | +0.940 | 4.24 | 12.72 / 0.17 | 6.00 / 39.83 | 0.14 / 8 |
  | easy-m55 vs easy-m55 | 93 | 0 | 107 | +0.465 | 5.00 | 13.23 / 2.29 | 4.92 / 37.72 | 0.10 / 4 |
  | **normal vs easy** | **200** | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | 2.56 | 14.25 / 0.00 | 2.63 / 40.00 | 2.4 / 71 |
  | normal-m55 vs easy | 200 | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | 2.50 | 14.19 / 0.00 | 2.77 / 40.00 | 2.1 / 75 |
  | **easy vs normal** | 3 | **0** | 197 | +0.015 | 5.00 | 12.80 / 9.47 | 5.79 / 30.52 | 2.5 / 32 |
  | easy vs normal-m55 | 1 | 0 | 199 | +0.005 | 5.00 | 12.68 / 9.46 | 6.01 / 30.55 | 2.0 / 19 |
  | **normal vs normal-m55** | **200** | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | 3.58 | 14.41 / 0.00 | 2.19 / 40.00 | 5.1 / 69 |
  | **normal-m55 vs normal** | 199 | **0** | 1 | +0.995 | 3.57 | 14.27 / 0.01 | 2.53 / 39.99 | 4.3 / 40 |
  | normal-m55 vs normal-m55 | 200 | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | 3.60 | 14.19 / 0.00 | 2.67 / 40.00 | 4.1 / 27 |
  | normal vs normal | 200 | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | 3.62 | 14.41 / 0.00 | 2.21 / 40.00 | 5.2 / 68 |
  | hard vs hard (plays as Normal) | 200 | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | 3.62 | 14.41 / 0.00 | 2.21 / 40.00 | 5.4 / 91 |

  Seeds 1–1000, the same build: easy vs normal 11 / 0 / 989 (Tyranid survivors 9.33, Marine wounds lost 5.66),
  easy vs normal-m55 5 / 0 / 995 (9.43, 5.78); normal-m55 vs normal 997 / 0 / 3 (end round 3.58, Marine wounds lost
  2.51), normal-m55 vs normal-m55 996 / 0 / 4 (3.60, 2.53); normal vs normal-m55 995 / 0 / 5 (3.60, Marine wounds lost
  2.16).
- **Against the literal targets:**
  - Normal beats Easy in at least 75%: **met as Marines** (200 of 200). **Open as Tyranids** (0 of 200: they draw
    197). Seat-relative: the Tyranid side's mean result is −0.015 against Easy-vs-Easy's −0.940 (−0.465 with
    the M55 Easy), with 9.47 Termagants left on average against 0.17.
  - The new Normal beats the M55 Normal in at least 55%: **met as Marines** (200 of 200; against M55's own Marines
    the same opponent gives 200 wins too, but the new Marines lose fewer wounds, 2.19 against 2.67, and end
    sooner, 3.58 against 3.60). **Open as Tyranids** (0 of 200). Seat-relative: level with M55 within the noise
    (200 seeds: 1 draw against 0, the Marines' end round 3.57 against 3.60 and their wounds lost 2.53 against
    2.67; 1000 seeds: 3 draws against 4, 3.58 against 3.60, 2.51 against 2.53).
  - Against the same Easy the new Normal Termagants are level to slightly below M55's (1000 seeds: 989 draws
    against 995, 9.33 survivors against 9.43, 5.66 wounds dealt against 5.78, about one to two standard errors).
    Shots at other Marine units are what cost them (with marginal exposure on both sides they kept 8.91); the
    losing side's closest-target rule (D-066) recovered most of it. Open for M57/M58.
- **Decision times** (Release, Strike, every bot decision): Normal vs Normal p50 0.004 ms, p95 5.2 ms, max 68 ms
  (M55: 4.1 / 27); Normal vs Easy 2.4 / 71; Easy vs Normal 2.5 / 32; Hard (as Normal) 5.4 / 91. Inside M54's
  100 ms / 1 s.
- **Tests (new, ai):** `M56_Easy.*` (4: aims at the closest unit, not the first; walks its full Move while far;
  a fresh bot replans the full move the same; the charge move goes at its target), `M56_Normal.*` (2: the unit
  closest to the enemy moves first; another unit's threat changes the move), `M56_Charge.*` (2: two targets
  share the charger's strength; M42's ringed-target fixture is charged for the most value),
  `M56_Casualties.WorthIsMeasuredAgainstTheAttacker`, `M56_StrikeGames.*` (2: five Strike games clean; a fresh bot
  gives the played answer at every decision of a Normal and an Easy Strike game), `M56_Regression.TheM55SwitchPlaysAsM55`
  (four games' per-game lines from the M55 build). Changed:
  `M42_ChargeTargets.ChargesBothUnitsWhenTheTargetAloneCannotBeEngaged` now runs with `Generation::M55` (its
  assertions unchanged; its expectation came from M42's double count, D-066). ctest (Debug): 763 → 775 tests, all
  pass. Debug and Release builds clean.

## M57: Hard across several units ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `max` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-07) · **Decision:** D-067

> The design is written into D-067 before the code.
> - The evaluator is generalised from `duel_of` (one unit each) to the army: per-unit-pair exchange values feed an
>   army-level P(win) − P(loss) over (models left per side, battle round, distance bands), still under D-007. The
>   exact 1v1 path stays when each side has one unit, so V1 play and `m45_bot_game.json` are unchanged.
> - Lookahead: the top move plans, per unit and for the whole Movement phase, are checked by the engine-backed
>   Monte Carlo (simulator, the bot's own dice, a fixed work budget in simulated actions, never wall-clock time)
>   with the reply of every enemy unit. `lookahead.cpp`'s first-enemy `enemy_of` is replaced by a real target
>   choice, and `kMaxLine` covers a 60" board.
> - Casualty order, coherency removal, consolidation and charge choices use the army value.

**Acceptance** (Release arena, 200 seeds, both seats)
- New scenario: Hard beats Normal in at least 60% of games in both seats. Hard p95 ≤ 500 ms and max ≤ 3 s per
  decision.
- V1 and the two bot scenarios: Hard's results unchanged. Zero rejections, reverts and fallbacks.
- A seeded ctest subset (`M57_...`). Replays unchanged.

**Results (2026-11-07):** design in D-067. Met: the Space Marine seat (Hard wins 200 of 200 against Normal, sooner
than Normal does), decision times, V1 and both bot scenarios unchanged (per-game hashes), zero rejections / reverts
/ fallbacks / unfinished games, the ctest subset, replays unchanged. **Open:** the Tyranid seat's 60% (no Tyranid side
wins a Strike game at any level); Hard's Tyranids are measurably better than Normal's there (more draws, longer
games), not lowered.
- **What changed** (bot only, D-067): with several units a side Hard values the army: M44's duel chain per pair of
  units (one table per pair of unit types, on a line as long as the board's diagonal: `kMaxLine` 24 → 38 buckets,
  76"), each unit's duels with every enemy unit started when that unit is free of the ones in front (a timeline of
  shared work, each attacker on its closest target in reach at its volley's pace), a unit's fate the product of its
  duels, and D-007 over the army (P(A wins) = the product of side B's units' P(wiped out), and so on; V = P(win) −
  P(loss); tempo and material from each side's longest-lasting unit). The lookahead's rollouts play every unit of
  both sides (moves relative to a real target, the most models in reach, a tie to the closest; Normal's shooting;
  charges at the closest eligible target), from each move plan through the rest of the Movement phase and the
  enemy's whole turn; the plans add the move ending furthest from every enemy model. The unit Normal would move
  moves; a charge is weighed for every unit that may declare; casualties, coherency removals, consolidations and
  charge moves use the army value (front and rear models measured against the attacker). One unit per side keeps
  M44's duel, unchanged. Budgets and `BotWeights{}` unchanged.
- **1v1 unchanged:** the arena's `--per-game 1` report of a Release build of 3afc2f0 (worktree, removed after) and of
  this build, on V1, Termagants first and 20 Termagants, seeds 1–200, pairings hard/hard, hard/normal, normal/hard,
  hard/easy and easy/hard, with normal/normal and easy/easy as controls (21 runs, 4200 games): every line identical
  (result, counts, hashes of the actions, the final state and the decision trace with its reasons), every game
  finished and clean. On the Strike scenario normal/normal and easy/easy are identical too (400 games: Normal and
  Easy are untouched), and the 3afc2f0 build's hard/normal and normal/hard give Normal's own results there (Hard
  played as Normal). The four replay regenerations (`Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays`, `M45_` / `M46_` /
  `M47_Replay`) leave `tests/replays` unchanged (`git status --short tests/replays` empty).
- **Strike 44x60, Release, seeds 1–200** (one process per run, four runs at a time on four cores; every run clean:
  0 rejected, 0 `UnitMoveReverted`, 0 fallbacks, 0 unfinished; the longest game in all of M57's Strike runs took 294
  steps, so the 5000-step cap stays):

  | A (Marines) vs B (Tyranids) | A wins | B wins | Draws | Mean result A | End round | Survivors A / B | Wounds lost A / B | Coherency removals A / B | p50 / p95 / max ms |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | normal vs normal | 200 | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | 3.625 | 14.41 / 0.00 | 2.21 / 40.00 | 0 / 0 | 0.005 / 5.5 / 68 |
  | **hard vs normal** | **200** | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | **3.575** | 14.38 / 0.00 | 2.29 / 40.00 | 0 / 0 | 0.005 / 76.6 / 1164 |
  | **normal vs hard** | 197 | **0** | **3** | +0.985 | **3.670** | 14.44 / 0.05 | 2.12 / 39.95 | 0 / 1 | 0.005 / 64.7 / 482 |
  | hard vs hard | 200 | 0 | 0 | +1.000 | 3.600 | 14.44 / 0.00 | 2.12 / 40.00 | 0 / 3 | 0.005 / 116.2 / 1137 |

- **The Tyranid seat over seeds 1–1000** (Release, against Normal's Space Marines):

  | B (Tyranids) | A wins | Draws | Mean result B | End round (s.e.) | Games reaching round 5 | Termagants left | Marine wounds lost |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Normal | 998 | 2 | −0.998 | 3.610 (0.019) | 54 | 0.003 | 2.184 |
  | **Hard** | 989 | **11** | **−0.989** | **3.692** (0.019) | **78** | **0.023** | 2.141 |

  Their losses are almost all to shooting (Normal against Normal, 60 games: about 14.5 Termagants in battle round 1,
  before they can act, then 13.4 and 9.8; under 0.6 a game in melee), and they deal about 2 wounds a game, so the
  draw (D-007) is all they can play for. A probe that only kept every unit as far away as it could (always an
  Advance) lasted longer than Normal's Termagants (round-3 endings 22% against 40%), which is where the keep-away
  plan and the attention model come from.
- **How it got there** (seeds 1–200 per seat; draws as the Tyranids, mean end round as the Tyranids / as the Space
  Marines): Hard as Normal (3afc2f0): 0, 3.625 / 3.625; the army value without attention and first rollouts (50
  seeds): 0, 3.54 / 3.68; the rollouts shooting and aiming as Normal does (the first try shot nearly-wiped units with
  full volleys and walked every squad at a 1-model unit, so its lookahead was far too hopeful for the Tyranids): 1
  of 50, 3.58 / 3.64; the keep-away plan: 2 of 50, 3.62 / 3.64; attention per attacker: 3, 3.625 / 3.63; the last
  unit's tempo (a unit kept back a round longer had moved the mean over all units by 0.006, below the 0.01 margin): 2,
  3.695 / 3.555; the shared timeline: 3, 3.675 / 3.560; with reach (final): 3, 3.670 / 3.575. Weights swept at the
  0.005 / 0.02 margin, 10 rollouts, share 0.7, 6 static plans and 5 plans: all level within the noise, so
  `BotWeights{}` stays (D-067).
- **Against the literal targets:**
  - Hard beats Normal in at least 60% of games, Space Marine seat: **met** (200 of 200 wins, 100%); it also ends
    games sooner than Normal does (3.575 against 3.625), with slightly more wounds lost (2.29 against 2.21).
  - Tyranid seat: **open** (0 wins of 200; no Tyranid side has won a Strike game at any level). Seat-relative:
    3 draws of 200 against Normal-as-Tyranids' 0, mean result −0.985 against −1.000, end round 3.670 against
    3.625; over 1000 seeds 11 draws against 2, −0.989 against −0.998, end round 3.692 against 3.610 (about three
    standard errors), 78 games reaching battle round 5 against 54, wounds dealt level (2.14 against 2.18).
  - Hard p95 ≤ 500 ms and max ≤ 3 s per decision: **met** (p95 at most 116 ms, max 1.16 s over 200 seeds; 1.15 s
    over 1000 seeds of Normal against Hard).
  - V1 and the two bot scenarios: Hard's results unchanged: **met** (above). Zero rejections, reverts and fallbacks:
    **met** in every run (sweeps included).
  - Seeded ctest subset `M57_...`: **met** (12 tests). Replays unchanged: **met**.
- **Decision times** (Release, Strike, every bot decision of the run): Hard against Normal p50 0.005 ms, p95 76.6 ms,
  max 1164 ms; Normal against Hard 0.005 / 64.7 / 482; Hard against Hard 0.005 / 116.2 / 1137 (Normal against Normal
  0.005 / 5.5 / 68). The army's table (38 buckets) costs about 80 ms once per game and bot; the slowest decisions are
  a Termagant unit's MoveUnits. `ChainTable::build_half_turn` is unchanged (not needed).
- **Tests (new, ai, `tests/ai/m57_test.cpp`):** `M57_Evaluator.*` (3: the army value of one unit each is the duel's,
  term by term along a V1 game; each unit's fate is its duel with its main enemy, the closest unit and not the first,
  and V is the same game for both sides; a unit the enemy is busy in front of lasts longer than the enemy's first
  target, a draw chance), `M57_Line.TheChainLineCoversA60InchBoard` (38 buckets cover the 74.4" diagonal; a duel
  57" across the board has a line over 48" and tells 50" from 56"; the army's line is 38),
  `M57_Lookahead.*` (2: a rollout's move aims at the real target, not side B's first unit; every enemy unit gets its
  move in the rollouts, within the action budget, deterministically), `M57_Casualties.FrontAndRearAreMeasuredAgainstTheAttacker`,
  `M57_Charge.TheCloseSquadDeclaresAndTheChargeIsWorthMoreThanHolding`,
  `M57_Consolidation.ConsolidatesBackIntoTheFightByTheArmyValue`, `M57_StrikeGames.*` (3: a clean Strike game with
  Hard in each seat, seed 12, over in battle round 2; a fresh Hard bot (memo forgotten) gives the played answer at
  every decision of Hard against Hard through side A's first turn and side B's first move, and the same with only the
  game dice changed at every MoveUnits, Charge and casualty decision, on a lighter lookahead to keep Debug quick).
  No existing test changed. ctest (Debug): 775 → 787 tests, all pass (21.8 s with `-j4`).
- **Open for M58:** the Tyranid seat (above); Hard's Tyranids lose a model to coherency in 1 of 200 games against
  Normal (3 in Hard against Hard; Normal's in none), from a casualty order the army value preferred to Normal's in a
  unit about to be wiped out anyway; the army value's attention timeline ignores the attackers' losses and the
  defenders' kiting before a delayed duel starts; tuning `BotWeights` for several units.

## M58: Tuning, bot-game replay, docs ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-07) · **Decision:** D-067

> - Tune `BotWeights` for several units with `--weights` sweeps on the new scenario, without making V1 worse;
>   the values and results go in D-067.
> - Bot-game replay `tests/replays/m58_strike_bot_game.json` (Hard vs Hard, new scenario, a fixed seed that ends
>   in a result rather than at the step cap), with determinism, `TheSavedFileMatchesTheGame` and
>   `M58_Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateStrikeBotGameReplay` tests in `tests/ai/`.
> - Docs: CLAUDE.md (scenarios, the regenerate command, the step cap), README (the new scenario), PLAN.md results;
>   check the README text `tools/package.ps1` writes.

**Acceptance**
- Clean Debug and Release builds, all of ctest, `git diff main -- tests/replays` shows only the two new files.
- Screenshot of a bot-vs-bot game on the new scenario mid-game.
- Manual check left for the user: play the new scenario by mouse on Windows.

**Results (2026-11-08):**
- **Tuning (D-067 "Tuning (M58)"): `BotWeights{}` unchanged.**
  - **The sweep:** Release arena, Strike, 200 seeds per value, with `--weights` one value at a time.
  - **The values swept:** tempo 0.1 and 0.4, material 0.02 and 0.15, share 0.3, survival 0.5 and 2.0, delay 0.25,
    edge 0.15, tempo 0.4 with material 0.02, and twice the rollout budget.
  - **The Tyranid seat** (Normal against Hard, the main target): every value came out at 1–4 draws in 200, against 3
    for the defaults, and a mean end round of 3.62–3.71, against 3.67.
  - **The Space Marine seat** (Hard against Normal): Hard won all 200 games every time, with a mean end round of
    3.50–3.62, against 3.58.
  - **The two best values over 1000 seeds** were both level with the defaults within the noise:
    - Tyranid seat: material 0.02 gave 13 draws and a mean end round of 3.698; tempo 0.4 gave 13 draws and 3.663. The
      defaults gave 11 draws and 3.692.
    - Space Marine seat: tempo 0.4 gave a mean end round of 3.533, against 3.550 for the defaults. It is under one
      standard error better there and worse in the Tyranid seat.
  - **Effect on other scenarios:** none, so V1, the two bot scenarios and `m45_bot_game.json` are untouched. No army-only
    weight was added. The Tyranid seat's 60% target stays open, as at M57.
  - **Runs:** every run was clean (0 rejected, reverted, fallbacks or unfinished). Hard's p95 was at most 83 ms and its
    slowest decision 2.0 s.
- **Bot-game replay** `tests/replays/m58_strike_bot_game.json`:
  - **The game:** Hard against Hard on `strike_44x60.json` with dice seed 11. It runs 159 steps and ends in battle round
    3 with a Space Marine wipe-out (D-007), not at the step cap. Both sides shoot and take casualties (the Marines lose
    4 wounds), and there is a charge, pile-ins on both sides and a fight.
  - **Choosing the seed:** Hard against Hard over seeds 1–200 (`--per-game 1`). Side A won every game, with 183 steps on
    average and the longest at 294.
  - **Regeneration:** regenerating twice gives a byte-identical file. The four older regenerations leave `tests/replays`
    unchanged, and `Replay.SavedReplayFilesPass` and `M38_Lists` pick the new file up.
- **Tests** (new, ai, `tests/ai/m58_test.cpp`):
  - `M58_Replay.TheStrikeBotGameReplaysToItsResultWithCoreOnly` replays the whole file through the engine twice: the
    expect block, the result, 3 + 4 units.
  - `M58_Replay.TheSavedFileMatchesTheGame` checks that Hard against Hard with seed 11 plays exactly the saved actions
    through battle round 1, 64 of the 159 steps. The whole game is about 30 s in Debug; the prefix is about 14 s alone
    and 26 s under `ctest -j8`.
  - `M58_Replay.DISABLED_TheWholeSavedFileMatchesTheGame` checks the whole game.
  - `M58_Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateStrikeBotGameReplay` writes the file.
  - No existing test changed. ctest (Debug): 787 → 789 tests, all pass.
- **Docs:** CLAUDE.md lists the Strike scenario (D-064), the M58 regeneration command and
  `M58_Replay.TheSavedFileMatchesTheGame`, the arena's `--step-cap` default (5000; a Strike game takes at most about
  300 steps), `--per-game 1` and the hidden `easy-m55` / `normal-m55` levels, and the `apply_action` backup overload
  (D-068). README and the README text `tools/package.ps1` writes describe the Strike scenario and how to pick it
  (`package.ps1` already copies every scenario and replay).
- **Final verification (Linux):** clean rebuilds of Debug and Release (no warnings outside third-party raylib);
  ctest 789 of 789 pass; all five regenerations (`Replay`, `M45_`, `M46_`, `M47_`, `M58_Replay`) leave
  `tests/replays` unchanged, and against `main` the only replay files added are `m53_strike_round1.json` and
  `m58_strike_bot_game.json`. Screenshot under `xvfb-run` of Hard vs Hard on Strike after 60 bot steps (round 1,
  the Tyranid move): seven numbered, tinted units, every one in its zone at the start. **Open for the user:** play
  the Strike scenario by mouse on Windows.

## Two rosters on a 44"x60" portrait board (M59–M70)

**Run note (agreed with the user 2026-11-08):** M59–M70 are pre-approved as one batch and run back to back
without `/clear` and without per-milestone sign-off, on branch `rosters-44x60-portrait`. This overrides "one
milestone per session" and "agree each milestone first" for these milestones only.
- **Process:** each milestone is implemented by a sub-agent at the effort shown, then verified, marked ✅,
  committed and pushed. Verification means:
  - clean Debug and Release builds, and all of `ctest`;
  - every replay regeneration leaves `tests/replays` byte-identical apart from the milestone's new file;
  - `--per-game` arena hashes on V1, both bot scenarios and Strike identical to the previous commit wherever
    play must not change;
  - screenshots where the client changes.
- **Design first:** in M61, M62, M65 and M69 the sub-agent writes its decision entry first, which is reviewed
  against the rules text before any code.
- **Every milestone also gives:**
  - new actions and events their log text, action text and dice cards;
  - undo grouping;
  - a `fallback.cpp` / `bot_driver.cpp` answer for a new decision;
  - JSON round trips, with new fields omitted when default so old files stay byte-identical;
  - a scripted replay of its flow, stopped before anything a later milestone changes.
- **Missed targets** are recorded as open items, never lowered silently.
- **Ambiguities** get the conservative choice, recorded in `docs/decisions.md`.
- **At the end** the branch goes to `main` by a rebase-merged pull request.

**Agreed with the user (2026-11-08):**
- **Armies:** the two New Recruit rosters in `docs/datasheets/`. Only the units, their weapons and their weapon
  abilities are used.
  - **Chaos Space Marines (side A, south edge, first turn):**
    - Chaos Lord (Astartes chainblade, plasma pistol);
    - 2 × 10 Legionaries (one squad with chainswords and bolt pistols, one with boltguns and bolt pistols, each led by an Aspiring Champion);
    - 10 Chaos Terminators (accursed weapon and combi-bolter);
    - 5 Havocs (4 lascannons; the champion has a plasma gun and chainsword).
  - **Tyranids (side B, north edge):**
    - Hive Tyrant (bonesword and lash whip, scything talons);
    - 2 × 10 Termagants;
    - 2 × 5 Genestealers;
    - 3 Tyrant Guard;
    - 2 Screamer-killers;
    - a Tyrannofex (120×92 mm oval base).
- **Not used:** stratagems, unit abilities (Synapse, Shadow in the Warp, Skulking Horrors, Death Scream, Onslaught, ...),
  Leader and attached units (the characters stay separate units), Deep Strike, Scouts, Feel No Pain,
  Deadly Demise and Damaged profiles. Their text is still shown on the datasheets.
- **Board:** 44" wide × 60" deep (portrait), with 12" deployment zones on the short north and south edges, so
  36" between the zones. No terrain, no objectives, fixed deployment from the scenario file.
- **Random Characteristics** (app-only, wording approved by the user; not verified against the app):
  - Attacks: each time a model attacks with a weapon whose A is random, roll it for that model and weapon
    (Blast bonus added after).
  - Damage: each time an attack with a random D is allocated to a model after a failed save, roll the damage
    for that attack.
  - The defender still gives one casualty order per batch (D-013).
- **[TWIN-LINKED]:** the player is asked which wound dice to re-roll, only when at least one wound roll failed.
  A "re-roll all failed" shortcut is offered.
- **Oval bases** are done properly, with a facing per model, so the Tyrannofex plays as listed.
- **Result for this scenario only:** a wipe-out still wins. Otherwise, after round 5, the side that destroyed
  more points wins, and equal points is a draw.
  - Points destroyed are the full points of destroyed units, plus points × wounds lost ÷ starting wounds for
    surviving units, compared exactly.
  - Unit points come from the rosters and are stored in the scenario file.
  - Every other scenario keeps D-007.
- **Conservative readings chosen in planning** (recorded in the decisions as they land):
  - an oval's turn must clear enemy bases and the battlefield edge (03.01);
  - Remaining Stationary forbids rotating (09.04);
  - a rotation-only move doesn't count as moving closer (12.03, 12.08, D-022);
  - 17.01 applies to normal and Advance moves only, and monsters may pass friendly monsters (03.01);
  - [BLAST] can't target an engaged MONSTER/VEHICLE (the 17.03 diagram);
  - random A is rolled when the weapon's batch resolves (04.03);
  - random-A melee weapons can't split their attacks across targets.

**What the batch added (summary, M70):**
- *Data:* the fetch tool's fixes and the nine roster datasheets (M59: 02.04–02.07, D-070), the fixture scenarios under
  `tests/data/scenarios/`, and the playable scenario `data/scenarios/rosters_44x60.json` (M67: 14 units, 44"x60"
  portrait, unit points from the rosters, D-076).
- *Weapon abilities:* unknown abilities refused when selected (M60, D-071); [HAZARDOUS] (24.15, with 06.02 and 06.03),
  [SUSTAINED HITS] (24.36), [EXTRA ATTACKS] (24.11) and mixed-loadout default plans (04.01) (M60); [TWIN-LINKED]
  (24.38) with a wound re-roll decision (M61, D-072 applying D-011).
- *Random characteristics:* random A rolled per model and weapon when its batch resolves, random D after the model
  is selected (M62: 04.03, 05.04; D-069 replaces D-002, amends D-013 and D-051; app-only wording approved by the user).
- *MONSTERS and VEHICLES:* moving through enemy infantry (17.01), shooting at and from Engagement Range with the -1s
  (17.03), and the 10.06 penalty added (M63, D-073).
- *Oval bases with a facing:* the geometry kernel (M64: 01.04, 03.01, 06.01), the engine (M65: 01.04, 03.01, 03.02,
  09.04, 11.04, 12.03, 12.08), the client and the bots (M66: dragging and turning an oval with Q / E and Shift+wheel,
  big-base polish; D-074, D-075).
- *The result:* the points tiebreak for the roster scenario only (M67: 07.03 via D-076; every other scenario keeps
  D-007), shown in the Game panel and the victory pop-up; ten unit shades per side (D-065 extended).
- *The bots:* Easy and Normal for the roster units (M68, D-077: per-model loadouts, [HAZARDOUS], the -1s, engaged
  MONSTERS, 17.01, material by points, the tiebreak); Hard for them (M69, D-078: one chain cache per game, the coarse
  wounds grid and compact tables, loadout groups, the tiebreak in the army value, the rollout budget by units, Normal's
  moves for a side of more than 7 units); M70's weight sweep on the rosters (PLAN.md M70 Results).
- *The client:* `--load`, `--pick-dice`, `--pick-models`, `--click-units`, `--turn` and `--window` screenshot flags; the
  re-roll pop-up; the action bar four rows high, fitting the casualty decision (M70).
- *Replays added:* `m59_rosters_fixture_round1`, `m60_hazard_and_extra_attacks`, `m61_twin_linked_reroll`,
  `m62_random_characteristics`, `m63_monsters`, `m65_oval_tyrannofex`, `m67_rosters_round1`,
  `m68_rosters_normal_bot_game` (Normal vs Normal) and `m70_rosters_bot_game` (Hard vs Hard).
- *Play elsewhere unchanged:* V1, both bot scenarios and Strike play exactly as before the batch (the arena's
  `--per-game` hashes against the pre-batch baseline, every milestone), and every pre-batch replay regenerates
  byte-identically.

## M59: Fetch tool fixes and the nine new datasheets ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-08) · **Rules:** 02.04–02.07 · **Decision:** D-070

> **`tools/fetch_bsdata.py` fixes:**
> - Composition comes from the model types, counting champions. Single models are min 1 / max 1.
> - Wargear is read recursively, skipping the Crusade, Weapon Modifications, Enhancements and Crusade Relic
>   subtrees.
> - Weapon profiles behind `infoLinks` are dereferenced.
> - Each profile gets its own ranged or melee type.
> - `default_weapons` comes from min-1 entries.
> - "AxBmm" bases become `oval` (`diameter_mm` = width B, `length_mm` = length A). Bases it can't read are
>   skipped with a warning instead of crashing.
> - "Hunter" profiles are marked conditional, and the loader refuses them.
>
> **Datasheets.** Generate `hive_tyrant`, `genestealers`, `tyrant_guard`, `screamer_killer`, `tyrannofex`,
> `chaos_lord`, `legionaries`, `chaos_terminator_squad` and `havocs` at the pinned commit 0f90355.
> - `intercessor_squad.json` and `termagants.json` are regenerated only if their engine-relevant content
>   comes out identical.
> - Differences from the roster are recorded in D-070. BSData wins.
>
> **Loader.** The unit loader reads the weapon type per profile. Oval bases are still refused at placement
> until M65.
>
> **Fixture scenarios.** Round-base scenarios under `tests/data/scenarios/` (not shown in the client): Chaos
> Space Marines vs Tyranids without the Tyrannofex, used for testing M60–M64.

**Acceptance**
- `datasheet_test` checks each new file against the roster: stats, InSv, base, weapons, profiles, abilities and
  composition. Loader tests.
- D-004 random play over the fixture scenarios, with weapons not implemented yet refused cleanly (the D-026
  exception backup is never hit).
- Old play and replays unchanged.

**Results (2026-11-08):** design in D-070.
- **Fetch tool** (`tools/fetch_bsdata.py`):
  - *Composition* comes from the model types and the groups holding them, champions counted (Legionaries and
    Terminators 5–10, Havocs 5–5, Genestealers 5–10, Tyrant Guard 3–6). Single models are 1–1.
  - *Wargear* is read recursively, skipping the Crusade, Weapon Modifications, Enhancements and Crusade Relic
    Upgrades subtrees. Weapon profiles behind `infoLinks` are read.
  - *Weapon type* is per profile: a mixed weapon writes `"type"` on each profile.
  - *Default weapons* come from min-1 entries and required groups' defaults, at any depth.
  - *Bases:* "AxBmm" is an oval (`diameter_mm` = width B, `length_mm` = length A); other unreadable bases become
    `null` with a warning.
  - *Hunter profiles* are marked with `"condition"`.
  - *Also added:* `set` modifiers conditioned only on the unit's or model's identity are applied (the Havocs'
    chainsword is A5, as in the roster), and Abilities profiles behind infoLinks get their text.
  - *Robustness:* no crash on about twenty other units (vehicles with "Hull", "32mm Flying", ...).
- **Datasheets:** the nine files are in `data/units/` at `0f90355`. Re-running the tool on `intercessor_squad.json`
  and `termagants.json` gives identical content apart from the `fetched` date, so they were left untouched.
  - Every roster stats row (M/T/Sv/W/Ld/OC/InSv/base) and weapon profile with its abilities matches.
  - The differences (an extra LEADER keyword, duplicate "Leader" abilities, Deadly Demise/Scouts without their
    value, the Genestealers' Stealth) are display-only and recorded in D-070.
- **Loader** (`unit_loader.cpp`):
  - an optional per-profile `"type"`;
  - ovals checked (`length_mm` ≥ `diameter_mm`);
  - a null base refused with a D-070 reason;
  - conditional profiles left out of the weapon and listed in `Datasheet::conditional_profiles` (a weapon with only
    those is dropped).
  - `scenario_loader.cpp` still refuses non-round bases at placement.
- **Fixtures** (`tests/data/scenarios/`, not listed by the client or packaged):
  - `rosters_no_tyrannofex_44x60.json`: 13 units, 72 models, the roster loadouts, 12" zones on the short edges
    36" apart;
  - `havocs_vs_hive_tyrant_24x24.json`: the two 8" apart.
  - `test_helpers.hpp` gains `load_rosters_fixture()` and `load_rosters_fixture_closer(in)` (the Tyranids moved
    south, for random play that reaches shooting and fighting).
- **Replay** `tests/replays/m59_rosters_fixture_round1.json` (112 steps): round 1 movement for both sides, then in
  round 2 a boltgun volley at the front Genestealers (3 slain, 1 wounded). It stops at Side B's first Move Units
  decision of round 2. It embeds its initial state, so the client's replay list and the package load it without
  `tests/data` (checked under `xvfb-run`, as is the fixture via `--scenario`).
- **Tests** (all new):
  - `tests/roster_datasheet_test.cpp`: `R02_Datasheet.*` for the nine files plus Termagants (stats, InSv, base,
    every roster weapon profile with its abilities, composition, model types and defaults), and no conditional
    profiles in any shipped file.
  - `datasheet_test.cpp`: `R02_04_Weapons.EachProfileCanCarryItsOwnType`, `AProfileTypeStillNeedsAMatchingRange`,
    `ProfilesWithoutTypeTakeTheWeaponsType`, `ConditionalProfileIsLeftOutAndListed`,
    `WeaponWithOnlyConditionalProfilesCannotBeEquipped`; and `R02_02_Profiles.OvalBaseReadsWidthAndLength`,
    `OvalBaseShorterThanItsWidthIsDataError`, `NullBaseIsDataError`.
  - `scenario_test.cpp`: `R02_06_UnitComposition.RostersFixtureLoadsThirteenUnitsWithTheRosterModelCounts`,
    `R02_07_WargearOptions.RostersFixtureCarriesTheRosterLoadouts`, `R03_02_SetUp.RostersFixtureIsALegalSetUp`,
    `R03_02_SetUp.HavocsVsHiveTyrantFixtureLoads`, `D008_Formation.TheTyrannofexOvalBaseIsStillRefusedAtPlacement`.
  - `engine_property_test.cpp`:
    - `D004_LegalActions.RandomPlayFromTheRostersFixture`: the full D-004 checks on 4 games (36", 10" and 0.5"
      apart). Each game ends, the state round-trips, and every fired or fought profile has fixed A/D and no
      [TWIN-LINKED]/[DEVASTATING WOUNDS].
    - `D004_LegalActions.RandomGamesFromTheRostersFixtureFinish`: 10 more games.
    - `D026_UnimplementedAbilities.RosterWeaponsTheEngineCantResolveAreRefusedWithARuleCitedReason`: neither the
      lascannon nor the bonesword is offered, and declaring them is refused with "D-002: ..." and "24.38: ...".
  - `replay_test.cpp`: `Replay.RandomGamesFromTheRostersFixtureReplayIdentically`.
  - No existing test changed. ctest (Debug): 789 → 817 tests, all pass.
- **Acceptance:**
  - *Datasheets and loader tests:* **met** (above).
  - *D-004 random play on the fixtures, with unimplemented weapons refused cleanly:* **met**.
    - Random A/D (D-002) and [TWIN-LINKED] (D-026) are refused by validation. The D-026 rollback is never needed:
      no test throws.
    - *Accepted silently until M60, as expected:* the Hive Tyrant's scything talons (Extra Attacks, an Unknown
      ability) and the plasma pistol / plasma gun supercharge profiles ([HAZARDOUS], not implemented). Random play
      uses both.
  - *Old play and replays unchanged:* **met**.
    - `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the four ai regenerations leave every existing replay byte-identical;

- **Open:** a datasheet view of a mixed ranged/melee weapon would list all its profiles under the first profile's

## M60: Unknown abilities refused; Hazardous, Sustained Hits, Extra Attacks; mixed-loadout default plans ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-08) · **Rules:** 24.15, 06.03, 24.36, 24.11, 04.01 · **Decision:** D-071 (extends D-026)

> **Unknown abilities.** Any weapon profile with an ability the engine doesn't know is refused at selection.
>
> **[HAZARDOUS].** After the unit's attacks, make one hazard roll per Hazardous weapon selected. The mortal
> wounds go through `OrderCasualties`, with its own pending field.
>
> **[SUSTAINED HITS X].** Each critical hit adds X hits that are not critical.
>
> **[EXTRA ATTACKS].** A model takes all its Extra Attacks weapons plus one other melee weapon if possible. This
> needs the one-weapon check, `melee_options` and the D-021 "every able model declares" rule relaxed, and the
> client melee plan and the minimal bot plan updated.
>
> **Default plans.** The client's default shooting and melee plans give every model its own weapon, instead of
> only the models that match the first option. They never default to a Hazardous profile.

**Acceptance**
- Tests `R24_15_`, `R24_36_`, `R24_11_`, `D071_` and client `M60_`, plus a replay.
- Units with a single loadout play exactly as before (hashes). Replays unchanged.

**Results (2026-11-08):** design in D-071 (extends D-026, amends D-021).
- **Unknown abilities refused** (`rules/attacks.cpp`):
  - `unimplemented_ability_problem` refuses any `ability::Unknown` with "D-071: '<profile>' has [<TEXT>], which the
    engine does not implement yet; weapons with it can't be selected". So `shot_options`, the Shoot templates,
    `melee_options`, validation and the bots' `profile_supported` all leave such profiles out, and the attack
    sequence throws on one as a second safety net.
  - No weapon equipped in V1, the bot scenarios or Strike has one (a test checks every loadout).
  - `ability::SustainedHits{x}` and `ability::ExtraAttacks` are parsed now. A random X or keyword-conditioned form
    stays Unknown and is refused.
  - JSON: `{"kind": "SustainedHits", "x": 1}` and `{"kind": "ExtraAttacks"}`. An old file's
    `{"kind": "Unknown", "text": ...}` is parsed again on load.
- **[HAZARDOUS]** (`engine/attack_flow.cpp`):
  - *Count:* recorded in Select Weapons as the distinct (model, weapon) pairs with a [HAZARDOUS] profile selected
    (`hazardous_weapons_selected`; a split melee weapon counts once), in `AttackFlow::hazard_rolls`.
  - *Rolls:* made after every batch is resolved or lost (D-012), event `HazardRollsMade`: 1 mortal wound per 1-2,
    3 if every model is a MONSTER/VEHICLE.
  - *Mortal wounds:* kept in `AttackFlow::pending_mortal_wounds` (both fields omitted from JSON when 0). The
    attacking unit's player answers `OrderCasualties{attacker}`, always asked (D-051). The dispatch and
    `preview_casualties` tell it apart from 05.04 and Desperate Escape. `inflict_mortal_wounds_by_priority` is
    shared with 09.07.
  - *Shooting and fighting alike.* A self-wipe ends the activation (the fight goes to the next unit). No roll is
    made once no enemy model is left (D-007 ends the game first).
  - The bots' state hash includes the two fields only when set.
- **[SUSTAINED HITS X]** (`rules/attack_sequence.cpp`):
  - `HitRolls::sustained_hits` holds X per critical hit (JSON omitted when 0). `hits()` includes them and
    `rolled_hits()` doesn't, so the wound rolls cover every hit.
  - Torrent and modified 6s are not critical. With two instances the highest X is used (24.02).
  - Bot odds: `HitWoundOdds::sustained` (in the cache key), and `expected_outcome` mixes exactly over 0, 1 or 1 + X
    hits per die. Profiles without it take the old code path unchanged.
- **[EXTRA ATTACKS]** (`rules/fight.cpp`):
  - `melee_problems` accepts several declarations for a model with [EXTRA ATTACKS] weapons: all of them, plus
    exactly one other melee weapon if it has one it can use. Refused weapons (D-002 / D-026 / D-071) count as absent.
  - New rule-cited refusals: a missing [EXTRA ATTACKS] weapon, a missing other weapon, two other weapons, or the
    same weapon twice. A model without [EXTRA ATTACKS] keeps 04.01's message.
  - It is excluded from D-006's attack-time abilities. `is_extra_attacks_weapon` is in `rules/attacks.hpp`.
  - The Hive Tyrant's talons are its legal selection alone while the bonesword is refused (until M61); then it must
    take both.
- **Default plans** (`client_2d/logic/shot_plan.cpp`, `melee_plan.cpp`):
  - *Single ranged loadout:* exactly the M12 default (every V1, bot and Strike unit).
  - *Mixed loadout:* each model fires its own weapons at the unit default's target. A weapon that can't reach it
    fires at its own first target, and 24.07 keeps the set with more weapons on the target (ties go to the
    non-pistol set).
  - *Never [HAZARDOUS]:* the default is a non-Hazardous profile of the same weapon, else another option.
  - *Melee:* each model's main weapon goes at the default target where it is engaged with it, and its [EXTRA
    ATTACKS] weapons are added (`extra_declarations_for`, `main_options_for`).
  - *Editors and menus:* the board menus count models rather than declarations, describe only the chosen profile's
    shots, and the per-model grids list a model's extra declarations.
  - *Client text:* log text and dice cards for sustained hits ("-> 3 hits (1 from [SUSTAINED HITS])"), a 24.15 hazard
    card ("... after its attacks ([HAZARDOUS] weapons)"), and help text and effect lines for the three abilities.
- **Bots:**
  - Easy uses the plans above.
  - Normal's melee plan picks candidate *sets* per model (main weapon plus [EXTRA ATTACKS] weapons), and
    `best_melee` / `unit_melee_volley` add [EXTRA ATTACKS] weapons. Hard plays Normal's melee.
  - Fixed a dangling reference in Normal's `melee_candidates`: undefined behaviour with attack splits, which crashed
    a roster-fixture bot game in Debug. See the hash note under Acceptance.
- **Replay** `tests/replays/m60_hazard_and_extra_attacks.json` (22 steps, seed 2):
  - The Havocs vs Hive Tyrant fixture, with the Tyrant carrying a heavy venom cannon instead of its bonesword (an
    embedded state, so the talons alone stay legal after M61).
  - The champion fires its plasma gun on supercharge, the hazard roll fails and Side A gives the mortal wound to the
    last Havoc. The Tyrant moves, charges into base contact and fights first with its talons alone, slaying the
    wounded Havoc. The Havocs fight back.
  - It stops at Side B's first Consolidate decision. Checked in the client under `xvfb-run` (`--replay`, frames 2
    and 18).
- **Tests** (all new unless listed under "Changed"):
  - `tests/m60_abilities_test.cpp` (31):
    - `R24_15_Hazardous.*` (12): the roll after the attacks, none without the ability, the attacker's casualty
      decision and preview, one roll per weapon, 3 mortal wounds for a MONSTER, the roll after a D-012 loss, none
      after the last enemy model, a self-wipe in shooting and in melee, a melee roll, a split weapon counting once,
      and round trips.
    - `R24_36_SustainedHits.*` (7) and `R24_11_ExtraAttacks.*` (6).
    - `D071_UnknownAbilities.*` (6): the reason, not offered for shooting, a model unable to fight, the engine
      throwing, every roster profile with one refused, and the old loadouts clean.
  - `weapon_ability_test.cpp`: `R24_36_SustainedHits.ParsesSustainedHitsX` and `R24_11_ExtraAttacks.ParsesExtraAttacks`.
  - `serialization_test.cpp`: `D071_UnknownAbilities.UnknownTextSavedBeforeTheEngineKnewItLoadsAsTheAbility`.
  - `engine_property_test.cpp`: `D004_LegalActions.RandomPlayExercisesHazardousSustainedHitsAndExtraAttacks`. This is
    D-004 random play on the roster fixture with stand-ins (heavy bolters with Sustained Hits, a usable bonesword);
    it asserts that hazard rolls, sustained hits and two-weapon Extra Attacks fights happen.
  - `tests/client/m60_test.cpp` (10): `M60_DefaultPlans.*` (single-loadout plans equal the M12 algorithm on every
    Shoot / fight random play reaches in V1 and Strike; Havocs; boltgun Legionaries; Hazardous listed first; the
    Tyrant with and without a usable bonesword; mixed melee) and `M60_ClientText.*`.
  - `tests/ai/m60_test.cpp` (7): sustained-hits odds within 2% of 20,000 engine batches, `profile_supported`, Extra
    Attacks in the volley, `best_melee` and every level's Fight action, no level firing a refused weapon, and clean
    Easy/Normal/Hard games on both fixtures.
  - *Changed, for a rule reason:* `Serialization.EveryWeaponAbilityRoundTrips` and
    `M24_RulesHelp.EveryWeaponAbilityHasPlainHelp` list the two new variant alternatives (their size guards), and the
    second now expects [HAZARDOUS] to be in the game. `R24_Abilities.UnknownAbilityKeepsOriginalTextAndDoesNotThrow`
    drops "Sustained Hits 1", which is parsed now. `R02_Datasheet.HiveTyrantMatchesTheRoster` and
    `TyrannofexMatchesTheRosterWithAnOvalBase` expect `ExtraAttacks{}` / `SustainedHits{1}` instead of Unknown text.
  - *Test helpers:* the random Fight fill and `first_melee_options` declare [EXTRA ATTACKS] weapons too (unchanged
    for other models, so old random games are the same), and `play_and_check` can collect events.
  - ctest (Debug): 817 → 869 tests, all pass.
- **Acceptance:**
  - *Tests and a replay:* **met** (above).
  - *Single-loadout play unchanged:* **met for every M60 rule and plan change; 3 games change because of the UB fix,
    accepted at review.** The old code read freed memory, so those games' results were not defined behaviour.
    Later milestones compare against the M60 build's output (`baseline/m60`).
    - With the old `melee_candidates` lines restored, the Release arena's `--per-game` hashes match `baseline/main`
      for every game of all 24 pairings (V1, both bot scenarios, Strike).
    - With the fix, 23 pairings are identical. Strike Normal vs Easy differs in 3 of 200 games (seeds 23, 65, 150):
      Normal now weighs the splits the undefined behaviour used to garble.
    - Reverting the two lines restores identical hashes, but then a Normal game on the roster fixture crashes in
      Debug, so the fixture game test would have to leave Normal out.
    - `M60_DefaultPlans.SingleLoadoutUnitsPlanExactlyAsBefore` compares the plans directly.
  - *Replays unchanged:* **met, with one rule-driven byte change.** `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the four ai
    regenerations leave every replay from M5 to M58 byte-identical. `m59_rosters_fixture_round1.json` embeds its
    initial state, and the regeneration now writes the talons' ability as `{"kind": "ExtraAttacks"}` instead of
    `{"kind": "Unknown", "text": "Extra Attacks"}`. That is the only change; its steps and expectation are the same,
    and the old file still loads to the same state (Unknown text is parsed again).
- **Open:**
  - The bots don't price the hazard of a supercharge profile (Normal and Hard may pick it; M68).
  - The per-model editors still choose one declaration per model (the others are listed, not editable), and the
    Split popup splits only the main weapon.
