## M61: [TWIN-LINKED] with a re-roll decision ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** design first, then pre-approved · **Rules:** 24.38, D-011 · **Decision:** D-072

> **Flow.** `start_batch` is split at the wound rolls. When a Twin-linked attack's wound roll fails, the
> attacker gets the `RerollWoundRolls` decision:
> - "re-roll all failed" is listed first, then "re-roll none";
> - any subset is valid;
> - each raw die is re-rolled once, then modifiers apply (D-011);
> - the event is `WoundRollsRerolled`.
>
> **Other weapons** keep exactly the old dice order.
>
> **Client.** A dice pop-up where you pick the dice to re-roll.
>
> **Bots.** They re-roll every failed die, and their odds use p + (1−p)p.

**Acceptance**
- Tests `R24_38_`, `D072_` and client `M61_`, round trips and a replay.
- Old play and replays unchanged.

**Results (2026-11-08):** design in D-072 (amends D-026, applies D-011), reviewed before any code.
- **Engine** (`engine/attack_flow.cpp`, `rules/attack_sequence`):
  - `start_batch` is split after the wound rolls. A [TWIN-LINKED] batch with a failed wound roll (the 05.02 result
    Fail) waits in `AttackFlow::wound_rerolls`, and the attacking unit's player gets `RerollWoundRolls{attacker}`
    (24.38). In the Fight phase that can be the player whose turn it is not (01.03).
  - `legal_actions` lists every failed die first, then none. Any subset of distinct dice is valid, successes too;
    out-of-range and repeated indices are refused (24.38, D-011).
  - The chosen dice are re-rolled once each, in index order, before modifiers (`reroll_wound_rolls`, sharing
    `wound_roll_result` with `make_wound_rolls`), then 05.03 goes on. Event `WoundRollsRerolled` (old and new dice,
    the new totals), also for "Keep".
  - No failed roll, or no ability: nothing asked, the same dice as before.
  - [SUSTAINED HITS]' extra hits' wound rolls are re-rollable too.
  - `resolve_attack_batch` takes the re-roll choice as an input.
  - [TWIN-LINKED] is out of `unimplemented_ability_problem` (D-026 amended); [DEVASTATING WOUNDS] stays refused.
  - JSON: the pending field is written only while it waits; the new decision, action and event are appended to their
    variants.
- **M60 carry-over, done here:** `decision_rule(state, decision)` / `describe(state, decision)` cite 06.02 for a
  casualty decision over mortal wounds ([HAZARDOUS], Desperate Escape) and 05.04 for saves; `validate_action`'s
  messages, the casualty validation messages, the action bar's headline and the replay viewer's "Waiting for" line
  use them. (Removed from M60's open list.)
- **Client:**
  - `RerollPicks` (logic) and a re-roll pop-up (`reroll_popup.cpp`): the wound dice, failed ones outlined in red, a
    click picks or drops a die; "Re-roll selected", "Re-roll all failed", "Keep".
  - The action bar's buttons are "Re-roll all failed (n)" first (what `--steps` clicks), "Keep the wound rolls", and
    "Re-roll selected (k)" once dice are picked.
  - Log text, action text, a "Wound re-rolls" dice card (24.38: which dice are re-rolled before the roll; their new
    faces, old -> new and the new totals after it; none for Keep), the wound card's note, the help and the hit card's
    ability line. A dice card's result line now wraps at the pop-up's edge (only the re-roll card's can be long).
  - The answer is its own Undo group; undoing it restores the dice position, so the same answer gives the same dice.
    The automatic settings never answer it.
  - `--load <save.json>` starts on a saved game (the Save / Load format; not with `--scenario`, `--replay` or
    `--seed`; the save's players unless `--bot` / `--bot-level`; a bad file exits with code 2), and
    `--pick-dice <i,j,...>` picks dice in the first re-roll pop-up, both for screenshots. The flags are parsed in
    `wh40k_client_logic` (`launch_options`, moved from `main.cpp`).
- **Bots:**
  - Every level re-rolls every failed wound roll and no other (`reroll_failed_wound_rolls`, Easy; Normal, Hard and the
    lookahead's rollouts fall through to it). `fallback.cpp` prefers it; the driver gives it the step pause.
  - Odds: a Twin-linked profile's wound chance is p + (1−p)p and its critical chance c + (1−p)c, enumerated through
    core's functions. Twin-linked is in the cache key; other profiles take the old arithmetic unchanged. Hard's chain
    tables get it through `expected_outcome`.
  - `profile_supported` lets it through. The state hash includes the pending re-roll only while it waits.
- **Replay** `tests/replays/m61_twin_linked_reroll.json` (22 steps, seed 1, embedded state):
  - The Havocs vs Hive Tyrant fixture as it is. The Tyrant moves, charges into base contact and fights first with its
    real bonesword ([TWIN-LINKED]) and its talons, bonesword first.
  - A wound roll fails, Side B re-rolls every failed one (1 -> 5), then saves, casualties, the talons, and the Havocs
    fighting back.
  - It stops at Side B's first Consolidate decision. Checked in the replay viewer (frame 17: "Waiting for: ... (24.38)").
- **Tests** (all new unless listed under "Changed"):
  - `tests/m61_twin_linked_test.cpp` (23): `R24_38_TwinLinked.*` (16): asked only after a failed roll, not when all
    wound or nothing hits, all failed, a subset, a success re-rolled, Keep, index order, modifiers after the re-roll,
    once only, a critical from a re-rolled 6, sustained hits, the dice stream without the ability, the Fight phase with
    the player whose turn it is not, validation, the weapons offered. `D072_TwinLinked.*` (5): old loadouts have no
    Twin-linked weapon, the pending state's round trip and omission, an old attack flow, the JSON names, and
    `resolve_attack_batch` matching the engine. `D072_DecisionRule.*` (2).
  - `movement_test.cpp`: `D072_DecisionRule.DesperateEscapeCasualtiesCite0602`. `m60_abilities_test.cpp`:
    `R24_11_ExtraAttacks.TheTyrantMustTakeItsTwinLinkedBoneswordWithItsTalons`. `engine_property_test.cpp`:
    `D004_LegalActions.RandomPlayExercisesTwinLinkedReRolls` (the real bonesword and a stand-in fleshborer hive; all
    failed, none and other subsets all happen, and sustained hits' rolls are re-rolled). The random-play helper picks a
    random subset a third of the time (only at this decision, so other games are unchanged).
  - `tests/client/m61_test.cpp` (12): `M61_RerollPicks.*`, `M61_DecisionPanel.*`, `M61_ClientText.*`, `M61_Undo.*`,
    `M61_Saves.*`, `M61_LaunchOptions.*` (a good and a bad file, the flag combinations).
  - `tests/ai/m61_test.cpp` (7): every level and the fallback, the state hash, the rollouts, the exact odds, 20,000
    engine batches (with and without sustained hits), `profile_supported`, clean bot games on both fixtures.
  - *Changed, for the rule reason ([TWIN-LINKED] is no longer refused; each keeps its check with a still-refused
    stand-in):* `R24_38_TwinLinked.IsRefusedUntilReRollsAreImplemented` is now
    `IsResolvedWhileDevastatingWoundsIsStillRefused`; `D026_UnimplementedAbilities.ATwinLinkedWeaponIsRejectedAndNotOffered`
    is now `ADevastatingWoundsRangedWeaponIsRejectedAndNotOffered`; `R24_37_Torrent.AbilityEffectsMarkTorrentActiveAndAssaultNot`
    (Twin-linked is active); `D004_LegalActions.RandomPlayFromTheRostersFixture` (the bonesword may be used) and the
    D026 roster test's melee part ([DEVASTATING WOUNDS] stand-in); `R24_11_ExtraAttacks.WhileItsOtherWeaponIsRefused...`,
    `D071_UnknownAbilities.AModelWhoseOnlyMeleeWeaponIsRefused...`, ai `M60_Odds.TheMeleeVolleyAddsTheExtraAttacksWeapon`,
    `M60_Bots.EveryLevelDeclaresTheExtraAttacksWeaponWithItsMainWeapon` and client
    `M60_DefaultPlans.TheHiveTyrantFightsWithItsTalonsWhileItsBoneswordIsRefused` (a refused stand-in bonesword); ai
    `M40_ExpectedDamage.RejectedProfilesAreExcluded` (the spinefist is supported) and the M40 grid (23 variants, the
    spinefist added to the ctest picks); `M24_RulesHelp.EveryWeaponAbilityHasPlainHelp` (24.38 in the game); the
    three serialization size guards.
  - ctest (Debug): 869 → 914 tests, all pass.
- **Acceptance:**
  - *Tests and a replay:* **met** (above).
  - *Old play and replays unchanged:* **met.** `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the four ai regenerations
    leave every existing replay byte-identical; only `m61_twin_linked_reroll.json` is new. The Release arena's
    `--per-game` output matches `baseline/m60` on every `game` line (actions, state and trace hashes) for all 24
    pairings of V1, both bot scenarios and Strike. No weapon equipped there is Twin-linked (a test checks), and
    profiles without the ability keep the old dice order and odds arithmetic.
  - *Screenshots* (`xvfb-run`, scratchpad `m61/`): the re-roll pop-up with the failed die outlined and another
    picked, the action bar's buttons, the "Wound re-rolls" card after `--steps 1`, and the replay viewer's
    "Waiting for" line citing 06.02 at the M60 hazard decision and 24.38 at the M61 decision.
- **Open:**

    re-roll decision uses a short headline.

## M62: Random characteristics ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** design first, then pre-approved · **Rules:** 04.03, 05.04 · **Decision:** D-069 (replaces D-002, amends D-013)

> **Random A** is rolled per selection when its batch resolves (event `AttackDiceRolled`). Blast is added
> after. Batches lost under D-012 never roll.
>
> **Random D** is rolled from the dice after the model is chosen.
> - The casualty-decision check and the preview use a stand-in that never consumes dice.
> - The preview marks later steps as provisional.
> - Fixed values never roll, so the dice stream of every old weapon is unchanged.
>
> **Bot odds** mix exactly over the A and D distributions.

**Acceptance**
- Tests `D069_`, `R04_03_` and `R05_04_`, including one proving that previews never move the dice position.
- Client dice cards and a replay.
- Old play and replays unchanged.

**Results (2026-11-08):** design in D-069 (replaces D-002, amends D-013 and D-051), reviewed before any code. At review,
random-A melee weapons were made refused rather than declared with a sentinel `attacks` 0 (none in the data).
- **Engine:**
  - *Rolling (01.05):* `rules/random_characteristics.{hpp,cpp}` (new). `roll_characteristic` rolls one D6 per die, a
    D3 halved, plus the modifier. `damage_stand_in` is the maximum (`DiceExpr::max_value`). `roll_random_attacks`
    rolls a batch's random A.
  - *Random A (04.03):*
    - `selection_dice` replaces the fixed-only arithmetic. `gather_attack_batches` keeps each random-A selection
      unrolled in `AttackBatch::random_attacks` (`{selection, model, A, bonus}`, the [BLAST] / [RAPID FIRE] dice).
    - `start_batch` rolls them when the batch is gathered for resolution, after it is selected and before its hit
      rolls: event `AttackDiceRolled` (04.03), then `AttackBatchSelected` with the full count.
    - A batch lost to D-012 is never rolled; `AttacksLost` lists its unrolled A. `gather_attack_dice` keeps its
      fixed-A precondition.
  - *Random D (05.04):* `inflict_damage` takes a `DamageRoller`. The engine rolls from its dice when `OrderCasualties`
    is applied, for each attack that inflicts damage, after the model is selected. `SaveStep::damage_roll` records the
    roll; the excess is lost; one order per batch.
  - *The stand-in:* `casualty_decision_needed` and `preview_casualties` use `damage_stand_in` and roll nothing.
    `CasualtyPreview` gains `random_damage` (the numbers are "up to") and `provisional_from` / `next_provisional()`.
  - *Refusals:* the D-002 refusals in shooting, fight and the bots are removed. A random A on a melee profile is
    refused through `unimplemented_ability_problem` ("D-069: ... it can't be selected"); `selection_dice` throws on
    one as the safety net.
  - *`resolve_attack_batch`* follows the engine's dice order (random A first, random D last) and reports
    `attack_rolls` / `attack_dice`.
  - *JSON:* `random_attacks` on `AttackBatch` and `AttacksLost`, and `damage_roll` on `SaveStep`, are omitted when
    empty. `AttackDiceRolled` is appended to the event variant.
- **Client:**
  - *Dice cards:* "Attack dice" (04.03; "Bio-plasmic scream: D6+3 -> 4+3 = 7, +2 [BLAST] = 9", one line per model when
    several roll) before the hit card, whose "N attack dice" counts the rolled dice. "Damage rolls" (05.04) after the
    casualties ("1+1 = 2 -> model 6 (-2); 5+1 = 6 -> model 6 (-6)", "(destroyed, 1 lost)").
  - *Log:* text for the three events (a D3 shows its D6 in brackets: "3[5]"); `is_roll_event` includes both.
  - *Expressions:* the start-of-attacks and lost-attacks lines show a random A as its expression ("2 + D6+3"). The
    weapon menus add an Info line per random characteristic and a "Random A (D6+3)" / "Random D (D6+1)" help entry
    citing D-069; the datasheet window shows the same help on hovering A or D.
  - *Casualty picking:* the hint reads "the next D6+1 damage (up to 7)" and "Thin rings are provisional." once the next
    pick is. The box reads "Damage left to allocate: up to N (rolled when you confirm)". A stand-in mark reads "-7?",
    and a provisional mark has a thin ring (`CasualtyMark::stand_in` / `provisional`).
  - *Flag:* `--pick-models <id,...>` picks models in the first casualty decision, as clicks would (for the
    screenshot, like M61's `--pick-dice`).
- **Bots:**
  - `profile_supported` lets random A and D through. `VolleyBatch` carries its random A expressions (`volley_batch()`
    converts an engine batch).
  - `expected_outcome` mixes exactly over A (the fixed dice, then each count up to the maximum, weighted by the
    convolution of the expressions) and over D (each damaging transition branches over the D's values). The
    distributions come from `roll_characteristic` on each face (`OddsCache::value_pmf` / `sum_pmf`). Fixed A and D run
    the same arithmetic as before.
  - `sample_outcome` rolls both with the bot's own dice through `resolve_attack_batch`.
  - Hard's chain tables replicate the random A per model. Normal's unpermuted batch order sorts by mean damage. The
    state hash includes `random_attacks` only while one waits.
  - The bots plan casualties against the preview's maximum (documented in D-069). Easy's default plans now fire the
    Havocs' lascannons (fixtures only).
- **Replay** `tests/replays/m62_random_characteristics.json` (9 steps, seed 10, picked by
  `Replay.DISABLED_SearchM62Seed`; embedded state):
  - The Havocs vs Hive Tyrant fixture with the m60 heavy venom cannon.
  - The four lascannons fire at the Tyrant; two saves fail. Side B orders the casualties once, and the two D6+1 rolls
    (2, then 6 on the wounded Tyrant) leave it on 2 wounds.
  - The Tyrant remains stationary and fires the cannon: D3 rolled (a 1), +1 [BLAST] for the five Havocs.
  - It stops at Side B's first Charge decision. It loads in the replay viewer.
- **Tests** (40 new, all pass):
  - `tests/m62_random_characteristics_test.cpp` (21):
    - `R04_03_GatherAttackDice.*` (6): per model and weapon, Blast after, Rapid Fire after, a fixed and a random A in one
      batch, Torrent, a D-012 loss never rolled, a fixed A never rolled.
    - `R05_04_InflictDamage.*` (5): rolled after the model is selected, the excess lost, wounded first, no roll for saved
      or lost attacks, one order per batch.
    - `D069_RandomCharacteristics.*` (8): the dice notation; the refused random-A melee stand-in; the preview's maximum
      and provisional steps; previews and decision checks never move the dice (scripted dice and the state's
      `DiceState`); damaged models always among the picks (all 216 roll combinations); `resolve_attack_batch` equals
      the engine on 24 seeds of real weapons; which roster weapons are now usable; none equipped in the old scenarios.
    - `D069_Json.*` (2): round trips with defaults omitted, and a state waiting with an unrolled random A.
  - `engine_property_test.cpp`: `D004_LegalActions.RandomPlayRollsTheRostersRandomAttacksAndDamage`.
  - `tests/ai/m62_test.cpp` (10, + a disabled timing diagnostic): `profile_supported`; the distributions; the exact A
    mixture equals the average of fixed counts; 20,000 engine batches each for the scream and the heavy venom cannon
    (random A, Blast) and the lascannon (random D) against the Tyrant, Tyrant Guard, a Screamer-killer and V1
    Intercessors; the samples' own dice; `volley_batch`; the state hash; every level fires the lascannons; clean games
    (the duel, the duel with the cannon at every level, the roster fixture at Easy and Normal).
  - `tests/client/m62_test.cpp` (8): both cards, the log text, the picks' marks and hint, the help, the Havocs' default
    plan, `--pick-models`.
  - *Changed, for the rule reason (random A and D are rolled now; each keeps its check, with a still-refused stand-in
    where it was about a refusal):*
    - `engine_property_test.cpp`: the `resolvable()` helper drops the fixed-A/D condition (a random-A melee weapon
      stays unresolvable). `RandomPlayFromTheRostersFixture` now expects the lascannons and scream to be used, where
      it expected them not to be. `D026_UnimplementedAbilities.RosterWeaponsTheEngineCantResolve...` uses a
      [DEVASTATING WOUNDS] stand-in lascannon (refusal "24.10: ").
    - Client `M60_DefaultPlans.TheHavocChampionFiresItsPlasmaGunOnTheStandardProfile` uses [MELTA 2] stand-in
      lascannons.
    - ai `M40_ExpectedDamage.RejectedProfilesAreExcluded`: the Shardlauncher is supported; the Strangleweb stays
      refused, now for [DEVASTATING WOUNDS]. The M40 grid builds its dice with `selection_dice` (a random A at its
      mean for sizing). The Shardlauncher adds 2 variants (23 -> 25), and a Shardlauncher case joins the ctest picks.
    - `Serialization.EveryEventKindRoundTrips` lists the new event.
    - The m59 generator's round-1 check allows the Havoc lascannons (see below).
    - The `D002_RandomCharacteristics.*` tests are unchanged and pass: a fixed-A precondition, and a random D without
      a roller.
  - ctest (Debug and Release): 914 -> 954 tests, all pass. The full M40 grid (75 cases x 20,000 batches) passes in
    Release.
- **Acceptance:**
  - *Tests `D069_`, `R04_03_` and `R05_04_`, incl. previews never moving the dice:* **met** (above).
  - *Client dice cards and a replay:* **met.** Screenshots in scratchpad `m62/`:
    - `attack_dice_card.png`: the heavy venom cannon, `--load` with the Easy bot.
    - `damage_rolls_card.png`: the lascannons on the Tyrant.
    - `preview_provisional_v1.png`, with crops `preview_provisional_bar.png` and `preview_provisional_marks.png`: V1
      with stand-in D3 fleshborers (on the 60" board the marks are too small at the fitted zoom). Two picks via
      `--pick-models 3,2`, both marked "-2?", the second with a thin ring, and the hint and box.
    - `replay_frame3.png`: the replay viewer.
  - *Old play and replays unchanged:* **met.**
    - `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the four ai regenerations leave every replay byte-identical except
      `m59_rosters_fixture_round1.json` and the new `m62_random_characteristics.json`.
    - m59 changes in its description only (agreed at review): its steps and expectation are identical. Its generator's
      round-1 check now allows the Havoc lascannons, which reach the Tyranids, and the text says the lascannons are in
      range and Side A ends its Shooting phase without firing.
    - No step of m59, m60 or m61 stopped validating.
    - The Release arena's `--per-game` output matches `baseline/m60` on every `game` line for all 24 pairings of V1,
      both bot scenarios and Strike.
  - *Normal timings (Release):*
    - Strike Normal vs Normal, M61 and M62 binaries run alternately on the same machine (three runs each): p95
      5.46-5.53 ms (M61) and 5.50-5.62 ms (M62); max 68.7-70.9 and 69.6-72.0 ms. That is within about 1.5%, inside the
      M61 runs' own spread. (`baseline/m60`'s 5.23 ms was measured at another time; M61 itself measures 5.46-5.53 ms

    - Roster fixture, 40 Normal-vs-Normal games: p95 5.86 ms, max 81.5 ms (M61, without the lascannons and screams:

- **Open:**

## M63: Monsters and vehicles (17.01, 17.03) ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-08) · **Rules:** 17.01, 17.03, 10.06 · **Decision:** D-073

> **17.01.** In normal and Advance moves, MONSTER/VEHICLE models may pass through enemy models that aren't
> MONSTER/VEHICLE. The path check, the movement reach and the client all follow this.
>
> **17.03.** An engaged enemy MONSTER/VEHICLE can be targeted at −1 to hit, except with close-quarters weapons
> fired by a unit engaged with it. [BLAST] is refused against it.
>
> **10.06.** The −1 becomes `+=`, so the D-011 cap applies.

**Acceptance**
- Tests `R17_01_` and `R17_03_`, plus a replay.
- Old play and replays unchanged.

**Results (2026-11-08):** decision D-073 (applies D-011). No design stop (pre-approved).
- **Engine:**
  - *17.01:* `moves_through_non_monster_enemies(type, unit, model)` (`engine/movement`): a Normal or Advance move of a
    MONSTER/VEHICLE model (`is_monster_or_vehicle`, a unit keyword). `PathContext` gains `passable_enemy` (crossed, not
    ended on). The ProposeModelMove check splits the enemy models into blocking (enemy MONSTER/VEHICLE) and passable
    ones when 17.01 applies, otherwise every enemy model blocks as before. `move_reach` leaves out a passable model's
    `blocking` circle; its `forbidden` circles (not on a model, not within Engagement Range for Normal/Advance) stay.
    Friendly MONSTERS stay passable (03.01, agreed). Fall Back, charge, pile-in and consolidation are unchanged.
  - *17.03:* `target_problem` / `target_ok` (`rules/shooting.cpp`) admit an engaged enemy MONSTER/VEHICLE unit in every
    shooting type; an engaged non-MONSTER stays refused (04.02). [BLAST] at an engaged MONSTER/VEHICLE is refused for
    every shooter ("17.03: '...' is a [BLAST] weapon and cannot target unit N, an engaged MONSTER/VEHICLE unit (Rules
    Appendix FAQ)"); 10.06's own [BLAST] refusal is checked first and keeps its message.
  - *The -1 and the 10.06 fix:* `make_weapon_selections` now adds 10.06's -1 (`-=`) and 17.03's -1 (an engaged
    MONSTER/VEHICLE target, except a [CLOSE-QUARTERS] weapon of a unit engaged with it), judged at Select Targets.
    Both make -2 on the selection; `make_hit_rolls` caps the net to -1 (D-011), so the cap is applied at the hit roll.
    `WeaponSelection::hit_modifier_rules` records the rules (`["10.06", "17.03"]` in JSON, omitted when empty).
- **Client:**
  - *Board:* the shaded area, the ring outline and the fix-illegal-move helper follow `move_reach`, so a MONSTER's
    Normal or Advance move is shaded through enemy infantry, and the path through is accepted. Target highlighting and
    menus come from `shot_options`, so they offer the engaged MONSTER.
  - *Text:* the hit card and the weapon menus get "Engaged MONSTER/VEHICLE target: -1 to hit (17.03)" and "Close-
    quarters shooting by a MONSTER/VEHICLE: -1 to hit (10.06)" lines (`hit_modifier_lines`), and "Net -2 to hit, capped
    at -1 (D-011)" when the cap bites; the card's need reads the applied modifier ("(modifier -1, capped from -2,
    D-011)"). Help: the MONSTER and VEHICLE keywords (17.01, 17.03, 10.06, 06.03), "Moving MONSTERS and VEHICLES"
    (17.01) on a MONSTER's Normal / Advance move entries, "Shooting at engaged MONSTERS and VEHICLES" (17.03) on a weapon
    entry at one, close-quarters shooting's help and the movement ring's text.
  - *Fix:* a weapon entry of a mixed loadout (D-071) described the first shot's weapon (the Havoc Champion's plasma gun
    under "Havoc lascannon"); it now describes the chosen weapon at the clicked target.
  - *Flag:* `--click-units <id,...>` clicks units on the board in the first decision, as clicks would, and opens the
    target's weapon menu next to it (for the screenshot); it submits nothing.
- **Bots:** no new decision. The live shooting odds (`shooting_batches`, `evaluate_shots`, `best_shooting`) take the
  -1 from the engine's selections and the cap from core's `make_hit_rolls`. Normal's and Hard's own movement planner
  still avoids every enemy model (legal, stricter); Easy's moves are validated by the engine. The hypothetical volleys
  (`unit_ranged_volley`) apply neither 10.06's nor 17.03's -1, as before for 10.06 (open, below).
- **Replay** `tests/replays/m63_monsters.json` (31 steps, seed 1, picked by `Replay.DISABLED_SearchM63Seed`; embedded
  state from the new monster fixture, `tests/support/monster_fixtures.hpp`: the Chaos Lord, chainsword Legionaries
  and Havocs vs the Hive Tyrant, Tyrant Guard and a Screamer-killer, placed by hand on the 44"x60" board):
  - Side B first: the Tyrant's Normal move 7.7" south straight through the Legionaries' rank, ending 2.04" beyond it;
    its charge back into base contact; the fight (four Legionaries slain).
  - Side A: the Havocs' four lascannons at the engaged Tyrant at -1 (17.03), then the engaged Legionaries' bolt
    pistols at it in close-quarters shooting at 0 (the exception). The Tyrant ends on 4 wounds.
  - It stops at Side A's first Charge decision (round bases only, before M64-M67).
- **Tests** (42 new, all pass):
  - `tests/m63_monsters_test.cpp` (29):
    - `R17_01_MovingMonsters.*` (14): the fixture; Normal and Advance moves through enemy infantry accepted; not
      through an enemy MONSTER or VEHICLE; not in Fall Back (a Desperate Escape still passes every enemy model), charge,
      pile-in or consolidation; not ending on a model; not ending engaged (reverted, 09.05); a non-MONSTER still can't
      pass (and has the shadows); the reach area includes the far side; an enemy VEHICLE still blocks the reach.
    - `R17_03_ShootingAtEngagedMonsters.*` (9): an engaged MONSTER can be targeted; an engaged non-MONSTER can't; -1
      (a 3 fails at BS3+, a 4 hits); none at an unengaged MONSTER; none for [CLOSE-QUARTERS] weapons of a unit engaged
      with it; -1 for the Chaos Lord's plasma pistol (not engaged with it); [BLAST] refused (krak allowed); the cap with
      10.06 (-2 on the selection and batch, the hit roll at -1); both exceptions at once.
    - `R10_06_CloseQuartersShooting.AMonstersPenaltyAddsToTheOthers`.
    - `D073_Monsters.*` (5): friendly MONSTERS passable; `check_model_path` crosses a `passable_enemy` model but
      can't end on it; only Normal / Advance moves of a MONSTER or VEHICLE; no
      MONSTER or VEHICLE in V1, the two bot scenarios or Strike; `hit_modifier_rules` JSON (round trip, omitted when
      empty, bad value refused, a state waiting in the attack flow).
  - `engine_property_test.cpp`: `D004_LegalActions.RandomPlayOnTheMonsterFixture` (16 games, apart and engaged,
    either side first; shots at the engaged Tyrant happen).
  - `tests/client/m63_test.cpp` (8): the shaded area through infantry (and the Tyrant Guard's shadow); the path in a
    session; the target menu with the 17.03 line and help; the hit card's -1; the cap noted; the move menu's 17.01
    help; the keyword, ring and close-quarters help and `hit_modifier_lines`; `--click-units`.
  - `tests/ai/m63_test.cpp` (4, + a disabled Hard test): the odds take the engine's -1 (3/6 hit chance instead of
    4/6, `evaluate_shots` and `best_shooting` agree); the cap in the odds; every level shoots legally at the engaged
    Tyrant; clean Easy / Normal games on the fixture. `M63_Bots.DISABLED_HardGamesOnTheMonsterFixtureAreClean` (Hard
    takes 10-40 s a game there even in Release) passes when run by hand in Release.
  - No existing test changed.
  - ctest (Debug and Release): 954 -> 996 tests, all pass.
- **Acceptance:**
  - *Tests `R17_01_` and `R17_03_`, plus a replay:* **met** (above).
  - *Old play and replays unchanged:* **met.**
    - `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the four ai regenerations leave every replay byte-identical; the only
      new file is `m63_monsters.json`.
    - The Release arena's `--per-game` output matches `baseline/m60` on every `game` line for all 24 pairings of V1,
      both bot scenarios and Strike.
    - Legality on the fixtures (Release arena): the Havocs vs Hive Tyrant duel, 50 games at each of the six pairings,
      and the roster fixture, 30 games each of Easy-Easy, Normal-Normal and Normal-Easy and 4 of Hard-Normal (p95 3.2 s,
      max 25.9 s a decision; Hard for the roster units is M69's): no rejected action, reverted move or fallback. The
      monster fixture: `M63_Bots.*`, Hard included.
  - *Screenshots* (scratchpad `m63/`): `tyrant_reach_through_infantry.png` (the Tyrant's Advance reach shaded beyond
    the Legionaries' rank, `--load`), `target_menu_engaged_tyrant.png` (the Havocs' target menu at the engaged Tyrant,
    with the 17.03 line, `--click-units 5,6`), `minus_one_card.png` (the Easy bot's Chaos Lord firing its plasma pistol
    at the engaged Tyrant: "Need 2+ to hit (modifier -1)", "Engaged MONSTER/VEHICLE target: -1 to hit (17.03)").
- **Open:**
  - The bots' hypothetical volleys (threat and movement scores) don't apply 10.06's or 17.03's -1, and Normal's charge
    planner still assumes an engaged enemy can shoot only the units it is engaged with (10.06), which a MONSTER can.
    For M68 / M69.
  - A MONSTER of Normal or Hard walks round enemy infantry it could pass (their planner keeps every enemy model as an
    obstacle). For M68 / M69.

## M64: Oval geometry kernel ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-08) · **Rules:** 01.04, 03.01 · **Decision:** D-074 (part 1)

> Pure `geometry/` functions, with no change to the state.
>
> **Distances.**
> - Round to round keeps today's code.
> - Round to oval uses a signed point-to-ellipse distance minus r.
> - Oval to oval and swept segments use GJK on support functions.
>
> **Overlap** means the bases intersect after each is shrunk by EPS/2.
>
> **Rotation sweeps.**
> - Exact against the battlefield edge and against round bases.
> - Conservative for oval against oval.
>
> **Line of sight.** Ovals block it, via closed-form chords and boundary samples.

**Acceptance**
- Within 0.01 mm of brute-force sampling over 10,000 random poses.
- Round-to-round results bit-identical. Arena wall time on Strike within 5%.

**Results (2026-11-08):** decision D-074 part 1. No design stop (pre-approved). Nothing in the state, the engine, the
client or the bots changes: they still call the round-only functions, and `base_radius()` still throws for an oval.
- **Kernel** (`src/core/geometry/pose.hpp`, `oval.hpp/.cpp`):
  - *Types and facing:*
    - `BasePose{position, base, facing_deg}`, `PosedModel` (PlacedModel plus `facing_deg`), `BaseExtents`, `RotationCheck`;
    - `normalise_facing_deg` ((-180, 180], never -0, non-finite to 0), `rotation_delta_deg` (the shorter way, a half
      turn +180), `circumscribed_radius` / `inscribed_radius`.
  - *01.04:* `base_distance(BasePose, BasePose)` and `signed_base_distance`.
    - Round to round: the old inline code, called first (bit-identical).
    - Round to oval: Eberly's point-to-ellipse bisection minus r. It is bisected in w = s + 1: bisecting s lost up to
      0.13 mm next to the major axis inside the evolute, which the tests found.
    - Oval to oval: GJK on the support functions when apart. Otherwise a certified bracket refined to 1e-9 mm, between
      an inner polygon of support points and an outer polygon of support lines, plus a curvature bound for two
      ellipses (D-074).
  - *Overlap:* `bases_overlap(BasePose, BasePose)`. Shrunk by EPS/2 each the bases still intersect, i.e. the
    penetration depth is more than EPS. The round case is today's formula (derived in D-074).
  - *D-009:* `within_area`, `wholly_within`, `base_extents`.
  - *03.01:*
    - straight lines: `translation_crosses`, `translation_signed_distance`, `translation_stays_within`;
    - turns: `rotation_against_edge` (exact), and `rotation_against_base` (exact against a round base; against an
      oval, conservative advancement with the (a - b) Hausdorff bound).
  - *`oval_detail`:* polynomial sine, cosine and atan2 (the same results on every platform), Eberly, the convex support
    shapes and `convex_signed_distance` (with a decide-at overload for the boolean queries).
- **Pose-aware overloads for M65.** They are added next to the round-only functions, which are untouched (the diff to
  the existing geometry files only adds lines):
  - `models_engaged`, `units_engaged`, `engaged_models`, `check_coherency`, `PosedCoherencyWithOneMoved`,
    `closest_distance`, `closest_models`, `closest_units`;
  - `has_line_of_sight` (`visibility_oval.cpp`: closed-form chords, 64 boundary samples on an oval, support-line
    tangents);
  - `check_model_path(mover, base, points, facings, PosedPathContext)`: a turn in place at each point, then the
    straight line. `PathViolationKind` gains `TurnCrossesEnemyModel` and `TurnLeavesBattlefield`.
- **Tests** (29 new, all pass; brute force in `tests/support/oval_brute_force.hpp`, which shares no code with the
  kernel):
  - `tests/m64_oval_distance_test.cpp` (14):
    - `R01_04_OvalDistance.*` (4): round-oval and oval-oval over 10,000 random poses each; touching, overlapping,
      nested and far cases; the degenerate oval.
    - `D074_OvalBases.*` (10): round-round bit-identical to a copy of the old formulas (200,000 poses, including the
      touching and EPS thresholds); the posed model functions equal to the round ones; ovals in them; the EPS/2
      overlap rule; the facing convention; normalisation and the turn delta; bounding circles; the round-only
      functions still throw; the trigonometry; determinism and the bracket.
  - `tests/m64_oval_sweep_test.cpp` (10):
    - `R03_01_OvalSweeps.*` (9): translation against dense sampling along the segment, and examples; edge crossing;
      turns against the edge (dense angle sampling, and examples); turns against a round base (the same); oval
      against oval never clear when dense sampling finds contact; oval path violations.
    - `D074_OvalBases.PosedPathCheckOfRoundBasesIsTheOldCheck`.
  - `tests/m64_oval_visibility_test.cpp` (5):
    - `R06_01_OvalLineOfSight.*`: an oval blocker blocks; the line just past its tip (1.05 / 0.95 mm gaps, square on
      and slanted) and between two tips; an oval observer sees around a blocker; round-only inputs give D-017's answer;
      the degenerate oval.
    - Plus the disabled benchmark `D074_Benchmark.DISABLED_OvalKernel`.
  - No existing test changed.
  - ctest (Debug and Release): 996 -> 1025 tests, all pass.
- **Acceptance:**
  - *Within 0.01 mm of brute-force sampling over 10,000 random poses:* **met.**
    - Round to oval: worst error 6e-5 mm, against boundary sampling (float rounding of the result).
    - Oval to oval: worst error 6e-5 mm for the signed distance, against the support-function duality. For the 01.04
      distance against boundary sampling it is 0.0041 mm, which is the sampling's own error where the boundaries cross.
    - Beyond the tests: over 100,000 pairs (10% concentric) the double values are within 1e-9 mm of the duality, the
      bracket at most 1e-9 mm wide, after at most 31 iterations.
    - Translation, edge-turn and round-turn sweeps agree with dense sampling within 0.01 mm. The oval-oval turn is
      never clear when sampling finds contact, and it is blocked only within its EPS/2 margin.
  - *Round-to-round results bit-identical:* **met.**
    - The `D074_` tests compare against copies of the old formulas, the path check and LoS.
    - `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the four ai regenerations leave all 17 replays byte-identical (M64 adds
      no replay: there is no flow to record).
    - The Release arena's `--per-game` output matches `baseline/m60` on every `game` line for all 24 pairings of V1,
      both bot scenarios and Strike.
  - *Arena wall time on Strike within 5%:* **met.** Strike Normal vs Normal, 60 games, the M63 and M64 Release binaries
    run alternately four times each:
    - M63: 8.67, 8.49, 8.72, 8.74 s (mean 8.66);
    - M64: 8.50, 8.90, 8.45, 8.77 s (mean 8.66);
    - p95 5.43-5.55 ms against 5.37-5.61 ms. Nothing calls the new code yet.
  - *Micro-benchmark* (Release, ns a call):
    - round-round `base_distance`: 4.4 for the old inline, 5.2 through `BasePose`;
    - round-oval: 511;
    - oval-oval: 7654 on average, most of it the overlapping poses (`bases_overlap` 424);
    - a capsule or a swept oval against an oval: 421 / 438;
    - turns against a round base 1085, an oval 9583, the edge 111;
    - line of sight past an oval blocker: 1121.
- **Open (for M65):**
  - Facings 180 degrees apart give the same footprint. The kernel turns to the facing it is given, the shorter way,
    so M65 decides whether 0 -> 170 is a 170-degree turn or the same footprint 10 degrees the other way (D-074).
  - The posed overloads take `PosedModel`. If M65 puts the facing in `Position`, as planned, the round-only functions
    can dispatch to the `BasePose` kernel with the round case first. `CoherencyWithOneMoved` (client) and the bots'
    own geometry are M66's.
  - Round-oval distance costs about 0.5 us (Eberly's bisection runs to the last bit). That is fine for the engine's
    checks; a Newton finish would speed it up if M65's `move_reach` or M66's sampling call it in bulk.

## M65: Oval bases in the engine ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `max` · **Plan mode:** design first, then pre-approved · **Rules:** 01.04, 03.01, 03.02, 09.04, 12.03, 12.08 · **Decision:** D-074 (part 2)

> **Facing.** `Position` gets `facing_deg`, defaulting to 0. It is omitted from JSON when 0 and refused on round
> bases.
>
> **Moves.**
> - Each path vertex may carry a facing.
> - A model rotates in place first, then translates.
> - The distance moved is the sum of the centre's moves.
>
> **Checks.** Validation of the turn and the path against enemy bases and the battlefield edge. Coherency,
> engagement, wholly-within, the closer checks and formation placement all handle ovals.
>
> **`MoveReach`** gains `ReachShape` for anything that isn't round to round.

**Acceptance**
- Tests `R01_04_`, `R03_01_` and `D074_`, plus round trips.
- D-004 random play over a fixture with the Tyrannofex, plus a replay.
- Old play and replays unchanged.

**Results (2026-11-08):** decision D-074 part 2, written first and approved with conditions before any code.
- **Departure from the wording above:** the facing is `Model::facing_deg`, not `Position::facing_deg`.
  - `Position` stays a plain 12-byte point: a path vertex, a ring centre, a mouse point, a bot's candidate.
  - Every caller that builds a path from coordinates (the client's drags, the auto moves, the bots) would otherwise
    turn an oval to 0 at each vertex. On the model, a path without facings keeps the facing.
  - The move carries the rest: `ModelStart::facing_deg`, and `facings` on `ProposeModelMove` (one per path vertex, or
    none), `ModelPath` and `ModelMoveProposed`.
  - The cost: `GameAction` grows from 40 to 64 bytes (`ProposeModelMove` gains a vector); the timing below covers it.
- **Engine** (`wh40k_core`):
  - *Facing (D-074 part 2):*
    - an oval's facing is stored modulo 180, in (-90, 90] (`footprint_facing_deg`); a turn between two vertices goes
      the shorter way between the footprints (`footprint_turn_deg`, at most 90 degrees; a quarter turn is +90);
    - a longer turn is several vertices at one point; the kernel gets unwrapped facings, so it sweeps exactly those
      turns;
    - a round base has no facing: a non-zero facing is refused in validation, the state JSON and the scenario loader.
  - *Moves (03.01):*
    - `ProposeModelMove{model, path, facings}`: the model turns in place, then moves; the distance is the centre's
      lines only;
    - a turn without moving is `path {start}` with one facing;
    - the kernel's posed `check_model_path` checks each turn and line against enemy models and the battlefield edge
      ("cannot be turned through enemy model N (before segment k)", "... while it turns"), and the end against every
      model (D-027). Friendly models may be passed; 09.07 Desperate Escape and 17.01 (passable infantry) apply to turns;
    - reverting or resetting a move restores the facing; `move_start_facing_deg` is the new query.
  - *Which moves turn:* every move type. 09.04 Remain Stationary starts no move, so nothing turns. 12.03 / 12.08
    Ongoing models in base contact can't be turned (moving includes rotating), nor M '-' models.
  - *"Closer" with a turn (11.04, 12.03, 12.08):* `after < min(before at the start facing, before at the end facing) -
    EPS`. A turn alone is never closer. This is agreed for 12.03 / 12.08 and applied to 11.04 as the conservative
    extension. Start and end states (engaged, unengaged, still engaged) use the poses as they stand.
  - *Every measurement takes the facing:*
    - engagement, coherency, closest model and unit, wholly within, range and half range, line of sight, charge and
      pile-in targets, base contact, the set-up checks (`pose_of`, `posed_models`, `posed_enemy_models`, `BasePose`
      overloads of the per-model queries);
    - the `Position` overloads keep their meaning, at the model's current facing;
    - the round-only functions and `PlacedModel` views stay, still throwing for an oval, for the client and the bots.
  - *Speed:*
    - the round-round case of `base_distance`, `signed_base_distance` and `bases_overlap` on `BasePose`s is inline in
      `oval.hpp`;
    - the posed path check keeps the old loop for round pairs;
    - the posed line of sight runs D-017's code on its spans with no copy.
    All bit-identical.
  - *`MoveReach`:*
    - `move_reach(state, model, facing_deg)`; a round pair keeps its `ReachCircle`;
    - any pair with an oval gets a `ReachShape{mover_base, mover_facing_deg, obstacle, threshold_mm, engagement, model}`
      in `forbidden_shapes`, `required_any_shapes` (empty, or one group per `required_any` group) or `blocking_shapes`;
    - `reach_shape_contains`, with `reach_shape_inner_radius_mm` / `reach_shape_outer_radius_mm` as bounds;
    - `centre_bounds` by the extents at the facing; `turn_blocked`;
    - a round-only state gives exactly the old `MoveReach`.
  - *Set-up (03.02, D-008, D-064):*
    - formations place each model by its half extents at the formation's facing (bit-identical for round bases);
    - optional `formation.facing_deg`: a round base refuses a non-zero value; a non-number or non-finite value is
      refused;
    - an oval shorter than its width is refused at placement (D-070);
    - coherency, wholly within, no overlap and unengaged are measured with the facings;
    - M59's refusal of ovals goes.
  - *JSON:* `facing_deg` (Model, ModelStart) and `facings` (ModelPath, ProposeModelMove, ModelMoveProposed) only when
    non-zero / non-empty; a replay's `expect` records `facing_deg` only when non-zero. A stored facing outside
    (-90, 90], a round base's facing or a path's facings of the wrong length are `DataError`s.
- **Outside core** (each in D-074 part 2):
  - `src/ai/state_hash.cpp`: a facing is hashed only when it isn't 0 (models, move starts, a path's facings), so
    round-only states hash as before.
  - `auto_fight_move` / `auto_charge_move`: a temporary guard (`TODO(M66): remove when auto moves handle ovals`).
    With any oval base on the battlefield they return no arrangement ("D-074: Auto move does not handle oval bases
    yet") instead of throwing.
  - `overlay_plan.cpp` `ring_circles`: the replay viewer threw from `base_radius` on the new replay (a moving
    Tyrannofex's rings). Converted, not guarded: the ring radius is the base's circumscribed radius (the radius for a
    round base, unchanged), exact for the movement and charge rings. Round an oval's engagement, coherency and weapon
    rings it is the circle through its ends, at most 14 mm too wide across its width (`TODO(M66)`).
- **Fixture:**
  - `tests/data/scenarios/rosters_tyrannofex_44x60.json`: the M59 roster fixture plus the Tyrannofex (unit 14, the
    roster loadout, behind the west Screamer-killer at x = 23", `depth_in` 4, facing 0);
  - `load_tyrannofex_fixture()` / `_closer(in)`;
  - `tests/support/monster_fixtures.hpp` `tyrannofex::fixture(first_turn, engaged)`: the Legionaries, Havocs, Tyrant
    Guard, a Screamer-killer and the Tyrannofex placed by hand. It is separate from the round-only M63 fixture, which
    the client and ai tests share.
- **Replay** `tests/replays/m65_oval_tyrannofex.json` (24 steps, seed 1, picked by `Replay.DISABLED_SearchM65Seed`;
  embedded state):
  - Side B first: the Tyrannofex turns 45 degrees and moves 3.00" south. The turn doesn't count, so no model moved
    more than 3" and its [HEAVY] fleshborer hive gets +1.
  - It fires both guns at the chainsword Legionaries.
  - It charges them, turned back to 0 and engaged, fights first, and the Legionaries fight back.
  - It stops at Side A's first Move Units decision.
  - The client's viewer plays all 24 frames under `xvfb-run` (screenshots in scratchpad `m65/`).
- **Tests** (50 new, all pass):
  - `tests/m65_oval_engine_test.cpp` (38): `R01_04_OvalEngine` (3), `R03_03_OvalCoherency` (1), `R06_01_OvalEngine`
    (1), `R03_01_OvalMoves` (9), `R09_04_RemainStationary` (1), `R11_04_OvalCharge` (4), `R12_03_OvalPileIn` (3),
    `R12_08_OvalConsolidation` (1), `D074_Facings` (5), `D074_MoveReach` (6), `D074_Json` (4).
  - `scenario_test.cpp` (5): `D074_Formation.*` (3), `D070_Formation.AnOvalShorterThanItsWidthIsRefused`,
    `R03_02_SetUp.TheTyrannofexFixtureIsALegalSetUp`.
  - `engine_property_test.cpp`: `D004_LegalActions.RandomPlayOnTheTyrannofexFixture`. 14 games (the roster fixture
    36", 10" and 0.5" apart, the hand-placed layout apart and engaged, either side first) end and round-trip. The
    random fills give the oval turns and corrupt facings: 42 applied proposals turned it, 19 without moving; it shot
    23 times and fought 26 times. Nothing threw.
  - `replay_test.cpp`: `Replay.RandomGamesFromTheTyrannofexFixtureReplayIdentically`, plus the M65 regeneration and
    `DISABLED_SearchM65Seed`.
  - `tests/client/m65_test.cpp` (3): the viewer's timeline and rings over the new replay; round rings unchanged; the
    auto-move guard.
  - `tests/ai/m65_test.cpp` (2): `M65_StateHash.RoundOnlyStatesHashAsBefore` (against a verbatim copy of the M64 hash,
    at every decision of three random games each on V1, Strike and the roster fixture, up to 400 decisions a game,
    moves in progress included), `ATurnChangesTheHash`.
  - The fill helper (`engine_helpers.hpp`) draws extra numbers only for oval models, so every older random game is
    unchanged. `neutral_action` / `play_neutral_until` are new shared helpers.
- **Existing tests changed** (for the stated rule reason: M65 removes the limitation they asserted):
  - `D008_Formation.ANonRoundBaseIsRejectedAsUnsupportedData` is now `...IsPlacedByItsExtents`;
    `D008_Formation.TheTyrannofexOvalBaseIsStillRefusedAtPlacement` is now `...IsPlacedByItsExtents`.
  - The refusal path they tested ("V1 supports round bases only") no longer exists. Every remaining loader refusal has
    a test with its message: a round base's facing, a non-number facing, a non-finite facing, a malformed oval (also
    `R02_02_Profiles.OvalBaseShorterThanItsWidthIsDataError`).
  - No other test changed.
- **ctest:** 1025 -> 1075 tests, all pass (Debug and Release).
- **Acceptance:**
  - *Tests `R01_04_`, `R03_01_`, `D074_` and round trips:* **met** (above).
  - *D-004 random play over a fixture with the Tyrannofex, plus a replay:* **met**.
  - *Old play and replays unchanged:* **met.**
    - `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the four ai regenerations leave all 17 replays byte-identical; the only
      new file is `m65_oval_tyrannofex.json`.
    - The Release arena's `--per-game` output matches `baseline/m60` on every `game` line (actions, final state and
      trace hashes) for all 24 pairings of V1, both bot scenarios and Strike: 4,380 games, with the final binary and
      with the one before its last two edits (a struct member and a rename, no behaviour change).
  - *Speed (arena wall time on Strike within 5% of M64):* **met.** Strike, Normal vs Normal, 60 games, Release, the
    M64 binary (`verify_arena_m64`) and M65 run alternately:
    - the near-final build: M64 8.86, 8.76, 8.99, 8.54 s (mean 8.79); M65 9.13, 8.93, 9.02, 8.60 s (mean 8.92, +1.5%);
    - the final build: M64 9.12, 8.82, 8.61, 8.82 s (mean 8.84); M65 9.10, 8.78, 9.07, 9.15 s (mean 9.02, +2.0%);
    - over all eight runs each +1.8%. The decision p95 is 5.51-5.60 ms for M64 and 5.62-5.94 ms for M65.
    - callgrind on a 3-game run: M65 executes 0.3% fewer instructions than M64 (4.487e9 against 4.501e9; the line of
      sight no longer copies its blockers). The planned fallback (the old round-only code for an all-round state) was
      not needed.
- **Left for M66** (client logic and bots that throw or misbehave with an oval on the battlefield; file:line at this
  commit):
  - *Throw (round-only geometry, `base_radius`):*
    - client logic:
      - `auto_fight_move.cpp` 65, 74, 115, 138, 150, 176, 207, 217, 273 and `auto_charge_move.cpp` 86, 92, 121, 123,
        170, 187, 218, 226, 386 (guarded in M65 at 311 and 577: remove the guards);
      - `fight_move_skip.cpp` 65, 71, 91 (the client's step skipping and every bot level);
      - `casualty_order.cpp` 32, 134, 137, 222, 227, 232, 269 (the default casualty order, `casualty_picks.cpp` 19,
        37, 45, Hard's candidate, Normal's `coherency_losses`).
    - bots:
      - `normal/movement.cpp` 142, 145, 157, 164, 166, 177, 181, 184, 188, 189, 261, 262, 289, 298, 300, 333, 725,
        736;
      - `normal/move_score.cpp` 193, 236, 267, 377, 413, 440, 694, 711;
      - `normal/defense.cpp` 223, 252, 310, 358, 363;
      - `hard/lookahead.cpp` 123, 126, 145, 151, 154, 158;
      - `hard/evaluator.cpp` 91;
      - `hard/hard_bot.cpp` 130, 140, 718;
      - `analysis/charge_odds.cpp` 142, 144, 145.
    - Seen: the client on the Tyrannofex fixture (`--scenario`, `--bot both`) throws at the first casualty decision,
      from `default_casualty_order` (`casualty_order.cpp` 137).
  - *Misbehave (no throw):*
    - `reach_area.cpp` 173-177 and 189-205, `drop_fix.cpp` 52-89 and 225-245, `ring_outline.cpp` 47-80: they read
      `ReachCircle`s only and ignore the shapes, `turn_blocked` and the facing.
    - `move_plan.cpp` 11-46: it drops `ModelPath::facings`, so extending a turned model's path undoes the turn.
    - `overlay_plan.cpp` 107-113: the circumscribed ring is an outer bound round an oval's engagement, coherency and
      weapon rings.
    - `event_text.cpp` / `action_text.cpp`: no text for a turn.
    - `board_view.cpp` 48, 88, 150 and `main.cpp` 1037-1326: ovals drawn and hit-tested as circles of their width; no
      rotation input.
- **Open:** the oval-against-oval turn check is conservative (part 1); the rosters have one oval, so it never applies
  in their games.

## M66: Oval bases in the client and bots; big-base polish ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-08) · **Decision:** D-074 (part 3), D-075

> **Client.**
> - Ovals are drawn and hit-tested.
> - You rotate a model while dragging it (Q/E and Shift+wheel).
> - The reach shading, the "fix illegal move" helper, the ring outlines and the auto charge and fight moves
>   handle ovals.
>
> **Bots** keep an oval's facing fixed and use bounding circles to choose moves, then check each move with the
> engine.
>
> **Big bases.**
> - A drag keeps the grab offset.
> - Wounds show past W 6.
> - Contact sampling is finer.
> - The bots can park large models.

**Acceptance**
- Client `M66_` tests.
- Screenshots of the Tyrannofex at several facings, mid-rotation and with its reach area.
- Old play and replays unchanged.

**Results (2026-11-08):** decisions D-074 part 3 (client and bots) and D-075 (big-base polish). No design stop
(pre-approved). Nothing in `wh40k_core` changes: the client logic and the bots move from the round-only functions to
core's pose-aware ones (D-074 part 2), and every move they build is still checked by the engine.
- **Client logic** (`wh40k_client_logic`):
  - *New `base_shape.hpp/.cpp`:* the outline of a base at its facing and the ring a distance out from its edge (the
    ellipse's offset curve), `base_contains` / `offset_contains` (hit-testing), `turn_swept_outline` (the turn's swept
    area, exact per direction), `contact_ring` / `ring_spot` / `distance_until_gap` (contact round an oval: closed form
    for a round–oval pair, bisection for two ovals), D-075's `contact_direction_count` and `direction_table`, and
    `wound_display`.
  - *Shaded area (`reach_area`):* `ReachRegion` carries core's `ReachShape`s (`inside_any_shapes`, `outside_shapes`,
    `outside_shape_shadows`); `in_region` tests them exactly (core's `reach_shape_contains`, the engine's line test for
    a shape's shadow); `reach_spans` bisects each shape's (convex) row interval to 0.5 mm; `unit_region`,
    `trimmed_region` and `RayRefusals` handle shapes; `turn_blocked` empties the area.
  - *Fix illegal moves (`drop_fix`):* engagement shapes become "not on it", shapes become chains of edge points for the
    nearest-spot search (0.05 mm on the legal side), shadows get tangent lines, the drag line is cut at the chains.
  - *Rings:* `Circle::oval` (`OvalRing`), `ring_contains`, `outline_paths`; `outline_arcs` hides a circle's rim inside
    a same-kind oval ring; `ring_circles` gives an oval its offset ring for Engagement Range, coherency and weapon
    range, at its facing or the dragged one (`FacingOverrides`).
  - *Moves (`move_plan`):* `extend_path` keeps `ModelPath::facings`; `model_path_facings`, `extend_path_turned`,
    `turned_drop` (turn then move, else move then turn by core's validation, else turn then move), `describe_path`,
    `Ruler::turned_deg`, D-075's `drag_drop`.
  - *Auto moves:* M65's guard removed; `auto_charge_move` / `auto_fight_move` keep an oval's facing, measure every pair
    with the pose-aware distance, sample contact round big bases more finely (D-075), share each target's contact
    spots across a unit's models, and keep the old code for round-only problems.
  - *`fight_move_skip`, `casualty_order` (and so `casualty_picks`, Normal's and Hard's casualty orders):* posed;
    `coherency_losses` gains a `PosedModel` overload.
  - *Text:* the log ("model 73 turns 45° then moves 3.00\"") and the action label ("Move model 73 (turns 45° then
    moves 3.00\")"); paths without facings keep the old text.
  - *Flags:* `--turn <model>:<deg>[:<dx>,<dy>]` (`CliTurn`, a scripted turned drag for screenshots).
- **Client** (`src/client_2d`): ovals drawn at their facing (`fill_base`, `draw_base_outline`, `draw_base_cross`:
  models, start ghosts, the drag ghost, selection and target outlines, the D-042 / D-051 crosses and rings);
  `model_at` hit-tests the ellipse; Q / E turn a dragged oval 15°, Shift+wheel 5° a notch; a drag without moving
  turns in place; the turn preview (the swept area where the turn is made, the start footprint, the ghost at the new
  facing); the ruler reads "turned 60° then 3.61\" / 9.00\""; the shaded area and the fix region follow the dragged
  facing (the spans of an area with shapes are cached until the move, model, facing, zoom or grab changes); oval
  rings drawn as paths; the drag keeps its grab offset; wounds past W 6 as a bar and "11/16"; the tooltip names the
  oval and its facing; the help line and the move hint give the turn keys. In a `--screenshot` run `--zoom` is applied
  every frame (a late window resize used to undo it).
- **Bots** (`src/ai`): no level turns an oval (proposals without facings). New `bounds.hpp` (circumscribed circles
  where a site avoids something, exact pose distances where it must reach something, exact half extents at the fixed
  facing for the battlefield edge; each the old formula for a round base). Converted: Normal's `movement.cpp`
  (geometry, `segment_clear`, `ends_ok`, slots, the parking step, formations, the furthest standing move; precomputed
  radii and extents; the round-only coherency check for an all-round unit), `move_score` (posed enemies and other
  units, facings from the move's start, extents for the room to back away, round fast paths), `defense` (posed
  coherency losses), Hard's `lookahead` (`translation_ok`), `evaluator` (`room_away`), `hard_bot` (posed coherency),
  `charge_odds` (posed charge reach). `normal::slot_moves` exposes the slot proposals for the parking test.
- **Tests** (all pass):
  - `tests/client/m66_test.cpp` (20): `M66_BaseShape` (3: hit-testing at facings 0/30/45/90/-60 and the round test
    unchanged; the outline on the ellipse and the ring its offset curve, not a scaled ellipse; the turn's swept area

## M67: The portrait scenario and the points tiebreak ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-08) · **Rules:** 07.03 · **Decision:** D-076 (amends D-007), D-065 extended

> **Scenario.** `data/scenarios/rosters_44x60.json`, "Incursion - Chaos Space Marines vs Tyranids (44x60
> portrait)", with each unit's roster loadout and points.
>
> **Tiebreak.** The scenario option `result: "points_destroyed"` decides the game as agreed. The result is
> shown in the end-of-game panel and the log.
>
> **Colours.** At least 10 unit shades per side. The single-unit and Strike looks are unchanged.

**Acceptance**
- D-004 random play on the new scenario.
- Tests `R07_03_`, `D076_` and client `M67_`.
- Easy vs Easy arena games all finish clean.
- Replay `m67_rosters_round1.json`, plus screenshots.

**Results (2026-11-08):** decision D-076 (amends D-007; the points tiebreak and the scenario) and D-065 extended (ten
shades). No design stop (pre-approved).
- **Scenario** `data/scenarios/rosters_44x60.json` ("Incursion - Chaos Space Marines vs Tyranids (44x60 portrait)"):
  - 44" wide by 60" deep, 12" zones on the short south (A) and north (B) edges, 36" apart, 5 battle rounds, A first,
    `"result": "points_destroyed"`.
  - Every unit of both rosters, model by model with the roster loadout (as the M59 fixture), and its roster points:
    CSM 960, Tyranids 955 (a duplicated roster unit is printed once with a count, at one unit's points).
  - Numbered west to east (the file's order): 1 Legionaries (chainswords), 2 Chaos Lord, 3 Chaos Terminator Squad,
    4 Havocs (held back 3"), 5 Legionaries (boltguns); 6 Termagants, 7 Genestealers, 8 Screamer-killer, 9 Tyrant Guard
    (held back 4"), 10 Hive Tyrant, 11 Tyrannofex (held back 4", facing 0), 12 Screamer-killer, 13 Genestealers,
    14 Termagants. Coherent, wholly within the zones, unengaged, no overlap; described in `notes`.
  - It is in the start screen's list and the package's `data/` (package.ps1 copies `data/`); `docs/datasheets/` is
    not copied anywhere.
- **Engine** (`wh40k_core`):
  - *State:* `GameState::result_rule` (`ResultRule::D007Draw` / `PointsDestroyed`), `Unit::points` and
    `Unit::starting_wounds` (the sum of W of the models as set up, recorded by the loader with the points).
  - *New `core/state/points_destroyed.{hpp,cpp}`:* `points_destroyed(state)` (a `PointsTally`: both totals as
    numerators over one denominator, the lcm of the starting wounds, so they compare as integers; display strings;
    the round), `check_points_data` (the bounds: points 1..100000, both-or-neither, every unit with the option, lcm x
    total points <= 2^59, proven overflow-free in D-076), `points_decimal` / `points_display` (2 places, more while two
    unequal totals read alike; at most 18 needed). It lives under `core/state/` with the other read-only queries, so
    the client logic may call it (M12's architecture test allows only listed `core/rules/` headers).
  - *Game end (`advance.cpp`):* after the End of Battle Round step of the last round, with the option, the side with the
    larger numerator wins, equal is a draw; the reason gives both totals ("D-076: battle round 5 has ended with both
    sides on the battlefield; points destroyed: Side A 113.5, Side B 19; Side A wins on points destroyed"). D-007's
    wipe-out check is untouched and runs first.
  - *Result and event:* `GameResult::points` and `GameOver::points` hold the tally when the game was decided on points;
    such a `GameOver` cites D-076 (`event_rule`).
  - *Loader:* `result` (any other value refused) and per-unit `points` (missing with the option, non-integer,
    < 1 or > 100000 refused as `DataError` with the unit named).
  - *JSON:* `result_rule`, `points`, `starting_wounds`, `result.points` and `GameOver.points` omitted at their defaults;
    the state JSON runs the same checks.
- **Bots** (`wh40k_ai`): no level knows the tiebreak yet (M68, M69). `state_hash` hashes the points, starting wounds
  and rule only when set. The arena reads the result's winner as before, so points wins and draws are counted as wins
  and draws; `GameRecord::on_points` and, for such a scenario only, a summary line "decided on points destroyed after
  the last battle round (D-076): A wins n, B wins n, draws n". `--per-game` lines are unchanged.
- **Client:** the victory pop-up shows the title, "Points destroyed: Side A (Chaos Space Marines) 95.5, Side B
  (Tyranids) 75" and the reason (after a wipe-out in such a scenario, the totals as it ended); the Game panel shows
  "Points destroyed: Side A 123.5, Side B 76.33" above the units while the scenario uses the tiebreak (the rule in its
  tooltip); the log reads "[D-076] Battle round 5 ends: Side A wins on points destroyed (95.5 to 75)". Nothing changes
  without the option. No new decision, action or flag (so no dice card, undo group or fallback answer is needed).
- **Shades (D-065 extended):** `kUnitShades` 6 -> 10. Shades 1-6 unchanged. New A: indigo, pale cyan, lavender,
  light violet; B: cream, dusty rose, brown, amber. Chosen by a search (each in turn the furthest from those before it
  under simulated colour blindness, within every M55 bound and a hue family) and checked with the Machado 2009
  simulations of protanopia, deuteranopia and tritanopia: every pair with a new shade >= 11.5 (CIE76), A against B
  >= 21.1; the M55 pairs unchanged (lowest 7.1 / 7.3). Labels: white outlined in black, so >= 4.62 WCAG contrast on
  every shade; >= 3.57 (large text) on the greyed look.
- **Tests** (27 new, all pass):
  - `tests/m67_points_tiebreak_test.cpp` (15): `R02_07_WargearOptions.TheRostersScenarioCarriesEveryRosterUnitWithItsLoadoutAndPoints`,
    `R03_02_SetUp.TheRostersScenarioIsALegalSetUpNumberedWestToEast`, `D076_Loader` (2: options read; the refusals),
    `R07_03_PointsTiebreak` (2: a win after round 5, a loss), `D076_PointsTiebreak` (2: an exact tie that doubles
    would give to Side A (0.1 + 0.2 against 0.3) is a draw; a near-tie that doubles can't tell apart, 6 / (154781107 x
    137511488) apart, is won exactly), `D007_GameEnd.AWipeOutStillWinsAtOnceUnderThePointsTiebreak` (in round 1, with
    Side B ahead on points), `D007_GameEnd.AScenarioWithoutTheOptionStillDrawsWhateverWasDestroyed`,
    `D076_PointsDestroyed` (2: destroyed units count in full, damaged their fraction; the display), `D076_Json` (3:
    round trips of the state, the result and the event; an old file loads and saves the same, and every saved replay
    re-saves byte for byte; malformed points in a state refused).
  - `engine_property_test.cpp`: `D004_LegalActions.RandomPlayOnTheRostersScenario` (the full D-004 checks on 3 games,
    36", 10" and 0.5" apart, and 9 more games of plain random play, A first: every game ends and round-trips; 12 were
    decided on points, each with the totals of its final state and the right winner; wipe-outs would be checked as
    D-007's).
  - `replay_test.cpp`: the M67 regeneration and `Replay.DISABLED_SearchM67Seed`.
  - `tests/client/m67_test.cpp` (8): `M67_EndPanel` (2), `M67_Log`, `M67_GamePanel`, `M67_Scenarios`,
    `M67_UnitShades` (3: ten per side with the first six and the Strike tints unchanged and the roster's nine Tyranid
    units on nine shades; distinct under simulated colour blindness; labels legible).
  - `tests/ai/m67_test.cpp` (3): `M67_StateHash.StatesWithoutPointsHashAsBefore` (against a verbatim copy of the M66
    hash, every decision of random games on V1, Strike and the Tyrannofex fixture, and the roster scenario with its

  - Test helpers `load_rosters_scenario()` / `load_rosters_scenario_closer(in)`.
  - **No existing test changed.** No existing test pins the exact scenario list (M38's and M53's check that their
    files are in it), so the new file needed no change; `M67_Scenarios` checks it is listed. M55's shade tests now run
    over ten shades per side and pass unchanged.
  - ctest: 1101 -> 1128 tests, all pass in Debug and in Release.
- **Replay** `tests/replays/m67_rosters_round1.json` (scenario-file, seed 1, 90 steps): round 1, the chainsword
  Legionaries move 6", the Havocs' four lascannons hit the west Termagants (2 slain), the west Genestealers move 8";
  round 2, the Legionaries move 6" again, the Genestealers move 8", charge, fight first (a Legionary slain) and the
  Legionaries fight back (2 Genestealers slain). It stops at Side A's first Move Units decision of round 3 (points

  with 36" between the zones and M 8" at most no charge is possible in round 1, so the replay covers rounds 1 and 2.
  The file name is kept as briefed.
- **Acceptance:**
  - *D-004 random play on the new scenario:* **met** (above: 12 games, every one ends and round-trips).
  - *Tests `R07_03_`, `D076_` and client `M67_`:* **met** (above).
  - *Easy vs Easy arena games all finish clean:* **met.** Release arena, the new scenario, seeds 1-200: 200 finished,
    rejected 0, UnitMoveReverted 0, fallbacks 0, unfinished 0; A 77, B 123, draws 0, every game decided on points
    after round 5 (no wipe-out); decision p50 0.008 ms, p95 0.43 ms, max 25.8 ms; 16 s, peak RSS 9.6 MB.
  - *For information (M68 and M69 own them):*
    - Normal vs Normal, seeds 1-60: all finished, rejected 0, reverted 0, fallbacks 0; A 46, B 14, all on points;

    - Hard vs Normal, seed 1, one game in each seat (the two at once, part of the time next to the Release ctest):

  - *Replay `m67_rosters_round1.json`:* **met** (above).
  - *Screenshots* (scratchpad `m67/`): the scenario at deployment with all fourteen shades and badges

## M68: Easy and Normal for the new units ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-08) · **Decision:** D-077 (amends D-066)

> **Odds.** The bots' odds learn:
> - per-model loadouts;
> - Extra Attacks;
> - Sustained Hits and Twin-linked;
> - random A and D;
> - the cost of Hazardous.
>
> **Value.** Material is weighted by points, but only for sides with more than one unit type, so Strike is
> unchanged. The objective knows the points tiebreak.

**Acceptance** (Release arena, new scenario, 200 seeds, both seats)
- Normal beats Easy in at least 75% of games.
- Zero rejections, reverts and fallbacks.
- Normal p95 ≤ 100 ms and max ≤ 1 s.
- The old scenarios play identically (hashes) and replays are unchanged.
- Tests `M68_`.

**Results (2026-11-08):** decision D-077 (amends D-066). No design stop (pre-approved). Bot only (`src/ai`): no rule,
core, state, action, event or JSON change, no new decision and no client change. Hard's own code is untouched.
- **Odds** (`analysis/odds.*`):
  - *Per-model loadouts:* checked; every estimate of Easy and Normal already used each model's own weapons (the
    hypothetical volleys, `best_shooting` / `best_melee`, Normal's `plan_shooting` / `plan_melee`, the move score, the
    casualty worth, Easy's plans), so a unit's volley is the sum of its loadout groups. The one representative site fixed:
    Desperate Escape's expected losses use each model's W through core's 06.02 when they differ (no roster unit's do).
  - *[EXTRA ATTACKS]:* checked in every melee estimate (each goes through `unit_melee_volley` or `plan_melee`); nothing to
    change.
  - *[HAZARDOUS]:* `hazard_model_equivalents` (core's `make_hazard_rolls` per face, a failure on 1-2, and
    `inflict_mortal_wounds`, 06.02). Normal's shooting and melee declarations subtract the firing unit's expected loss (the
    engine's count of hazard rolls) in its own share; the hypothetical picks price it at an exchange rate
    (`ShotContext::hazard_rate`, `best_shooting(..., hazard_rate)`). The Havoc champion supercharges only when it is worth
    it (at the Tyrant Guard: standard at 18", supercharge at 8"); Easy is unchanged (its plans never pick [HAZARDOUS]).
  - *The -1s in hypothetical volleys:* `ShotContext` gains `target_engaged` and `elsewhere`; `unit_ranged_volley` judges
    targets as the engine's `target_ok` does (04.02, 10.06, 17.03, [BLAST]) and adds 10.06's and 17.03's -1s as
    `make_weapon_selections` does; the odds cap them through `make_hit_rolls` (D-011).
- **Normal** (`normal/*`):
  - *Engaged MONSTERS:* the charge planner's hold value lets an engaged enemy MONSTER/VEHICLE shoot us (close-quarters
    shooting by its MONSTER models, at -1; 10.06) and shoots it at -1 (17.03); a MONSTER of ours stays a target while
    engaged (17.03); the same in the consolidation and move scores (`next_turn_shot`).
  - *17.01:* the movement planner lets a MONSTER's Normal or Advance move pass enemy infantry (`segment_clear`); its ends
    stay off every model and out of Engagement Range, and every plan is still simulated on the engine.
  - *Points-weighted material:* `share_weight` (the unit's points over the mean of every unit, when every unit has points
    and its side has more than one unit type; else 1), applied in `unit_share`, so every exchange is in points.
  - *The tiebreak objective:* under `points_destroyed` the move score has no time preference, keeps the survival term only
    for a side's last unit, scaled by the engine's exact tally ((1 + R) times D-007's), and every side counts the other
    units' replies by marginal exposure (no side plays for a draw).
  - *Desperate Escape only when it stands:* the arena's 200 Normal vs Normal games found one reset (seed 193: battle-shocked
    Chaos Terminators, the Hive Tyrant among them against the board edge, no arrangement left after the hazard losses).
    Normal now checks one to three failed rolls first, as Hard does (`escape_stands`).
  - *Left as they are (reasons and measures in D-077):* random damage planned at the preview's maximum; oval facings never
    turned.
- **Arena:** a points scenario's report adds the mean points destroyed by each side; every report adds each side's
  decision times (Normal's own against Easy). `--per-game` lines unchanged.
- **Tests** (`tests/ai/m68_test.cpp`, 13 + 2 disabled, all pass; no existing test changed):
  - `M68_Loadouts.AUnitsVolleyIsTheSumOfItsLoadoutGroupsAsTheEngineGathersIt`: the Havocs, the boltgun Legionaries and the
    Chaos Lord at the Tyrant Guard, and the Legionaries in melee: the engine's own batches, and within 2% of 20,000 engine
    batches.
  - `M68_ExtraAttacks.EveryMeleeEstimateCountsTheTyrantsTalons`.
  - `M68_Hazard.TheCostMatchesTwentyThousandEngineHazardRolls` (the Havocs at one and two rolls, the Chaos Lord, the Hive
    Tyrant: exact, and within 2% of 20,000 engine rolls), `M68_Hazard.NormalSuperchargesOnlyWhenItIsWorthIt` (Easy never).
  - `M68_Modifiers.HypotheticalVolleysApplyTenOhSixAndSeventeenOhThreeAsTheEngine`.
  - `M68_ChargePlanner.AnEngagedMonsterStillShootsOtherUnits`.
  - `M68_Movement.AMonstersNormalMoveThroughInfantryIsPlannedAndTheEngineAcceptsIt`,

  games): Chaos Space Marines 46 wins, Tyranids 14, 22 pile-ins and consolidations, all the Chaos Space Marines'. Every
  Tyranid unit lost its exchange with its closest Chaos Space Marine unit in unit shares (a 75-point Genestealer unit loses
  about half of itself to a 180-point boltgun squad's shooting in a turn and takes under a fifth of it back), so each kept
  away as a losing side (D-066), and D-007's survival and time-preference terms paid for hiding to reach a draw the
  tiebreak no longer gives. Now (200 games): Tyranids 140, Chaos Space Marines 60, 462 pile-ins and consolidations (433 and
  29); the Tyranid MONSTERS close in and fight (34 charge moves in 20 traced games: the Hive Tyrant and the
  Screamer-killers). The infantry still holds back from the Chaos Space Marine infantry, which is right for these armies:
  even in points, Genestealers charging boltgun Legionaries deal about 0.24 (points-weighted shares) and take about 0.50
  back; against Easy, which walks into them, the Genestealers and Tyrant Guard charge too.
- **Open:**
  - *For M69 (Hard):* Hard's code is untouched. Its chain still uses a unit's representative model (per-model loadouts),
    prices no [HAZARDOUS] profile (its calls pass no rate), knows no engaged-unit facts beyond its own close-quarters
    band, its translations (`translation_ok`) don't pass infantry (17.01), and its army value has no points tiebreak. Where
    it uses Normal's functions (move plans and candidates, Normal's answers as its defaults, the lookahead's shooting) it
    gets M68's changes on the roster scenario (its old-scenario play is unchanged: hashes).
  - Normal's horizon is two half-turns, so units far from anything they can reach hold rather than walk into range (on
    the rosters the Tyranid infantry; measured right for these armies, above).
  - The Desperate Escape guard checks one to three failed hazard rolls, as Hard's; more are not checked.
  - The firer's own hazard loss is priced in the choice of profile but not taken off the move score's line; and D-071's
    "no hazard roll after the last enemy model is destroyed" is ignored.
  - Random damage is still planned at the preview's maximum, and the bots still never turn an oval (both measured, D-077).
