## M69: Hard for the new units ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `max` · **Plan mode:** design first, then pre-approved · **Decision:** D-078 (amends D-067)

> **Measure first.**
>
> **Chain tables.**
> - One cache per game, sized to every pair of unit types in play, with deterministic eviction under a memory cap.
> - A coarser wounds grid only above 20 wounds per side.
> - A unit's volley is the sum of its loadout groups.
>
> **Army value** includes the points tiebreak.
>
> **Rollout budget** scales with the number of units, counted in simulated actions.

**Acceptance** (Release arena, new scenario, 200 seeds, both seats)
- Hard beats Normal in at least 60% of games.
- Hard p95 ≤ 500 ms and max ≤ 3 s, including the first decision.
- Zero rejections, reverts and fallbacks.
- V1, the bot scenarios and Strike play identically (hashes). Replays are unchanged.
- Tests `M69_`.

**Results (2026-11-08):** design in D-078 (amends D-067), measured and written first and approved with conditions before
any code (compact storage and parallel table builds added at the review). Bot only (`src/ai`): no rule, core, state,
action, event or JSON change, no new decision and no client change. Normal and Easy are untouched.
- **Measured first** (the M68 binary, Release, `--trace` seed 1; D-078 has the profile): Hard as the Chaos Space Marines
  against Normal took 555 s a game (p95 20.3 s, max 36.7 s, peak RSS 2.87 GB), as the Tyranids 852 s (p95 24.2 s, max
  33.7 s, 2.59 GB). The time was chain-table thrash: 30 pairs of unit types against an 8-table cache, every Evaluator
  asking for all 30 in the same order, so the first decision built 240 tables in 35.7 of its 36.0 s. With that fixed,
  65% of the instructions were core's line of sight (D-074's oval path), a third of it the army value's per-pair
  `best_shooting`. Every Hard reason read "draw 100%": D-007's army value can't see a points win.
- **What changed** (`src/ai/hard/*`, D-078):
  - *One chain cache per game* (`hard::ChainCache`, now a handle on a shared store). Its capacity is the larger of 8 and
    the pairs the latest army Evaluator needs (30 on the rosters). Table bytes are capped at 640 MiB, and the least
    recently used table goes first (a lookup counter, so the order is deterministic), never one in use. The half-turns
    already played are trimmed at each Hard decision. The arena's two Hard bots share one cache
    (`Bot::share_chain_cache`).
  - *The tables' form.* A side whose unit type starts with more than 20 wounds gets a coarse wounds grid (a level every
    ceil(W / 20) wounds, plus W: the Chaos Terminators' 16 levels). A volley's outcomes between levels are split in
    proportion and lookups interpolate. When the game's tables would take more than 256 MiB at full precision (the
    rosters), they are stored as floats (compact). Every table now has a position-major layout and no rows for 0
    wounds. The rosters' 30 tables take about 525 MB at the first decision instead of 1.45 GB. V1, both bot scenarios
    and Strike keep exact, full-precision tables, the same value for value as M68's (fingerprints).
  - *Parallel builds:* up to `LookaheadWeights::table_workers` `std::thread`s (default min(4, the hardware's)). Each
    worker has its own `OddsCache` and output table, every worker is joined before the Evaluator returns, errors are
    rethrown after the join, and the cache changes only on the calling thread, in pair order. The client's path is
    safe: `BotDriver` runs one decision at a time, and its config changes wait until no worker runs.
  - *Loadout groups:* a unit whose models differ in loadout (the Havocs, the squads' champions) is its own models in
    keep order (Normal's `model_worth` against the pair's other unit type), padded with its most common loadout. Its
    volley is the engine-shaped sum of those models' picks.
  - *[HAZARDOUS] and 17.01:* a [HAZARDOUS] profile is priced at Normal's `hazard_rate` in the chain and in the half-turn
    in progress. In the rollouts' translations a MONSTER/VEHICLE passes enemy infantry.
  - *The points tiebreak in the army value:* under `points_destroyed`, V = P(A wipes B out) - P(B wipes A out) +
    P(neither) x T, where T = erf(mu / (sigma sqrt 2)) comes from the engine's tally and increments (Abramowitz–Stegun
    7.1.28; the exact sign when sigma = 0). A finished game, or one with every half-turn played, takes the engine's
    result exactly. One unit a side keeps the army path under the rule (`duel_play`).
  - *Rollout budget by units:* when a Monte Carlo starts with more than 7 units with models, its rollouts and action
    budget scale by 7 / n, with at least 3 rollouts (14 units: 3 rollouts and 3,000 actions;
    `LookaheadWeights::full_budget_units = 7`).
  - *Shared shot options:* the half-turn in progress computes each shooter's `shot_options` once per evaluation
    (`analysis::shot_option_sets` and a `best_shooting` overload; the plans are the same).
  - *Strength, after the design* (D-078 part 11; gated on facts the old scenarios don't have, as the go-ahead allowed):
    - Under the points rule there is no keep-away move: D-067's furthest move, which plays for D-007's draw.
    - A side with more than 7 units with models moves as Normal (`BotWeights::move_search_units = 7`) and still makes
      Hard's own charge, casualty, coherency and consolidation decisions.
  - *Arena:* `--weights` takes `full_budget_units`, `table_workers` and `move_search_units`.
- **Tests** (`tests/ai/m69_test.cpp`: 17, plus 1 disabled; all pass; no existing test changed):
  - `M69_Cache`:
    - `TheRostersArmyNeedsThirtyTablesAndOneCacheHoldsThemAll`;
    - `EvictionTakesTheLeastRecentlyUsedTableAndNeverOneInUse`;
    - `TrimmingDropsOnlyTheHalfTurnsAlreadyPlayed`;
    - `TheOldScenariosTablesAreTheM68Codes` (V1, both bot scenarios and Strike against M68's table fingerprints; glibc
      only);
    - `OneWorkerAndFourBuildTheSameTablesAndHardDecidesAlike`.
  - `M69_Grid`:
    - `OnlyASideOverTwentyWoundsIsCoarseAndOnlyTheRostersAreCompact`;
    - `ACoarseSideInterpolatesBetweenLevelsAndKeepsTheExpectedWoundsOfAVolley`.
  - `M69_Loadouts.TheChainsVolleyIsTheSumOfTheUnitsLoadoutGroups`.
  - `M69_Rollouts`:
    - `AMonstersTranslationPassesThroughInfantryButNotInfantrys`;
    - `TheBudgetScalesWithTheUnitsWithModelsWhenEachMonteCarloStarts`.
  - `M69_Points`:
    - `AnIncrementIsWhatTheEnginesTallyAddsWhenTheUnitIsDestroyed`;
    - `TheLeadIsTheExactSignWithoutVarianceAndErfOtherwise`;
    - `AFinishedGameIsValuedAsTheEngineDecidedIt`;
    - `ThePointsRuleSettlesTheDrawAndWithoutItTheArmyValueIsDSevens`;
    - `UnderThePointsRuleHardOffersNoKeepAwayMove`.
  - `M69_Moves.ASideWithMoreThanSevenUnitsWithModelsMovesAsNormal`.
  - `M69_Games.HardAgainstNormalInEachSeatPlaysTheRostersClean` (two-round games, 36 s in Debug) and the disabled
    `DISABLED_FullGamesOfHardOnTheRostersAreClean`.
  - ctest (Debug and Release): 1141 -> 1158 tests, all pass. The disabled full-games test passes in 59 s in Release:
    4 games in each seat and 2 Hard vs Hard, 2.9–10.3 s a game.
- **Acceptance** (Release arena, `data/scenarios/rosters_44x60.json`, seeds 1–200 a seat; the two seats ran at once on
  the 4-core machine, under `/usr/bin/time -v`):
  - *Hard beats Normal in at least 60% of games in each seat:* **met.**
    - Hard as the Chaos Space Marines (A) against Normal: 159 wins, 41 losses, 0 draws (79.5%). Every game went to
      points after battle round 5; on average 331.3 points destroyed to 207.6. Normal's own Chaos Space Marines won 60 of
      200 against Normal (M68).
    - Hard as the Tyranids (B) against Normal: 137 wins, 63 losses, 0 draws (68.5%). Every game went to points after
      battle round 5; on average 423.8 to 344.5. This is no better than Normal's own Tyranids, which won 140 of 200
      against Normal (M68). The design as first built won 93 of 200 here (46.5%); part 11's two changes raised it, the
      second by playing Normal's moves while the Tyranids have more than 7 units (Open, below).
  - *Hard's decision time p95 ≤ 500 ms and max ≤ 3 s, including the first decision:* **met.** Each decision is timed
    around `Bot::decide` by the arena (steady clock, Release, the per-side report line):
    - as the Chaos Space Marines: p50 0.009 ms, p95 231 ms, max 1.91 s;
    - as the Tyranids: p50 0.008 ms, p95 9.3 ms, max 1.71 s;
    - Normal against them: p95 7.0 / 11.0 ms, max 100 / 312 ms.
    - The first decision, quiet machine, one process, `--trace` seeds 1–3 in each seat:
      - default workers (4 here): 895–927 ms as the Chaos Space Marines (a MoveUnits), 753–871 ms as the Tyranids
        (their first Hard decision, an OrderCasualties);
      - 1 worker (what a 1–2 core PC sees, at this machine's speed per core): 2.78–2.92 s and 2.73–2.83 s.
      - In every traced game the first decision is the slowest. The maxima above (1.7–1.9 s) are first decisions with
        two processes' table workers sharing the 4 cores.
  - *Zero rejected actions, reverted moves, fallbacks and unfinished games:* **met** in the 400 games and in Hard vs
    Hard (below). Normal's side removed models for coherency in 2 games (seeds 64 and 158), as at M68.
  - *Peak memory (the go-ahead's 1 GB per arena process):* **met.** Maximum resident set size: Hard vs Normal 662 MB,
    Normal vs Hard 651 MB, Hard vs Hard (two Hard bots sharing the cache) 610 MB and 574 MB (M68: 2.59–2.87 GB).
  - *Hard vs Hard on the rosters* (8 games, seeds 1–8, in two runs of 4): clean. The Chaos Space Marines won all 8 on
    points (402.9 to 205.6 on average). 33.3 s and 31.8 s for each run of 4, about 8 s a game. Hard as A: p95
    237 / 191 ms, max 1.63 / 1.26 s; Hard as B: p95 12.7 / 19.8 ms, max 216 / 434 ms.
  - *V1, both bot scenarios and Strike play identically:* **met.**
    - The Release arena's `--per-game` output matches `baseline/m60` on every `game` line (actions, final state and
      trace hashes) for all 24 pairings of V1, both bot scenarios and Strike: 4,380 games (`baseline/run.sh`, run on its
      own).
    - `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the five ai regenerations (M45, M46, M47, M58, M68) leave
      `tests/replays` byte-identical (`git status --short tests/replays` is empty).
    - `M47_`, `M58_` and `M68_Replay.TheSavedFileMatchesTheGame` pass in Debug and Release.
  - *Normal's roster play identical to M68:* **met.** `--per-game` on the rosters, seeds 1–200, matches the M68 binary
    on every `game` line for Easy–Easy, Normal–Easy, Easy–Normal and Normal–Normal (800 games). The M68 replay
    (`m68_rosters_normal_bot_game.json`) regenerates byte-identical.
  - *Strike timing* (quiet machine, alternating with `verify_arena_m68`):
    - Normal vs Normal, 60 games, 4 runs each: **met.** M68 9.09, 8.98, 8.82, 9.30 s (mean 9.05); M69 8.99, 9.07, 9.04,
      8.81 s (mean 8.98): -0.8%. Decision p95 5.81–5.97 against 5.78–5.92 ms.
    - Hard vs Hard, `--games 20`, 3 runs each: **met** on the run time. M68 60.05, 59.67, 59.90 s (mean 59.87); M69
      61.67, 61.61, 59.89 s (mean 61.06): +2.0%. The decision p95 is 5.7% higher (134.3–136.3 against 127.9–128.8
      ms), and the max is lower (501–550 against 549–570 ms).
  - *Tests `M69_`:* **met** (above).
- **Not added:** a replay. M69's flow is Hard's play on the rosters, which M70's weight sweep will change, and M70's
  plan adds the Hard vs Hard rosters replay (`m70_rosters_bot_game.json`). M57 did the same (its bot-game replay came
  in M58).
- **Open:**
  - *Hard's own move search for a big army:* the rosters' Tyranids (9 units) win with Normal's moves. With Hard's own
    search they won 35 of 60 seeds against 44 with Normal's. The army value is optimistic for them early (a win at
    60–100% in battle round 1), so the search holds units back while the Chaos Space Marines shoot them. Calibrating the
    chain for these units is left to M70's sweep or later. Part 11 lists what was tried and not kept: `margin` 0.05,
    points payoffs in the pair chains, a wider points lead, 6 rollouts at every size, and `share` 0.8.
  - *Strike's Hard vs Hard decision p95* is up 5.7% at the same play. The run time is within the acceptance; the cause
    was not profiled.
  - From D-078 part 10:
    - the chain has no state for a third unit, so an engaged target's 17.03 -1 and a MONSTER's close-quarters shot at
      another unit appear only in the half-turn in progress;
    - ovals are not turned;
    - the Desperate Escape guard checks one to three failed rolls.

## M70: Bot-game replay, docs, final verification ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-08)

> **Tuning.** A `BotWeights` sweep on the new scenario.
>
> **Replay.** `tests/replays/m70_rosters_bot_game.json` (Hard vs Hard, a seed that ends in a result), with
> determinism, `TheSavedFileMatchesTheGame` and `M70_Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateRostersBotGameReplay` tests.
>
> **Docs.** CLAUDE.md (the scenario, the regenerate command, the client keys, the fixture scenarios), the
> README, the README text `package.ps1` writes, and PLAN.md.

**Acceptance**
- Clean Debug and Release builds and all of `ctest`.
- `git diff main -- tests/replays` shows only new files.
- A mid-game bot screenshot on the new board.
- Manual check left for the user: play the new scenario by mouse on Windows, including rotating the Tyrannofex,
  the re-roll prompt and the tiebreak.

**Results (2026-11-08):** no decision added (no default and no bot behaviour changed); the client layout fix amends
D-030. Bot code (`src/ai`) untouched: the clean Release arena binary is byte-identical to M69's.
- **Tuning: `BotWeights{}` unchanged.** Release arena, `rosters_44x60.json`, against Normal; `--weights` on the M69
  binary, two processes at a time, about 1.4 process-hours in all (well inside the 6-hour budget).
  - *Target:* the Tyranid seat (Normal vs Hard), where Hard won 137 of 200 at M69 against Normal's own 140. Defaults
    on seeds 1–60: 44 wins.
  - *Screened on seeds 1–60 (Tyranid wins):* margin 0.05: 47; margin -1 (Normal's answer just one more candidate): 42;
    tempo 0.4: 42; tempo 0: 43; material 0.2: 42; material 0: 43. With the Tyranids' 9 units moving by Hard's own
    search (`move_search_units=9`), combined with what D-078 part 11 had not tried: rollouts only (`share=1`): 22;
    `margin=0.03,share=0.8`: 23 (M69's own search, with the defaults: 35). The moves are what matter there, and Hard's
    search loses the seat even when its plans are valued by the engine-played rollouts alone (one turn and the
    enemy's reply), not only through the chain's optimism: holding back looks better than closing over that horizon.
  - *Confirmed on seeds 1–200:* margin 0.05 (only the Tyranids' charge, casualty, coherency and consolidation
    decisions change while they move as Normal): 140 against the defaults' 137, +3, short of the +10 needed, so it was
    not run on seeds 201–400 and not kept.
  - *The defaults on seeds 201–400* (for the comparison): Hard won 130 as the Tyranids (70 losses) and 156 as the
    Chaos Space Marines (44 losses).
  - *Every run* was clean: 0 rejected, reverted, fallbacks or unfinished.
- **Bot-game replay** `tests/replays/m70_rosters_bot_game.json`: Hard vs Hard (the arena's play, both bots sharing one
  chain cache) on `rosters_44x60.json`, dice seed 93, 501 steps, decided on points destroyed after battle round 5
  (Side A 329.5, Side B 523: a Tyranid win), not at the step cap.

  - *`git diff main -- tests/replays` shows only new files:* **met.** `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the M45,
    M46, M47, M58, M68 and M70 regenerations leave every tracked replay byte-identical. Against `main` the only
    replay files are the batch's nine new ones.
  - *A mid-game bot screenshot on the new board:* **met** (scratchpad `m70/client/`):
    - `hard_both_150.png`, `hard_both_220.png` (seed 1, the brief's command, round 3);
    - `hard_both_seed93_400.png`, `hard_both_seed93_440.png` (the replay's game in round 5, after the Terminators'
      charge, with a Screamer-killer destroyed);
    - `normal_both_120.png`, `normal_both_200.png` (Normal, round 2).
    - No seed of the 100 searched has units engaged in round 2 or 3 in Hard vs Hard, so the engaged board is in
      round 5.
  - *Old play unchanged:* **met.**
    - The 24 `--per-game` files of V1, both bot scenarios and Strike match `baseline/m60` on every `game` line.
    - Normal's roster play (Easy–Easy, Normal–Easy, Easy–Normal, Normal–Normal, seeds 1–200) matches M69's.
  - *Strike timing against the M69 binary*, alternating, 3 runs each: **met.**
    - Normal vs Normal (60 games): 8.90 against 8.87 s.
    - Hard vs Hard (20 games): 60.39 against 60.65 s (+0.4%; decision p95 133.0–134.1 against 131.7–134.3 ms).
  - *Hard vs Normal on the rosters, seeds 1–200 each seat* (final binary, the two seats at once, `/usr/bin/time -v`),
    the same as M69:
    - Hard as the Chaos Space Marines: 159 wins and 41 losses (points destroyed 331.3 to 207.6), p95 230 ms, max
      1.91 s, peak RSS 622 MB.
    - Hard as the Tyranids: 137 wins and 63 losses (423.8 to 344.5), p95 9.1 ms, max 1.75 s, peak RSS 617 MB.
    - 0 rejected, reverted or fallbacks.
  - *Manual check for the user* (by mouse on Windows: the roster scenario, turning the Tyrannofex, the re-roll prompt,
    the tiebreak): **open.**
- **Open:**
  - Hard is still no better than Normal from the Tyranid seat (137 against Normal's 140). The sweep's numbers above
    show that the move horizon, not only the army value, keeps Hard's own search from closing in. Carried to Later
    item 5.
  - At a narrow window the casualty decision's box can still fall below the bar's bottom (the bar scrolls).
  - During the sweep one Hard decision took 2.65 s (max), with two arenas and a build sharing the 4 cores. Under the
    3 s limit; the quiet final runs' maximum was 1.91 s.

## Custom battles: army builder and hand deployment (M71–M77)

**Run note (agreed with the user 2026-11-09):** M71–M77 are pre-approved as one batch and run back to back
without `/clear` and without per-milestone sign-off, on branch `custom-battles`. This overrides "one
milestone per session" and "agree each milestone first" for these milestones only.
- **Process:** each milestone is implemented by a sub-agent at the effort shown, then verified, marked ✅,
  committed and pushed. Verification means:
  - clean Debug and Release builds, and all of `ctest`;
  - every replay regeneration leaves `tests/replays` byte-identical apart from the milestone's new file;
  - the identity set: Release arena `--per-game` on V1, both bot scenarios, Strike **and the rosters** matches
    the pre-batch baseline (recorded from `main` at Step 0) wherever play must not change;
  - screenshots where the client changes.
- **Design first:** in M71, M73 and M74 the sub-agent writes its decision entry first. It is reviewed against
  the rules and BSData before any code.
- **Every milestone also gives:**
  - new actions and events their log text, action text and dice cards;
  - undo grouping;
  - a `fallback.cpp` / bot answer for a new decision;
  - JSON round trips, with new fields omitted when default so old files stay byte-identical;
  - a replay of its flow.
- **Stability rules:**
  - `state_hash` hashes the `Stage` integer and the decision kind's index, so a new stage, decision, action
    or event is **appended** at the end of its enum or variant.
  - New state is hashed behind a tag only when set.
  - New read-only queries the client logic calls go in `core/state/` or `core/data/` (the client-logic
    include allow-list).
- **Missed targets** are recorded as open items, never lowered silently.
- **Ambiguities** get the conservative reading, recorded in `docs/decisions.md`.
- **At the end** the branch goes to `main` by a rebase-merged pull request. No release (version stays 0.1.0).

**Agreed with the user (2026-11-09):**
- **Custom battle mode:** each side builds an army from its faction's datasheets up to a points limit, then
  plays hotseat, against the bot, or bot vs bot.
- **Factions:** Chaos Space Marines and Tyranids (the ten roster datasheets).
  - Space Marines (Intercessors) stay for the old scenarios only.
  - The two sides must be different factions: no mirror matches.
- **Battle size:** Incursion only, 1000 pts. The battle is set up as in `rosters_44x60.json`:
  - 44"x60" portrait, with 12" zones on the south (A) and north (B) edges;
  - 5 battle rounds;
  - `result: points_destroyed` (D-076).
- **List rules from BSData, enforced:**
  - points by unit size, plus wargear costs;
  - the surcharge on a datasheet's 3rd+ copy (+10, or +30 for Terminators), kept in the data although it can't
    trigger at Incursion;
  - copy limits at Incursion: 2 per datasheet, 4 for Battleline;
  - at least one CHARACTER per army (a Chaos Lord or a Hive Tyrant), BSData's roster rule (agreed after M71's
    survey).
- **Loadouts:** model counts and full wargear slots.
  - An option with a weapon the engine refuses is greyed out with the reason and can't be picked. Defaults skip
    such options.
  - A model type with no playable loadout is shown as unavailable.
  - A weapon is pickable only if every one of its profiles is playable.
- **Armies:** either side can be custom; each side's default is its stock Incursion roster. In vs-bot play the
  bot's side starts on its default roster and the player may edit it.
- **First turn:** chosen in the builder (default A).
- **Deployment:** "Auto-place" (generated formations, as today) or "Place by hand".
  - Place by hand is an engine step through `PendingDecision`.
  - Players alternate setting up one unit at a time, starting with the side that takes the second turn. Once one
    side has set up everything, the other sets up the rest. This wording is the user's (mission-pack text, not
    core rules) and goes in D-081.
  - Each set-up follows 03.02: coherency (03.03), unengaged (03.04), wholly within its own zone (D-009), no overlap
    (D-064).
  - A unit with no legal spot left counts as destroyed, and the opponent scores its points (D-076). This option
    is offered only when the engine finds no legal spot.
- **Bots:**
  - *Deployment:* a simple heuristic. Easy uses the auto-layout spot. Normal and Hard order their units (long-range
    shooters first and back, melee last and forward) and nudge them toward the enemy.
  - *Normal's points weighting:* fixed for one-datasheet sides (amends D-077). The one `M68_Points` expectation
    that pins today's lopsided value is updated, with the reason recorded.
- **Colours:** the 10 unit shades per side wrap; the board numbers tell units apart.

**What the batch added (summary, M77):**
- *List-building data:* the fetch tool reads BSData's list rules into a `list_building` block (points by unit size,
  the 3rd-copy surcharge, copy limits with Incursion's, wargear slots, option costs and limits) with a `--fetched` flag;
  the ten roster datasheets regenerated, every existing key byte-identical (M71: 02.06, 02.07, D-079 part 1).
- *Army lists:* `ArmyList` and the army file format, `data/factions.json`, the stock lists in `data/armies/`, every list
  rule enforced and rule-cited (`army_problems`: factions, 02.06 counts, 02.07 slots, limits, refused weapons, copies,
  1000 points, a CHARACTER, fitting the zone), playable defaults, random legal armies, the auto-layout, and
  `custom_battle_scenario` (M72: 02.06, 02.07, 03.02, 03.03, 24.11, D-079 part 2); the arena's `--army-a`, `--army-b`,
  `--first` and `--random-armies 1`.
- *Bots on custom armies:* a budget in front of Hard's chain tables (over 1 GiB or 80 tables Normal answers), Normal's
  points weighting for one-datasheet sides, the budgets measured (M73, D-080, amends D-077 and D-078).
- *Deployment by hand:* `Stage::Deploy`, the `DeployUnits` decision and the `DeployUnit` / `CannotSetUp` actions, the
  sides alternating one unit at a time from the second-turn side, every set-up checked by `set_up_problems` (03.02,
  03.03, 03.04, D-009, D-064), core's proposals (the auto-layout slot, else a scan), a unit with no legal spot counting
  as destroyed (M74: 03.02–03.04, 01.03, D-081, amends D-003, D-007, D-065); the arena's `--deploy hand|auto`.
- *The client:* the army builder ("Custom battle..." on the start screen: two lists, unit editor with greyed refused
  options, problems, save / load, first turn, deployment mode, play mode and bot level; M75, D-082 part 1) and hand
  deployment on the board (a ghost unit with core's verdict and the models at fault, dragging, Shift-dragging, turning
  ovals, Auto-place this unit / the rest, Can't be set up, one undo group per unit; M76, D-082 part 2); the flags
  `--builder 1`, `--army-a`, `--army-b`, `--first`, `--deploy`, `--open-slot` and `--deploy-unit`.
- *The bots' deployment:* Easy at Auto-place's spots in id order; Normal and Hard by a heuristic (long-range shooters
  first and back while in range, melee units last and nearer the enemy, nudged toward the enemy's set-up units within
  a bounded set of translations core accepts; M77, D-081).
- *Replays added:* `m72_custom_battle_round2`, `m73_custom_bot_game` (Normal vs Normal), `m74_hand_deployment`,
  `m75_builder_battle` (Easy vs Easy) and `m77_custom_hand_deploy_bot_game` (Normal vs Normal, set up by hand), with the
  fixtures `tests/data/scenarios/hand_deployment_44x60.json`, `m73_custom_battle_44x60.json`,
  `m75_builder_battle_44x60.json`, `m77_custom_hand_deploy_44x60.json` and the lists in `tests/data/armies/`.
- *Play elsewhere unchanged:* V1, both bot scenarios, Strike and the rosters play exactly as before the batch (the
  arena's `--per-game` hashes against the pre-batch baseline, 30 files and 4,740 games, every milestone), and every
  pre-batch replay regenerates byte-identically. No release (the version stays 0.1.0).

## M71: List-building data from BSData ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-09), design first · **Rules:** 02.06, 02.07 · **Decision:** D-079 part 1

> **Fetch tool.** `tools/fetch_bsdata.py` emits one new root key, `list_building`, after `weapons`. Every existing
> key stays byte-identical. A new `--fetched <date>` flag keeps each file's date. It reads:
> - points brackets by model count, the copy surcharge, and `max_copies` (`default` and `incursion`);
> - wargear slots (min 1 / max 1 groups with their default). Nested slots are flattened into composite options;
> - limits counting `weapons`, `option` or `model_type`, with `max`, `below_models` and `per_models`. This
>   includes the "models < T and count > K" error groups and per-model mutual exclusions;
> - option costs.
>
> Only limits scoped to the model, the unit, `parent` or `force`/`roster` count. Limits scoped to other datasheets
> are dropped (e.g. the Berzerker plasma-pistol limit). Non-weapon subtrees (Marks, icons, Unholy Patronage,
> Warlord, Boons, Weapon Upgrades, the D-070 list) are skipped once they are confirmed to cost 0. Anything
> unrecognised in scope warns.
>
> **Data.** Regenerate the 10 CSM and Tyranid files at `0f90355`. `intercessor_squad.json` is untouched.
>
> **Core.** `Datasheet::list_building` (optional) with `WargearOption`, `WargearSlot`, `WargearLimit`,
> `ModelWargear`, `PointsBracket`, `CopySurcharge` and `ListBuilding`. Inconsistent data is a `DataError` citing
> D-079. `loadout_fits_slots` goes in `core/data/army_rules.*`. The scenario loader's flat 02.07 check is unchanged.
>
> **D-079 part 1** gives the shape, the patterns and a full per-unit table (slots, options, limits, brackets,
> copies, surcharge) for review. It also gives the readings: Terminators +30; a champion's plasma pistol together
> with the special "Plasma pistol and Astartes chainsword"; a Hive Tyrant may take two scything talons.

**Acceptance**
- `git diff data/units` shows only the `list_building` blocks; the old loader and datasheet tests are unchanged.
- Tests `D079_ListData`:
  - slots and fixed weapons equal the flat `weapon_options`, and the defaults equal `default_weapons`;
  - points by size, wargear costs, copy limits, the surcharge and the limits as read;
  - the stock rosters cost 960 and 955;
  - malformed data is refused.
- Tests `R02_02_Datasheet.FilesWithoutListDataStillLoad` and
  `R02_07_WargearOptions.EveryShippedScenarioFitsTheSlotModel`.
- Replays and the identity set unchanged.

**Results (2026-11-09):** D-079 part 1 was written first and stopped for review (the survey, the shape and the per-unit
table); it was approved as written, with the "at least one CHARACTER" note reworded (BSData's Army Roster force
requires one). The user then decided that custom armies must include at least one CHARACTER; M72 enforces it,
and the batch's agreed list gains it there.
- **Survey** (every entry, group, link, constraint and modifier under the ten datasheets, links' own children and
  costs included): nothing unrecognised. Read: copy limits with their Incursion modifiers, 5 size brackets, 5
  surcharges, 15 slots (+2 nested, flattened), 50 fixed weapons, 2 option costs, 88 per-model maxima (3 bind), 22
  unit-scoped maxima (15 limits) and 2 model-type error groups. Dropped: other datasheets' scopes (Khorne Berzerkers,
  Legionaries seen from Havocs, Chosen), Crusade, Boarding Actions, Enhancements cost type, leader associations,
  display. D-079 has the counts, the patterns and the table.
- **Fetch tool** (`tools/fetch_bsdata.py`): a D-079 reader (`ListReader`) writes `list_building` after `weapons`
  (points brackets, `copy_surcharge`, `max_copies`, per model type `fixed` / `slots` / `limits`, `unit_limits`). An
  entry link's own children, costs, constraints and modifiers count; per-model maxima are written only where they bind;
  mutual exclusions become one limit; nested slots are flattened ("Power fist and Combi-weapon"). Anything
  unrecognised in scope is warned about and the datasheet gets no `list_building` (`intercessor_squad.json` would get
  none: its two unread shapes are warned; Chosen and Gargoyles likewise). New `--fetched <YYYY-MM-DD>`.
- **Data:** the ten files regenerated at `0f90355` with their own dates. `git diff --numstat data/units`: insertions
  only (the new block and the comma line), 0 deletions; every existing key byte-identical. `intercessor_squad.json`
  untouched.
- **Core** (loader-side only; `GameState`, the scenario loader and its flat 02.07 check unchanged):
  - `datasheet.hpp`: `WargearOption`, `WargearSlot` (`find_option`), `LimitCounts`, `BelowModels`, `PerModels`,
    `WargearLimit`, `ModelWargear`, `PointsBracket`, `CopySurcharge`, `ListBuilding` (`find_wargear`) and
    `std::optional<ListBuilding> Datasheet::list_building`.
  - `unit_loader.cpp` parses it and refuses inconsistent data with a `DataError` citing D-079 (model types out of step,
    unknown or out-of-options weapons, empty or duplicate options, bad defaults, brackets, copies, surcharge, limits).
  - New `core/data/army_rules.{hpp,cpp}`: `loadout_fits_slots` (deterministic depth-first decomposition into fixed
    weapons plus one option per slot, or a 02.07 / D-079 reason), `fit_option_points`, `bracket_points`, `limit_max`.
- **Tests** (`tests/list_data_test.cpp`, 14; no existing test changed):
  - `D079_ListData.*`: `EveryFactionDatasheetHasListData`, `SlotsAndFixedCoverExactlyTheFlatOptions` (no exceptions),
    `DefaultChoicesGiveTheDefaultWeapons`, `PointsByUnitSize`, `WargearCosts`, `CopyLimits`, `CopySurcharge`,
    `UnitAndModelLimitsAsRead` (every limit of the ten; the Berzerker plasma limit not applied, the champion's plasma
    pistol with the composite special allowed, no Terminator combi-weapon limit, two scything talons but one cannon,
    two Havoc power fists but one accursed weapon, limits by unit size), `StockRostersCost960And955`,
    `MalformedListDataIsADataError` (26 malformed cases, each refused citing D-079).
  - `R02_02_Datasheet.FilesWithoutListDataStillLoad`; `R02_07_WargearOptions.ALoadoutDecomposesIntoFixedWeaponsAndOne
    OptionPerSlot`, `...ALoadoutThatDoesNotDecomposeIsRefusedWithAReason`, `...EveryShippedScenarioFitsTheSlotModel`
    (50 units of the 8 scenario files: slots, limits and points).
  - ctest (Debug and Release): 1162 -> 1176 tests, all pass.
- **No replay:** M71 changes no flow (loader-side data only), so none was added.
- **Acceptance:**
  - *`git diff data/units` shows only the `list_building` blocks; the old loader and datasheet tests unchanged:* **met.**
  - *`D079_ListData` tests, `R02_02_Datasheet.FilesWithoutListDataStillLoad`,
    `R02_07_WargearOptions.EveryShippedScenarioFitsTheSlotModel`:* **met** (the stock rosters cost 960 and 955).
  - *Replays unchanged:* **met.** `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the M45, M46, M47, M58, M68 and M70
    regenerations leave `tests/replays` byte-identical.
  - *Identity set unchanged:* **met.** The Release arena's `--per-game` output on V1, both bot scenarios, Strike and
    the rosters (30 files, 4,740 games) matches the pre-batch baseline on every `game` line (actions, state and trace
    hashes).

## M72: Army lists, auto-layout, arena flags ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-09) · **Rules:** 02.06, 02.07, 03.02, 03.03 · **Decision:** D-079 part 2

> **Core.**
> - `core/data/formation.*`: the formation code lifted out of the loader verbatim, placing bit for bit as before.
> - `core/data/zone_layout.*`: the packer.
> - `core/data/army_list.*`:
>   - `ArmyList`, `ArmyUnit`, `ArmyModelGroup`, `Faction`, `unit_points` and `load_factions`;
>   - `army_problems`: faction (opposing factions only), at least one unit, 02.06 counts, 02.07 slot
>     decomposition, limits, refused weapons, copies, the 1000-pt limit, and fitting the zone. Every problem is
>     rule-cited;
>   - `weapon_refusal` and `default_unit` (playable defaults per slot; unavailable model types skipped);
>   - `random_army` (seeded, capped retries);
>   - `custom_battle_scenario(a, b, first_turn, mode, resolver)`, which writes rosters-style scenario JSON for
>     `build_game_state`.
>
> **Data.**
> - `data/factions.json`.
> - `data/armies/chaos_space_marines_incursion.json` and `tyranids_incursion.json`: the stock rosters as units
>   and loadouts only, in roster order.
> - Army file: `{"format":1,"kind":"vibehammer-army","name","faction","units":[{unit_file, models:[{type,count,weapons}]}]}`.
>   Points are computed, never stored.
>
> **Auto-layout (D-079).**
> - Each unit gets the fewest ranks that make it coherent and fit the zone (20 Termagants 3 ranks, 10
>   Legionaries 2, 6 Tyrant Guard 1); ovals are at facing 0.
> - Shelves are packed in list order, west to east, 1" apart, the first touching the zone front; each shelf is
>   centred.
> - The fallback is first-fit decreasing by width, else the problem "does not fit".
> - Positions are on a 1/8" grid, so they round-trip exactly.
>
> **Arena.** `--army-a`, `--army-b`, `--first A|B`, `--random-armies 1`. The `--per-game` format is unchanged.
>
> **Package.** `tools/package.ps1` ships `data/armies/` and `data/factions.json`. `/armies/` (user lists) is
> gitignored.
