    never), `D079_CustomBattle.ScenarioBuildsAPointsGame`;
  - `R24_11_ExtraAttacks.ATyrantWithTwoScythingTalonsSelectsBoth` (24.11: all [EXTRA ATTACKS] weapons, and no other melee
    weapon to add: one talon alone is refused, both are valid);
  - `D004_LegalActions.RandomPlayOnRandomLegalArmies` (the showcase armies with the full D-004 check at three distances,
    12 random-army battles of plain random play; every never-played loadout fires or fights; every game ends and
    round-trips), `Replay.RandomGamesOnRandomLegalArmiesReplayIdentically`;
  - `M72_Arena.*` (ai, 4): a scenario per seed plays as the fixed scenario line for line; the arena's random armies are
    the tests'; random-army games finish clean at Easy; army lists make a custom battle.
  - No engine bug turned up: random play and the arena on random legal armies were clean, so no engine change.
- **Existing tests changed** (two, not weakened): `D074_Json.OldFilesLoadTheSame` and `D076_Json.AnOldFileLoadsAndSaves
  TheSame` iterate every saved replay with expectations for files older than M65 / M67 (no facing; an embedded state
  without points). The new `m72_custom_battle_round2.json` legitimately has both, so it is exempted from those two
  expectations by name (as `m65_` / `m67_` files already are); it still goes through both byte-for-byte round trips.
- **Replay:** `tests/replays/m72_custom_battle_round2.json` (the two showcase armies, auto-placed, embedded initial
  state; random legal play with seed 10, picked by `Replay.DISABLED_SearchM72Seed`, through battle round 2: the Havoc
  autocannon and missile launcher, the Legionaries' missile launcher and the rupture cannon fire), written by
  `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and checked by `Replay.SavedReplayFilesPass`. It opens in the client's replay
  viewer (screenshot under `xvfb-run`).
- **Acceptance:**
  - *`D079_*` tests (stock armies at 960 / 955 and equal to the rosters scenario; the limits; 2000 random armies per
    faction fit):* **met.**
  - *`R02_06_*`, `R02_07_*`, `R24_11_ExtraAttacks.ATyrantWithTwoScythingTalonsSelectsBoth`,
    `D004_LegalActions.RandomPlayOnRandomLegalArmies`, the replay:* **met.**
  - *Easy vs Easy on random armies, seeds 1–200, clean:* **met.** Release: "games 200: side A (Chaos Space Marines) wins
    66, side B (Tyranids) wins 134, draws 0, unfinished 0 ... rejected actions 0, UnitMoveReverted 0, fallbacks 0"
    (16.8 s).
  - Normal vs Normal on random armies, seeds 1–200 (Release, extra): clean too ("games 200: ... wins 39, ... wins 161,
    draws 0, unfinished 0 ... rejected actions 0, UnitMoveReverted 0, fallbacks 0"; decision max 184 ms). Hard was not
    run on random armies (its memory guard is M73's).
  - *Every shipped scenario's state, every replay and the identity set unchanged:* **met.** The formation code is the
    loader's own, moved; its effect is shown by the replays and the identity set rather than by a separate state test.
    `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the M45, M46, M47, M58, M68 and M70 regenerations leave `tests/replays`
    byte-identical apart from the new `m72_custom_battle_round2.json`. The Release arena's `--per-game` output on V1, both
    bot scenarios, Strike and the rosters (30 files, 4,740 games) matches the pre-batch baseline on every `game` line.
  - ctest (Debug and Release): 1176 -> 1206 tests, all pass.

## M73: Bots on custom armies ✅ (done, with an open item)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-09), design first · **Decision:** D-080 (amends D-077, D-078)

> Bot work comes before the builder, so nobody can pick Hard against an oversized army.
>
> **Hard's guard.** Before any `Evaluator` is built, sum the planned chain tables' bytes and count. Above a budget
> (about 1 GiB and 40 tables, at least 1.5x the rosters' 30 tables and 0.6 GB), the decision is answered by Normal.
> The trace gives a D-080 reason. The guard is a pure function of the state and never triggers on the shipped
> scenarios.
>
> **Normal's weighting.** Weight by points whenever every unit has points and either side has several datasheets.
> Both sides single-datasheet stays at 1. The `M68_Points` one-type expectation is updated, with the reason
> recorded (agreed).
>
> **Budgets.** Measure how often `kMaxLoadouts`, `kMaxAdvanceSteps` and `move_search_units` are hit on random
> armies. A change is made only if gated, with play elsewhere proven unchanged.

**Acceptance**
- Tests `M73_HardGuard`, `M73_Weights` and `M73_Arena`.
- Replay `m73_custom_bot_game.json` (Normal vs Normal on two non-stock armies, to the result), with
  `TheSavedFileMatchesTheGame` and `M73_Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateCustomBotGameReplay`.
- Release arena on random armies, seeds 1–200:
  - pairings: Easy–Easy, Normal–Normal, Hard–Normal in both seats, and Hard–Hard over 20 games;
  - 0 rejected, reverted, fallback or unfinished;
  - Hard peak RSS ≤ 1.5 GB and decision max ≤ 3 s.
- Identity set unchanged (no shipped scenario is affected by the D-077 change).

**Results (2026-11-09):** D-080 (amends D-077 and D-078) was measured and written first and stopped for review. It was
approved as written, including the 80-table limit: the bytes drive memory and time, the count doesn't. Bot only
(`src/ai`): no rule, core, state, action, event or JSON change, no new decision, so no client or fallback change.
- **Measured first** (the M72 binary; D-080 has the numbers):
  - random armies (seeds 1–10,000) plan 1–64 chain tables (mean 22) and at most 1,066 MB at the first decision;
  - the shipped scenarios plan at most the rosters' 30 tables and 525 MB;
  - legal hand-built lists reach 1,449 MB (56 tables) and 80 tables (857 MB);
  - peak RSS is the tables plus 20–130 MB, and build time follows the bytes (about 1.7 ms per MB on 4 workers), not
    the count;
  - none of the budgets binds on random armies;
  - Hard's slowest decisions are searches with 10–14 units on the table.
- **Hard's guard** (`src/ai/hard/`):
  - `hard::chain_plan_size(state)` (`evaluator.*`) gives the count and estimated bytes of the tables an Evaluator at the
    state would hold. These are `plan_chain_tables`' figures, computed from the unit types and wounds alone:
    `army_plan`'s type collection is split out (`army_types`) and the byte formula is shared (`table_bytes`), so both
    count alike. The estimate itself is unchanged. It costs 0.64 ms on average on random armies (the full plan 3.8 ms),
    0.5 ms on the rosters and 0.07 ms on V1.
  - `BotWeights::chain_budget_mib = 1024` and `chain_budget_tables = 80` (`weights.hpp`). The arena's `--weights` takes
    the same names (0: no limit).
  - `hard::chain_budget_problem(state, weights)` (`hard_bot.*`) returns nullopt within the budget, else the reason's
    prefix. `hard_answer` checks it after `hard_applies` and `moves_as_normal`, only for the decisions Hard answers
    itself, and before any Evaluator is built. Over the budget, `normal_policy` answers with the same seed, and every
    reason of its plan reads "D-080: Hard's chain tables here would be 56 tables of 1381.5 MiB, over its budget of
    1024 MiB and 80 tables; Normal decides: ...". The chain cache is unchanged.
  - Examples on the most-bytes armies: as side A, Hard answers as Normal until battle round 2's second turn (1,014 MB
    then). Its first decision of its own (battle round 3, the tables built then) took 3.23 s. Peak RSS was 836 MiB,
    against 1,446–1,458 MiB unguarded.
- **Normal's weighting** (`src/ai/normal/value.cpp`): `share_weight` weights by points whenever every unit has points
  and either side has several datasheets. When both sides have one datasheet each, every weight stays 1. This affects
  13 of the random battles of seeds 1–200 (a side of only Chaos Lords or only Hive Tyrants). Normal vs Normal over
  seeds 1–200: 37 / 163 (M72: 39 / 161).
- **Budgets:** no change (D-080 part 3). Over 840 instrumented games:
  - `kMaxLoadouts`: never capped (at most 49 of 64 combinations);
  - `kMaxAdvanceSteps`: never exhausted (8 of 64 steps);
  - `move_search_units`: 1.1% of Hard's unit moves were made as Normal;
  - the rollout budget: scaled in 91% of Monte Carlos, never cut (the longest rollout 167 of 400 actions).
- **Tests** (`tests/ai/m73_test.cpp`, 11 plus 2 disabled):
  - `M73_HardGuard.*`:
    - `ShippedScenariosAreUnderTheBudget` prints the five scenarios' figures and pins them: V1 and
      `bot_termagants_first` 1 table of 2.1 MB, `bot_20_termagants` 4.2 MB, Strike 20.6 MB, the rosters 30 of 525.1 MB.
    - `ChainPlanSizeIsTheFullPlanWithoutBuildingIt` holds it equal to `plan_chain_tables` on 64 states (random armies,
      the worst cases, V1 and the rosters, each also at a later half-turn, with units destroyed and with models lost).
    - `AnOversizedArmyAnswersAsNormalWithTheReason`: in side A's whole first turn, every action is Normal's, the prefix
      appears where Hard would decide, and no table is built.
    - `PureFunctionOfTheState`: fresh and used bots alike, the half-turns, unit types destroyed, each limit and 0, a
      lower budget that hands the rosters to Normal.
  - `M73_Weights.*`: a one-datasheet side weighted by points, both sides single at 1, the shipped scenarios unchanged.
  - `M73_Arena.RandomArmiesFinishCleanAtEveryLevel`: Easy, Normal, Hard–Normal, Normal–Hard and Hard–Hard on short
    random armies, 25 s in Debug.
  - `M73_Replay.*`: see the replay below.
  - Fixtures in `tests/data/armies/`: the most-bytes and most-tables lists and the replay's two.
  - ctest (Debug and Release): 1206 -> 1217 tests, all pass.
- **Existing test changed (one, as agreed):** `M68_Points.SharesAreWeightedByPointsOnlyForSidesOfSeveralUnitTypes`.
  Its one-type expectation (the rosters with the Tyranids cut to their two Termagant units) was weight 1 and is now
  55 x 7 / 1070. The comment cites D-080 and the user's agreement, and the test gains an assertion of the seven units.
  `M68_Tiebreak` (a Chaos Lord alone against two Tyranid datasheets) passes unchanged.
- **Replay** `tests/replays/m73_custom_bot_game.json`: Normal vs Normal, dice seed 1, 240 steps, decided on points
  after battle round 5 (a Tyranid win). Both sides shoot and fight.
  - *The armies* (non-stock, `tests/data/armies/m73_*.json`): side A, Chaos Space Marines, 595 points: a Chaos Lord
    with a power fist; 10 Legionaries with a plasma gun and a heavy melee weapon; missile-launcher Havocs; 5 Chaos
    Terminators. Side B, Tyranids, 390 points: two Hive Tyrants, a one-datasheet side under D-080's weighting.
  - *How the battle is named:* as a scenario file, `tests/data/scenarios/m73_custom_battle_44x60.json`, which is
    `custom_battle_scenario` of the two army files. M68's replay names the rosters the same way. No state is embedded,
    so the tests over every saved replay stay unchanged.
  - *Tests:* `TheScenarioIsTheTwoArmyFiles`, `TheCustomBotGameReplaysToItsResultWithCoreOnly`,
    `TheSavedFileMatchesTheGame`, `DISABLED_RegenerateCustomBotGameReplay` (it writes both files) and
    `DISABLED_SearchCustomBotGameSeed` (seed 1 is the first seed of 30 with a Shoot and a Fight from each side).
- **Acceptance:**
  - *Tests `M73_HardGuard`, `M73_Weights`, `M73_Arena`, the replay and its two tests:* **met.**
  - *Release arena, `--random-armies 1`, seeds 1–200, one run at a time, peak RSS by `/usr/bin/time -v`:* every run
    had 0 rejected, reverted, fallback or unfinished. The guard never triggers on these seeds (at most 894 MB and 49
    tables), so every run measures Hard itself.
    - Easy–Easy: 66 / 134, 16 s.
    - Normal–Normal: 37 / 163, decision p95 7.0 ms, max 174 ms.
    - Hard–Normal: 47 / 153. Hard's decisions p50 0.009 ms, p95 253 ms, max 3,164 ms. Peak RSS 946 MiB.
    - Normal–Hard: 51 / 149. Hard's p50 0.009 ms, p95 225 ms, max 2,239 ms. Peak RSS 970 MiB.
    - Hard–Hard, 20 games: 9 / 11. p95 260 ms, max 1,571 ms. Peak RSS 701 MiB.
  - *Hard peak RSS ≤ 1.5 GB:* **met** (970 MiB at most).
  - *Hard's decision max ≤ 3 s:* **open** (3.16 s in Hard–Normal, 5% over). The slowest decisions are two searches,
    not table builds:
    - seed 83's first decision (890 MB of tables, then the search): 2.66, 2.68 and 3.09 s in three runs alone;
    - seed 188's battle round 3 Move Units decisions (13 units, no table built): 2.67, 2.94 and 3.22 s.
    - Runs vary by about 15%. No budget binds there (D-080 part 3), and no gate was found that the rosters, which must
      play as before, don't share.
  - *Identity set unchanged:* **met.** The Release arena's `--per-game` output on V1, both bot scenarios, Strike and the
    rosters (30 files, 4,740 games) matches the pre-batch baseline on every `game` line. The arena binary rebuilt
    after the last (comment-only) edits is byte-identical to the one measured.
  - *Replays:* **met.** `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the M45, M46, M47, M58, M68 and M70 regenerations leave
    `tests/replays` byte-identical apart from the new file.
- **Open items:**
  - The 3 s decision maximum (above).
  - Hard's strength on random armies. As the Chaos Space Marines, Hard wins 47 of 200 against Normal (Normal from that
    seat: 37). As the Tyranids it wins 149 (Normal: 163), the rosters' M69 pattern again.
  - The budget rates are to be watched in M77's sweeps.
  - CLAUDE.md's regenerate commands, which should gain `M73_Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateCustomBotGameReplay`, and the new
    `tests/data/armies/` (M77's docs).

## M74: Deployment step in the engine ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `max` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-09), design first · **Rules:** 03.02, 03.03, 03.04, 01.03 · **Decision:** D-081 (amends D-003)

> **State.** `GameState::undeployed`: the units not yet set up, in id order, omitted from JSON when empty. They are
> not in `units`, so no query, bot or board code sees them. A unit set up moves into `units` at its id position.
>
> **Scenario.** `"deployment": "alternating"` builds the units into `undeployed`. A formation given with it is
> refused.
>
> **Flow.**
> - `Stage::Deploy` is appended after `GameOver` (rule 03.02). `start_game` enters it when `undeployed` is
>   non-empty; otherwise D-003 is unchanged.
> - The order is derived from the counts: the second-turn side first, then alternating, then the rest for the
>   side not yet done.
> - `active_side` is the first-turn side (01.03: neither player's turn).
> - Round 1 starts after the last unit.
>
> **Decision and actions** (appended).
> - `DeployUnits` is answered by `DeployUnit{unit, models[{model, position, facing_deg}]}`, a D-004 template per
>   undeployed unit.
> - `CannotSetUp{unit}` is offered only when no legal spot is found. The unit counts as destroyed, and its points
>   go to the opponent.
> - Events `UnitSetUp`, `UnitNotSetUp` and `DeploymentStarted`.
>
> **Queries** (`core/state/deployment.*`).
> - `set_up_problems` checks: each model once; round bases at facing 0; 03.03; 03.04; D-009; D-064.
> - `propose_set_up`: the auto-layout slot if legal, else a deterministic scan.
> - `find_undeployed`.
>
> **Audit.**
> - The D-007 wipe-out checks are skipped during Deploy.
> - `check_points_data` and `from_json` include `undeployed`.
> - `state_hash` covers `undeployed` only when set.
> - Unit numbering is stable.
>
> **Answers.**
> - The bots go through a level-independent `deploy_policy` (the lowest-id unit at its proposal) before any level
>   policy. The fallback gives the same answer, or `CannotSetUp`.
> - Client logic: log and action text, decision view, phase track, undo groups, and a minimal "Set up <unit>
>   (auto-place)" panel button.

**Acceptance**
- Tests `D081_Deploy`, `R03_02_SetUp`, `R03_03_*`, `R03_04_*`, `D009_*`, `D064_*`, `D081_Json`, `D081_Loader`,
  `D004_LegalActions.RandomPlayWithHandDeployment`, `M74_StateHash`, `M74_Bots`, `M74_UnitStyle` and `M74_Log`.
- Replay `m74_hand_deployment.json` (fixture `tests/data/scenarios/hand_deployment_44x60.json`, B first, a
  Tyrannofex at an angle), stopped at A's first Move Units decision.
- Arena `--deploy hand|auto`: Easy and Normal on random armies, clean. Bots deploying by hand end up exactly where
  auto-placement puts them.
- Old replays and saves byte-identical; identity set unchanged.

**Results (2026-11-09):** D-081 (amends D-003, D-007 and D-065; extends D-054 and D-079) was written first and stopped
for review; it was approved as written. The implementation follows it, and D-081 now also records the speed-ups and the
measured cost.
- **Core:**
  - *State and flow:* `GameState::undeployed` holds the units not yet set up, in id order. It is written to JSON only
    when non-empty.
    - `Stage::Deploy` is appended after `GameOver` (rule 03.02). It has its own `next_stage` case, which starts battle
      round 1 with the same code `start_game` uses for D-003 (`start_first_battle_round`).
    - `start_game` enters Deploy when units wait to be set up.
    - `advance` answers the Deploy stage before D-007's wipe-out checks, so there is no wipe-out during deployment.
    - `active_side` is the first-turn side (01.03).
  - *Decision, actions and events (appended):* decision `DeployUnits`; actions `DeployUnit{unit, models[{model,
    position, facing_deg}]}` (facing omitted at 0) and `CannotSetUp{unit}`; events `DeploymentStarted{first}`,
    `UnitSetUp` and `UnitNotSetUp` (`{unit, side}`), each with its rule and JSON.
  - *Queries* (`core/state/deployment.{hpp,cpp}`): `find_undeployed`, `side_to_deploy`, `set_up_problems`,
    `cannot_set_up_problems`, `propose_set_up` and `deployment_proposals`.
    - `zone_layout`'s block measurement is now public (`formation_block`, `block_fits_zone`), so the scan measures
      ranks exactly as the auto-layout does. The layout itself is unchanged.
  - *Engine* (`engine/deployment.cpp`):
    - `legal_for(DeployUnits)` lists each unit's proposal, or `CannotSetUp` when it has none, then the templates;
    - validation of both actions;
    - setting a unit up moves it into `units` at its id position, an oval's facing stored modulo 180;
    - a unit not set up goes into `units` with no models (`UnitNotSetUp`, then `UnitDestroyed`).
  - *Loader:* `"deployment": "alternating"`. Refused with it: a formation, any other value, an east or west zone, and a
    unit that has no legal spot even alone.
    - `check_points_data` and the state JSON cover `undeployed`.
    - The state JSON also refuses units waiting outside NotStarted or Deploy, repeated unit or model ids, a wrong order
      and a waiting unit without models.
  - *Custom battles:* `custom_battle_scenario(..., DeploymentMode)`. `Auto` writes exactly the old JSON.
- **Bots** (`src/ai`):
  - `deploy_policy` (`deploy_policy.*`) answers `DeployUnits` in `Bot::decide` before any level: the lowest-id unit at
    its proposal, or `CannotSetUp`.
  - The fallback prefers the same answer, and the driver paces a set-up like a unit's activation (0.8 s).
  - `state_hash` covers `undeployed` behind a tag, only when there are some.
  - Easy's template switch knows the new editor kind.
  - Arena: `--deploy hand|auto`, for `--army-a`, `--army-b` and `--random-armies` (exit 2 otherwise). The game record's
    starting wounds count units not yet set up, so "wounds lost" is right with `hand`.
- **Client:** `unit_style` (D-065 amended: units waiting count at their id positions); `action_text` ("Set up
  Legionaries 1 (1) (auto-place)", "Can't be set up: ..."); `event_text`; `decision_view` (`EditorKind::Deploy`);
  `phase_track` (`deploying`); the decision panel's hint and buttons; the phase bar's "Deployment (before battle round
  1 of 5)". Screenshots under `xvfb-run` (start, mid-deployment, after the last unit, both bots deploying) show the
  flow, stable unit numbers and nothing drawn for units waiting.
- **Cost (asked for at the go-ahead):** measured on the stock custom battle (`D081_Deploy.DISABLED_LegalActionsTiming`).
  The speed-ups are exact; the 1/4" grid is unchanged.
  - Typical cases, Release (Debug): `legal_actions` on a Deploy state takes 0.03 ms (0.21 ms) at the first decision,
    0.02 ms (0.18 ms) half deployed, and 0.03 ms (0.24 ms) with two slots taken, whose proposals come from the scan.
  - Worst case: the deploying side's zone covered by a test-only grid of 100 mm bases, so 9 full scans all end in
    `CannotSetUp`. It takes 15 ms in Release and 53 ms in Debug (181 ms before the speed-ups); one `CannotSetUp`
    validation there takes 3.1 ms (15.7 ms).
  - The bots' set-up decisions are under 0.05 ms in Release, even at Hard. The Debug random-play test takes 2.1 s.
  - Speed-ups, none of which changes a result: one layout and one view of the battlefield per call; rank counts that
    give the same formation are skipped; bounding boxes are tested per deployed unit, then per deployed model against
    the whole placed unit, then per placed model; the scan moves the formation's boxes with it.
- **Tests** (43 new):
  - `tests/m74_deployment_test.cpp` (28, plus the disabled timing test):
    - `D081_Deploy.*` (12): the second-turn side first; alternation, then the rest; battle round 1 with the same state
      and events as D-003's start; proposals in id order equal to Auto-place's state on the stock armies and 12
      random-army seeds in both turn orders; undeployed units invisible to the queries; buttons then templates; the
      scan when a slot is taken; `CannotSetUp` only when nothing fits, scoring its full points; no wipe-out during
      deployment and D-007 at battle round 1 (touching zones); every proposal valid after random hand placements.
    - `D081_Loader.*` (6), `D081_Json.*` (3), `R03_02_SetUp.*` (4).
    - `R03_03_Coherency.DeployedUnitMustBeCoherent` and `R03_04_Engagement.DeployedUnitMustBeUnengaged` (zones 1"
      apart: the proposal stands 1" back, because no Termagant is straight in front of an Intercessor; 3/4" back is
      refused).
    - `D009_WhollyWithin.DeployedOvalAtAnAngle` (a Tyrannofex at 0 and 45 degrees refused, at 90 and 270 degrees
      accepted, stored as 90) and `D064_SetUp.NoModelOnAnother`.
  - `D004_LegalActions.RandomPlayWithHandDeployment` (`fill_template` learns `DeployUnit`): 9 battles, including zones
    too small and touching zones.
  - `D081_Replay.*` (2).
  - `tests/ai/m74_test.cpp`: `M74_StateHash.*` (2; against a copy of M73's hash), `M74_Bots.*` (5) and `M74_Arena.*`
    (1).
  - `tests/client/m74_test.cpp`: `M74_UnitStyle.*`, `M74_Log.*` (2) and `M74_Undo.*`.
- **Existing tests changed (extended as M61 and M62 did, none weakened):**
  - `Serialization.EveryDecisionKindRoundTrips`, `...EveryActionKindRoundTrips` and `...EveryEventKindRoundTrips`
    assert the variant sizes, so they gain the new kinds.
  - `D074_Json.OldFilesLoadTheSame` exempts the new `m74_` file from "no facing in the file", as it does `m65_` and
    `m72_`: its Tyrannofex is set up at an angle.
  - The test support gains `fill_template` for `DeployUnit`, and defaulted mode arguments for `custom_battle` and
    `random_armies_battle`.
- **Replay** `tests/replays/m74_hand_deployment.json`, on the fixture `tests/data/scenarios/hand_deployment_44x60.json`:
  - The fixture is the stock lists' custom battle in Hand mode, with B taking the first turn. It is written by
    `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and checked equal to `custom_battle_scenario`.
  - The 25 steps: A sets up first, the sides alternate, then B sets up the rest. The Tyrannofex is set up by hand,
    turned 30 degrees, on the next Screamer-killer's slot; that unit's proposal then comes from the scan (x 36" instead
    of 41.5"). B's turn passes, and the replay stops at A's first Move Units decision of battle round 1 (PLAN.md's stop
    point; the brief's "first Move Units decision" would have been B's).
- **Acceptance:**
  - *The tests listed:* **met.**
  - *The replay:* **met.**
  - *Arena `--deploy hand`, Easy and Normal on `--random-armies 1`, seeds 1–200, clean:* **met** (Release).
    - Easy: "games 200: side A (Chaos Space Marines) wins 66, side B (Tyranids) wins 134, draws 0, unfinished 0 ...
      rejected actions 0, UnitMoveReverted 0, fallbacks 0".
    - Normal: "... wins 37, ... wins 163, draws 0, unfinished 0 ... rejected actions 0, UnitMoveReverted 0, fallbacks 0".
  - *Bots deploying by hand end up exactly where auto-placement puts them:* **met.** `--per-game` of `--deploy hand`
    against `--deploy auto`, Easy and Normal, seeds 1–200: all 400 games end in the same final-state hash and winner.
    The hand games have 4–14 more steps, one per unit set up. Also tested (`M74_Bots`, `D081_Deploy`).
  - *Old replays and saves byte-identical:* **met.** `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the M45, M46, M47, M58,
    M68 and M70 regenerations leave `tests/replays` byte-identical apart from the new file. No state field is written
    at its default; `D081_Json` re-saves the old JSON.
  - *Identity set unchanged:* **met.** The Release arena's `--per-game` output on V1, both bot scenarios, Strike and the
    rosters (30 files, 4,740 games) matches the pre-batch baseline on every `game` line.
  - ctest (Debug and Release): 1217 -> 1260 tests, all pass.

## M75: Army builder screen ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-09) · **Decision:** D-082 part 1

> **Logic** (`client_2d/logic/army_builder.*`).
> - Edits: `set_faction` (opposing factions only), `add_unit`, `remove_unit`, `duplicate_unit`, `set_count`,
>   `split_group` and `set_option`.
> - Views: `points`, `problems`, `options` (greyed options with their reason, plus option points),
>   `unavailable_model_types`, `can_start` and `setup()`.
>
> **Screen** (`client_2d/army_builder_screen.*`, a new `Mode::Builder`).
> - Two columns, each with a faction combo, army name and points bar.
> - The units, and an add-unit list showing copies x/limit.
> - A unit editor: counts, slot combos and "+ another loadout".
> - The problems list.
> - First turn, Auto-place / Place by hand, the play mode and bot level.
> - Reset to stock, Save, Load (`data/armies` and `<data root>/armies/`), Start and Back.
>
> **Elsewhere.**
> - The start screen gets a "Custom battle…" button.
> - `new_game()` rebuilds from the stored setup.
> - `AppSettings::custom_battle` remembers the setup.
> - Flags: `--builder 1`, `--army-a`, `--army-b`, `--first`, `--deploy auto|hand`.

**Acceptance**
- Tests `M75_ArmyBuilder`, `M75_Settings`, `M75_CustomBattle` (a new game equals core's scenario) and
  `M75_LaunchOptions`.
- Replay `m75_builder_battle.json`.
- Screenshots: the stock builder, an edited list with greyed options and a problem, and a custom battle's start.

**Results (2026-11-09):** D-082 part 1 records the design and every reading taken. Client only: no rule, `GameState`
field, action, event or file format changes.
- **Core** (read-only list queries in `core/data/`, so the client re-implements no list rule, D-024):
  `loadout_weapons` (`army_rules`; the auto-layout's `make_loadout` now uses it, same output), `default_model_weapons`
  and `unavailable_reason` (`army_list`).
- **Logic** (`src/client_2d/logic/army_builder.*`): `ArmyBuilder` holds both lists, the first turn, the deployment
  mode, the play mode, the selection, and per side an edited flag and the list's source.
  - *Edits:* `set_faction` (picking the other side's faction swaps the two armies: with two factions a refusal would
    make any change impossible), `rename`, `add_unit` (core's `default_unit`; refused at the copy limit),
    `remove_unit`, `duplicate_unit`, `set_count` (each type within its range and the unit within its maximum; the unit
    may drop below its minimum while edited, shown as the 02.06 problem, so Havocs can swap a weapon type),
    `add_model_type`, `split_group` ("+ another loadout"), `merge_group`, `remove_group`, `set_option` (refused options
    can't be picked), `reset_to_stock`, `load` (not the other side's faction), `save` / `save_user` (`<data
    root>/armies/<name>.json`; `data/armies` refused).
  - *Views:* points per side and unit, problems (`army_problems` + `battle_problems`, attached to units), slots with
    option points and refusals, model types with unavailable reasons, the add-unit list (from-points, copies / limit,
    refusal), army files per faction, `preview_unit` (a one-unit battle for the datasheet window), `can_start`,
    `start_problem`, `setup`, `battle_scenario` and `battle_state`; `custom_battle_state(setup, ...)` for "New game";
    `plain_first` (problem text with the plain words first).
- **Settings:** `AppSettings::custom_battle` (`CustomBattleSettings`: both armies as army JSON, first turn,
  deployment, play mode), every key left out at its default and an army left out while it is its side's stock, so an
  old settings file loads and saves byte for byte; read key by key (a bad army is the stock army).
- **Screen** (`src/client_2d/army_builder_screen.*`, `Mode::Builder`, from the start screen's new "Custom battle..."
  button): side A, the unit editor and side B in three columns over the battle options (first turn, Auto-place /
  Place by hand with M74's step and a tooltip until M76, bot level, Hotseat / Vs bot with "You play" / Bot vs bot,
  Back, Start disabled with the reason). Each side: faction, name, points bar (red over 1000), units with D-065's
  shade and number, Edit / Copy / Remove, "Add unit...", problems, Load / Save / Reset to stock. The editor: per model
  type its count and range (unavailable types greyed with the reason), per group a stepper, "+ another loadout",
  Merge, Remove and the slot combos (refused options greyed, the reason in the tooltip), the unit's problems, and
  "Datasheet" (the game's datasheet window, D-034).
- **`main.cpp`:** Start builds the battle through core, sets the bot sides and starts a session; "New game" (toolbar
  and victory pop-up) rebuilds the custom battle from the stored setup with a new seed unless fixed; the start
  screen's Play buttons, Continue, loads, replays and Main menu forget it, so they behave as before.
- **Flags:** `--builder 1`, `--army-a <file>`, `--army-b <file>`, `--first A|B`, `--deploy auto|hand` (a custom battle,
  or the builder preloaded with `--builder 1`); exit 2 for bad values, the same faction twice, an unreadable list, a
  list with a problem (without `--builder 1`), and with `--scenario`, `--replay`, `--load` (or `--builder 1` with
  `--menu 1`). For screenshots, `--select <unit id>` selects a builder unit and `--open-slot <group>:<slot>` opens one
  of its combos.
- **Tests** (20 new): `tests/client/m75_test.cpp`: `M75_ArmyBuilder.*` (12: the stock lists at 960 and 955; playable
  defaults; greyed options with the engine's reason and option points; unavailable types with reasons; counts within
  the datasheet; a split group with two different specials and a spinefist Termagant; copy, points, CHARACTER and
  loaded-copy problems on their units; opposing factions only; save / load round trip; stock files never
  overwritten; Start only without problems; the datasheet preview), `M75_Settings.CustomBattleRoundTripsAndOldFilesLoad`,
  `M75_CustomBattle.NewGameEqualsCoreScenario` (both deployment modes and both first turns),
  `M75_LaunchOptions.*` (3). `tests/ai/m75_test.cpp`: `M75_Replay.*` (3, plus the disabled regenerate and seed-search
  tests). No existing test changed.
- **Replay** `tests/replays/m75_builder_battle.json` (scenario form, seed 1) on the fixture
  `tests/data/scenarios/m75_builder_battle_44x60.json`, the builder's `battle_scenario()` after ArmyBuilder edits:
  "Black Legion" with a heavy melee weapon Legionary (960 points) against "Hive Fleet Leviathan" with a split
  Termagant group (9 Fleshborers, 1 spinefist) and a Rupture cannon Tyrannofex (975 points), Tyranids first,
  auto-placed. The Easy bot plays both sides through battle round 1 (112 steps; both sides shoot), stopping at battle
  round 2's first decision. Written by `M75_Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateBuilderBattleReplay` (in the ai tests: Easy plays
  it); the scenario form keeps `D076_Json.AnOldFileLoadsAndSavesTheSame` unchanged (an embedded custom state would
  carry points).
- **Acceptance:**
  - *The tests listed:* **met.**
  - *The replay:* **met.**
  - *Screenshots* (`xvfb-run`, 1600x1000 and 1280x800, looked at): the stock builder; an edited list (a second Chaos
    Lord, a Havoc short) with the Lord's melee combo open showing the Daemon hammer greyed, both problems listed, the
    Havocs marked and Start disabled with the reason; the Termagants' split groups with the spinefist combo open and
    the Strangleweb type greyed; the Hive Tyrant's long slot names; the datasheet window; a custom battle's start
    from `--army-b` with Tyranids first; a hand-deployed custom battle mid-deployment. **Met**; the layout fits at
    1280x800 (one long unit name, "Chaos Terminator Squad 3", is clipped before its number, which its swatch shows).
  - *Old play unchanged:* **met.** `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the M45, M46, M47, M58, M68, M70 and M73
    regenerations leave `tests/replays` byte-identical; the only new files are the M75 replay and its fixture. The
    Release arena's `--per-game` output on V1, both bot scenarios, Strike and the rosters (30 files, 4,740 games)
    matches the pre-batch baseline on every `game` line (the client isn't in the arena: a guard against the core
    change, `make_loadout` now calling `loadout_weapons`).
  - ctest (Debug and Release): 1260 -> 1280 tests, all pass.

## M76: Hand deployment in the client ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-09) · **Rules:** 03.02 · **Decision:** D-082 part 2

> **Plan.** `client_2d/logic/deploy_plan.*`: `propose`, `translate`, `move_model`, `turn_model` (ovals), `problems`
> and `action`.
>
> **Board.**
> - The selected unit is drawn as a ghost, and the deploying side's zone is highlighted.
> - Drag moves the block; Shift-drag moves one model; Q / E and Shift+wheel turn an oval.
>
> **Sidebar.**
> - The undeployed units.
> - "Auto-place this unit" and "Auto-place the rest".
> - "Can't be set up", shown with its consequence.
> - Log lines, and one undo group per unit.
>
> **Also.** An audit of the client's `state.units` loops. The builder's "Place by hand" is fully enabled. A new
> flag, `--deploy-unit <id>:<dx>,<dy>`, makes screenshots.

**Acceptance**
- Tests `M76_DeployPlan`, `M76_AutoDeploy`, `M76_Undo` and `M76_LaunchOptions`.
- Screenshots: mid-deployment with a refused ghost, a turned oval, and round 1.

**Results (2026-11-09):** D-082 part 2 records the design and every reading taken. Client, plus one read-only core
query: no rule, `GameState` field, action, event, decision or file format changes.
- **Core** (`core/state/deployment.*`): `set_up_problem_details` gives `set_up_problems`' reasons (the same texts, in
  the same order; `set_up_problems` is now built from it) with the models of the unit being set up that cause each, so
  the client marks them without judging anything (D-024). An engagement reason still names the first pair found and
  lists every placed model within 2" of that enemy unit; only the validation path collects them, so proposals and the
  bots are unchanged.
- **Logic** (`src/client_2d/logic/`):
  - `deploy_plan.*`: `DeployPlan`, the placement in progress (a value type): `start` (core's proposal; with none, the
    formation at the middle of the zone's front, which core refuses), `from_action` (a set-up Undo took back),
    `translate`, `move_model`, `turn_model` (ovals, modulo 180; round bases stay at 0), `rotate_block` (ovals turn with
    it), `model_at`, `action`, `problems` (core's); `models_at_fault`, `deploy_choices` (the unit's proposal and, only
    when core lists it, `CannotSetUp`), `first_undeployed`, `cannot_set_up_text`, `models_and_points_text`.
  - `auto_actions`: `AutoSettings::auto_deploy` ("Auto-place the rest", per side: the first `DeployUnit` button, the
    lowest-id unit with a legal spot, only on that side's own decisions, never for a bot's side, never `CannotSetUp`),
    `is_set_up` and `run_auto_actions_grouped` (each automatic set-up its own Undo group, the human's).
  - `launch_options`: `--deploy-unit <unit>:<dx>,<dy>[:<model>:<x>,<y>]`.
- **Board and panels** (`main.cpp`, `board_view.*`, `decision_panel.*`), while a human answers `DeployUnits`:
  - the unit being placed is a translucent ghost in its shade with its number; the models core names are red, and its
    first reason shows in a strip under the unit;
  - the deciding side's zone is shaded more strongly and outlined;
  - a press on the ghost or its diamond drags the whole unit, Shift-press drags one model (with D-075's grab offset);
    Q / E and Shift+wheel turn a dragged oval, or the whole block in a whole-unit drag; Esc drops a drag; Enter or
    "Place" submits;
  - the action bar: "Set up a unit by hand (03.02, D-081)", the unit, core's verdict (all reasons on hover), and
    Place (disabled while core refuses), "Auto-place this unit" (with a proposal), Reset, "Auto-place the rest" and
    "Can't be set up" (only when core offers it, with its consequence);
  - the Game panel: the units set up, then "Side B sets up a unit (03.02, D-081)" and the units not set up yet by side,
    the deciding side's first (a click starts placing one), the other side's greyed;
  - Undo takes back the last set-up (one group per unit) and brings it back as the placement to adjust; it stops
    "Auto-place the rest".
  - "Fix illegal moves" is not applied to set-ups (conservative, D-082 part 2: core has no set-up area, and building one
    in the client would be rules logic): a refused ghost stays where it was let go, with its reasons.
- **Audit** (exe side; D-082 part 2 lists it): the unchecked `find_unit(...)->models` in the drag preview (M74's
  finding), the whole-unit preview, the move markers and the panel's shot and fight grids now check for null;
  datasheet windows find units not set up; hover, click and right-click see the ghost first. The board drawing,
  labels, hit-testing, ruler, pickers, overlays and pop-ups were checked and need no change.
- **Builder:** "Place by hand" is fully enabled; the tooltip explains the alternating set-up and the dragging.
- **Bots:** unchanged. In vs-bot play the bot's set-ups come one at a time at the driver's pacing.
- **Tests** (14 new):
  - `tests/client/m76_test.cpp` (12): `M76_DeployPlan.*` (6: `StartsAtTheProposal`,
    `WithNoLegalSpotItStartsAtTheZoneFrontAndCoreRefusesIt`, `TranslateKeepsTheShape`, `ProblemsComeFromCore` (off the
    battlefield 03.02, out of the zone D-009, a model on another D-064, incoherent 03.03, engaged 03.04 on zones 1"
    apart; the same reasons as `validate_action`), `OvalTurnKeepsFacingModulo180AndRoundBasesStayAtZero`,
    `SubmitIsAccepted`), `M76_AutoDeploy.*` (2: `TheRestPlacesAtTheProposalsAndPausesForTheOpponent` (the result
    equals the auto-placed battle) and `AUnitWithNoLegalSpotIsLeftToThePlayer`), `M76_Undo.OneSetUpPerGroup` (hotseat
    and against a bot), `M76_CannotSetUp.OfferedOnlyWhenCoreOffersIt`,
    `M76_Sidebar.UnitsNotSetUpYetShowTheirModelsAndPoints`, `M76_LaunchOptions.ParsesDeployUnit`.
  - `tests/m76_set_up_problems_test.cpp` (2): `D082_SetUpProblems.TheReasonsOfSetUpProblemsWithTheModelsAtFault` and
    `R03_04_Engagement.EveryPlacedModelWithinEngagementRangeIsAtFault`.
  - No existing test changed.
- **Replay:** none (not required): the flow's actions are M74's, whose `m74_hand_deployment.json` already sets a unit up
  by hand, and the editor makes no game state of its own.
- **Acceptance:**
  - *The tests listed:* **met.**
  - *Screenshots* (`xvfb-run`, 1600x1000, and the bar at 1280x800, looked at): mid-deployment with a ghost moved to a
    legal spot; refused ghosts (out of the zone, and over a deployed unit with only the three overlapping models red);
    the Tyrannofex turned 30 degrees (refused across the zone's front, and accepted inside it); a unit with no legal
    spot left ("Can't be set up" with its consequence, on a narrow-zone scenario); the lists of units not set up; a
    vs-bot game after the bot set up three units; battle round 1 after a hand deployment (the Tyrannofex turned 90
    degrees). **Met.** The diamond sits on a one-model unit's number, as in a move.
  - *Old play unchanged:* **met.** `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the M45, M46, M47, M58, M68, M70, M73 and M75
    regenerations leave `tests/replays` byte-identical. The Release arena's `--per-game` output on V1, both bot
    scenarios, Strike and the rosters (30 files, 4,740 games) matches the pre-batch baseline on every `game` line (a
    guard against the core change: `set_up_problems` is now built from `set_up_problem_details`).
  - ctest (Debug and Release): 1280 -> 1294 tests, all pass.
  - *Manual check by mouse on Windows:* **open** (for the user): dragging, Shift-dragging, turning with Q / E and
    Shift+wheel, Enter, and Undo during a hand deployment.

## M77: Bot deployment heuristic, final verification, docs, PR ✅ (done, with open items)
**Effort:** `high` · **Plan mode:** pre-approved (2026-11-09) · **Decision:** D-081 extended (bot deployment)

> **Bot deployment.**
> - Easy keeps the auto-layout spot in list order.
> - Normal and Hard place long-range shooters first and back, melee last and forward. Each spot is nudged along the
>   zone toward the enemy's deployed mass where `set_up_problems` passes.
> - Deterministic. A search-based Hard deployment goes to "Later".
>
> **Docs.**
> - CLAUDE.md: layout, flags, the deployment step, regenerate commands, the saved-file list.
> - README: custom battles; "interactive deployment" is no longer under "Not yet".
> - `package.ps1`'s README text.
> - PLAN.md: results, a batch summary, and the open items moved to "Later".

**Acceptance**
- Replay `m77_custom_hand_deploy_bot_game.json` (Normal vs Normal with hand deployment, to the result), with
  `TheSavedFileMatchesTheGame` and `M77_Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateCustomHandDeployBotGameReplay`.
- Clean Debug and Release builds and all of `ctest`.
- `git diff main -- tests/replays` shows only new files.
- Identity set unchanged against the Step 0 baseline.
- Arena on random armies at every level, in both deployment modes, clean.
- Screenshots.
- Manual check left for the user (by mouse on Windows):
  - build, save and reload two lists;
  - deploy by hand against the bot, turning the Tyrannofex;
  - finish a custom battle.
- Rebase-merged PR.

**Results (2026-11-09):** D-081 gains a "Bot deployment (M77)" part (the design, the readings and the measurements).
Bot and docs only: no rule, core, `GameState` field, action, event, decision or file format changes; the fallback, the
client and Easy are unchanged.
- **Bots** (`src/ai/deploy_policy.*`, `bot.cpp`): `Bot::decide` still answers `DeployUnits` before any level's policy.
  Easy (and the arena's `normal-m55`) keeps M74's policy; Normal and Hard use `heuristic_deploy_policy`, Hard exactly
  Normal's, so it builds no chain table during deployment.
  - *Roles* (`deploy_assessment`): the ranged share of the points the unit is expected to destroy (`unit_role`'s model
    equivalents against every enemy unit, weighted by its points per model). Long-range shooter: longest usable ranged
    weapon 24" or more and the share above 0.55; melee-oriented: below 0.45; the rest between. Weighting by points was
    needed: by model equivalents alone the lascannon Havocs came out melee-leaning (0.43; by points 0.68).
  - *Order* (`deploy_order`): shooters, the rest, melee units; ties by id.
  - *Spot:* core's proposal with no enemy set up; otherwise translations of it (x within 6" in 1" steps; a shooter up to
    6" back, a melee unit forward to the zone's front) ordered by `deploy_candidates` toward the centre of the enemy units
    set up (melee: nearest; shooters: the furthest back still within range, else no further back, then nearest in x;
    the rest: nearest in x), the first that `set_up_problems` accepts and that leaves every other unit of the side with a
    legal spot (`deployment_proposals` on the scratch game, at most 3 checks). The proposal is always a candidate, so
    `CannotSetUp` comes only when core lists it. Under 0.5 ms a decision in Release.
- **Tests** (14 new, `tests/ai/m77_test.cpp`): `M77_BotDeploy.*` (11: `RolesComeFromTheWeapons`,
  `LongRangeShootersFirstMeleeLastTiesById`, `EverySpotIsLegalAndNothingFallsBack` (150 set-ups on the stock armies both
  ways round and 12 random-army battles, 104 nudged), `WithNoEnemyUnitSetUpItIsTheProposal`,
  `MeleeUnitsStandNearerTheEnemyThanTheirProposal` (96 melee set-ups, 35 more than 1/2" nearer, none further),
  `LongRangeShootersStandBackWhileInRange` (29, 15 further back, never forward, never out of range for it),
  `CandidatesAreBoundedAndStartWithTheBestFacing`, `DeterministicForFreshAndUsedBots`,
  `HardDeploysAsNormalAndBuildsNoTables`, `NoCannotSetUpWhileTheEngineFindsASpot`,
  `HandDeployedGamesAtNormalAndHardFinishClean`) and `M77_Replay.*` (3, plus the disabled regenerate test).
- **Existing tests changed (planned, as M74's Results said; each comment says so):** in `tests/ai/m74_test.cpp`,
  - `M74_Bots.EveryLevelSetsUpTheLowestIdUnitAtItsProposal` is now `M74_Bots.EasySetsUpTheLowestIdUnitAtItsProposal`:
    Easy is pinned exactly as before (the proposal, the first legal action, the auto-placed state at the end); Normal
    and Hard must set up the first unit of `deploy_order` legally, with no fallback and no chain table;
  - `M74_Bots.ABotDeclaresAUnitNotSetUpOnlyWhenNothingFits` ran Normal and required the fallback's answer; it now runs
    every level (both lines set up, the third declared not set up) and requires the fallback's answer of Easy;
  - `M74_Bots.HandDeployedBotGamesEndWhereAutoPlacedOnesDo` is now `M74_Bots.HandDeployedEasyGamesEndWhereAutoPlacedOnesDo`
    (Easy only; Normal's hand games are checked clean by `M77_BotDeploy.HandDeployedGamesAtNormalAndHardFinishClean`).
- **Replay** `tests/replays/m77_custom_hand_deploy_bot_game.json` (scenario form, dice seed 1) on the fixture
  `tests/data/scenarios/m77_custom_hand_deploy_44x60.json`: the Hand-mode custom battle of
  `tests/data/armies/m73_chaos_space_marines.json` (595 points) and the new `m77_tyranids.json` (a Hive Tyrant with a
  heavy venom cannon, 10 Termagants, 5 Genestealers, 3 Tyrant Guard, a Tyrannofex), Tyranids first, so the Chaos Space
  Marines set up first. Normal vs Normal, 322 steps: the 9 set-ups (several nudged), both sides shoot, charge and fight;
  the Tyranids win on points after battle round 5 (299.67 to 168.88). Written by
  `M77_Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateCustomHandDeployBotGameReplay` (with the fixture), checked by
  `M77_Replay.TheScenarioIsTheTwoArmyFilesSetUpByHand`, `...TheGameReplaysToItsResultWithCoreOnly` and
  `...TheSavedFileMatchesTheGame`.
- **Docs:** CLAUDE.md (the intro, the layout's `data/factions.json`, `data/armies/` and `tests/data/armies/`, the
  M73 / M75 / M77 regenerate commands and saved-file list, the fetch tool's `--fetched` and `list_building`, the
  package's lists, the M75–M76 client flags, the arena's custom-battle flags, and that custom battles and hand
  deployment leave the identity set's play unchanged); README.md (custom battles and deployment under "What's
  implemented", the Custom battle button, "interactive deployment" off "Not yet", the data folders); `tools/package.ps1`'s
  README text (Custom battle, Place by hand, the `armies` folder); this section's batch summary and "Later" item 5's
  "Open items carried from M71–M77".
- **Final verification:**
  - *Builds and ctest:* Debug and Release rebuilt from clean (`--clean-first`, about 6 minutes each) with no warning in
    the project's code (Release shows one in raylib's bundled `jar_mod.h`, third-party); ctest (Debug and Release):
    1294 -> 1308 tests, all pass.
  - *Replays:* `Replay.DISABLED_RegenerateReplays` and the M45, M46, M47, M58, M68, M70, M73, M75 and M77 regenerations
    leave `tests/replays` and the fixtures byte-identical apart from the new M77 files. `git diff origin/main --
    tests/replays` (the local `main` is behind it) shows only new files across the batch: `m72_custom_battle_round2`,
    `m73_custom_bot_game`, `m74_hand_deployment`, `m75_builder_battle`, and the untracked `m77_custom_hand_deploy_bot_game`.
  - *Identity set:* **met.** The Release arena's `--per-game` output on V1, both bot scenarios, Strike and the rosters
    (30 files, 4,740 games) matches the pre-batch baseline on every `game` line.
  - *Release arena, `--random-armies 1`, seeds 1–200, one run at a time:* every run had 0 rejected, reverted, fallback
    or unfinished games (A Chaos Space Marines / B Tyranids; Hard's decision times and peak RSS by `/usr/bin/time -v`).

    | pairing | auto | hand |
    |---|---|---|
    | Easy–Easy | 66 / 134, 15 s | 66 / 134 (identical: Easy deploys at Auto-place's spots), 16 s |
    | Normal–Normal | 37 / 163, max 166 ms | 49 / 150, 1 draw; max 293 ms |
    | Hard–Normal | 47 / 153; Hard p95 241 ms, max 2,818 ms; 957 MiB | 39 / 161; Hard p95 293 ms, max 3,243 ms; 950 MiB |
    | Normal–Hard | 51 / 149; Hard p95 219 ms, max 2,355 ms; 942 MiB | 62 / 138; Hard p95 250 ms, max 2,502 ms; 999 MiB |
    | Hard–Hard (20) | 9 / 11; p95 261 ms, max 1,629 ms; 708 MiB | 8 / 12; p95 291 ms, max 1,618 ms; 707 MiB |

    The auto rows equal M73's. Hard's peak RSS stays under 1.5 GB (999 MiB at most); its decision maximum is over 3 s
    once (3.24 s, Hard–Normal by hand), M73's open item again.
  - *Does the heuristic help Normal?* Recorded, no target: hand 49 / 150 / 1 against auto 37 / 163. A scratch probe with
    only one side on the heuristic: the Chaos Space Marines alone 48 / 151 / 1 (+11 wins for them), the Tyranids alone
    48 / 151 / 1 (12 fewer for them). It helps the Chaos side and costs the Tyranid side about as much (D-081). With
    Hard, the Chaos Space Marines' Hard did worse by hand (39 against 47 wins) and the Tyranids' Hard too (138 against 149).
  - *Screenshots* (`xvfb-run`, 1600x1000, looked at): the stock builder; a vs-bot hand deployment (the human's Chaos Lord
    set up, the Normal bot's Tyrannofex 6" west of its Auto-place spot toward it, the human's next unit as a ghost with
    core's verdict); both bots' deployment complete (the last reason "D-081 (M77): set up Genestealers 8 (melee-oriented
    ...) 2" west of where Auto-place puts it ..."); a custom battle mid-game (battle round 2, points destroyed 44 / 119).
- **Acceptance:**
  - *Replay `m77_custom_hand_deploy_bot_game.json` with `TheSavedFileMatchesTheGame` and the regenerate test:* **met.**

  - *`git diff main -- tests/replays` shows only new files:* **met** against `origin/main`.
  - *Identity set unchanged:* **met.**

  - *Screenshots:* **met.**

    Tyrannofex; finish a custom battle): **open, for the user.**

## Teamwork: the bots fight as an army (M78–M83)

**Why (feedback, 2026-11-10):** in multi-unit battles every unit except the big monsters runs away; units should
team up to take out bigger threats. The code agrees:
- Normal scores each unit's move alone against its closest enemy unit (`MoveScoreContext`), and friendly units are
  only obstacles (D-066, "approximations kept").
- Hard's army value (D-067) treats every pair of units as an independent duel and orders each unit's targets by
  distance only. On the rosters, Hard's Tyranids (9 units) move as Normal (`move_search_units`, D-078).
- A traced rosters game (Normal vs Normal, `--trace 3`):
  - units out of reach score every move about ±0.00 and drift back or sideways;
  - the Genestealers, Termagants and Tyrant Guard stay 40–50" away all game, and the CSM infantry backs away from the
    monsters;
  - the monsters arrive one at a time, and the Havocs split their lascannons over three targets.
- Holding back is right for a unit alone (M68: lone Genestealers charging boltgun Legionaries deal about 0.24 and take
  0.50 back). What the bots miss is that several units committing together win, because the enemy answers only one
  of them.

**Agreed with the user (2026-11-10):**
- Normal and Hard learn to team up. Easy is unchanged.
- One-unit-a-side games (V1, `bot_termagants_first`, `bot_20_termagants`) play exactly as before.
- Strike, the rosters and custom battles change on purpose:
  - their bot replays (m58, m68, m70, m73, m75, m77) are regenerated;
  - the old play stays reproducible behind the hidden arena levels `normal-m77` / `hard-m77` (`Generation::M77`, as
    `easy-m55` / `normal-m55` were for M56).

**Run note (agreed with the user 2026-11-10: "just do all of it"):**
- M78–M83 are pre-approved as one batch on branch `teamwork` and run back to back without `/clear` and without
  per-milestone sign-off. This overrides "one milestone per session" and "agree each milestone first" for these
  milestones only.
- Each milestone is implemented by a sub-agent at the effort shown, then verified, marked ✅, committed and pushed.
- M79 and M82 are design first: their part of D-083 is written before any code and reviewed by the orchestrator.
- Tests: while iterating, only the suites a change can touch; the full `ctest -j4` before every milestone's commit;
  at the end, a Release `ctest` too.
- Each milestone is verified the usual way:
  - clean Debug and Release builds and all of `ctest`;
  - the identity set (below) unchanged;
  - `git diff tests/replays` shows only the regenerations the milestone means to make.
- **Identity set for this batch:** V1 and both bot scenarios (every pairing) match the pre-batch baseline from Step 0.
  `normal-m77` / `hard-m77` match it on Strike and the rosters.
- Missed targets are recorded as open items, never lowered. At the end the branch goes to `main` by a rebase-merged
  pull request, when the user asks.

**Pre-batch baseline** (Release arena at `c54c47c`, seeds 1–200; A = Chaos Space Marines, B = Tyranids):

| Pairing | A wins | B wins | Points destroyed A / B | Survivors A / B | p95 / max ms |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rosters normal vs normal | 60 | 140 | 352 / 473 | 19.2 / 35.5 | 11.6 / 470 |
| rosters normal vs easy | 200 | 0 | 567 / 128 | 30.9 / 10.1 | 8.9 / 759 |
| rosters easy vs normal | 3 | 197 | 317 / 888 | 2.5 / 30.3 | 5.9 / 596 |
| random armies normal vs normal | 37 | 163 | 222 / 406 | 11.6 / 19.3 | 8.1 / 190 |

**Acceptance for the batch** (Release arena, seeds 1–200, both seats; confirmed on 201–400 where tuned):
- *Teamwork visible* (M78's statistics, rosters and random armies):
  - gang-ups at least 2x the baseline;
  - lone engagements halved;
  - units that never come within 12" of an enemy unit cut by at least half;
  - shooting more concentrated.
- *Not weaker:*
  - rosters: the new Normal beats `normal-m77` in at least 55% of games from each seat, and the new Hard beats the
    new Normal in at least 50% from each seat;
  - random armies: the new Normal beats `normal-m77` in at least 50% from each seat;
  - Strike: the Marines win at least as often as now.
- *Times:* Normal decision p95 at most 15 ms and max under 1 s; Hard max under 3 s, rosters RSS no more than about
  0.65 GB.
- *Clean:* 0 rejected actions, reverted moves, fallbacks or unfinished games in every run.
- *Identity:* as in the run note; replays m45, m46 and m47 byte-identical.

## M78: Measuring teamwork ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Rules:** none (bot and arena only) · **Decision:** none

> **Arena statistics** (`src/ai/arena.cpp`): counted in `play_bot_session`'s event walk, kept in `GameRecord` /
> `ArenaResult` and printed by `format_arena`. The `--per-game` line is unchanged. Per side:
> - **gang-ups:** fight phases in which an enemy unit is engaged by two or more of our units;
> - **lone engagements:** one of our units engaged with an enemy unit while no other unit of ours is engaged with
>   that unit or within 12" of it;
> - **shooting spread:** the mean distinct target units per Shoot, and the share of a phase's shots at its most-shot
>   target;
> - **contact:** units that never came within 12" of an enemy unit, and the mean round of first engagement.
>
> **The regression switch:** `Generation::M77` (`src/ai/generation.hpp`) and the hidden arena levels `normal-m77` /
> `hard-m77` (arena only: not in the client, the settings or the save file). Hard learns to take the generation, as
> Normal does. Nothing plays differently yet.

- `M78_Arena` tests count each statistic on hand-built positions (two units engaged with one Screamer-killer is one

- the statistics' baseline is recorded here, for the batch's targets.

## M79: The army plan (Normal) ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Plan mode:** design first (D-083 part 1) · **Rules:** none (bot only) · **Decision:** D-083 (amends D-066)

> New `src/ai/normal/army_plan.{hpp,cpp}`: `army_plan(cache, state, side, generation)`. It is a pure, deterministic
> function of the live state (the bot stays stateless) and gives each of our units an optional **focus** enemy unit
> and its **partners**.
> 1. **Reach:** for each pair (our unit, enemy unit), the turns until our unit can attack it: shooting by
>    `ThreatRanges::shooting_reach_mm`, a charge by `p_charge_after_move`.
> 2. **Joint exchange** of a group on one target, over our next two volleys and the target's reply:
>    - the members' shares on the target add up (the points-weighted `ranged_share` / `melee_share`), each from the
>      turn it arrives;
>    - the target's reply lands on the one member it hurts most (it attacks one unit a phase; D-010 for ties);
>    - `MultiExchange` with the roles turned round (one defender, several attackers).
> 3. **Greedy assignment:**
>    - enemy units in order of the best joint value per points we put at risk;
>    - each takes the smallest group (at most 3 units, from those that reach it soonest) whose joint value is
>      positive and beats every member's best value alone;
>    - assigned units leave the pool, and this repeats until no group pays;
>    - unassigned units keep today's play (the closest enemy unit, no partners);
>    - ties go by unit order.
> 4. **No-ops:** with one unit a side, or under `Generation::M77` / `M55`, every unit gets the closest enemy unit and
>    no partners.

**Acceptance:**
- `M79_ArmyPlan` tests on hand-built roster positions:
  - two Legionaries units 18" from a Screamer-killer form a group, and neither does alone;
  - Genestealers, Termagants and the Hive Tyrant pair up on a lone Legionaries unit;
  - repeated calls and a copied state give the same plan;
  - one unit a side gives the closest unit with no partners;
- the plan's cost is measured (decision p95 within the batch's target);
- nothing plays differently yet (the identity set, and Strike and the rosters, unchanged).

**Results (2026-11-10):** D-083 part 1 records the design, written and measured first with a scratch prototype, then
reviewed and implemented. Bot only: no rule, core, `GameState` field, action, event or file format change. Nothing
reads the plan yet, so no play changes anywhere.
- **The plan** (`src/ai/normal/army_plan.{hpp,cpp}`): `army_plan(cache, state, side, generation)` gives each of our
  units with models a focus and its partners, plus per group the joint value, the members' alone values, the gain, the
  risk, each member's arrival and mode, and who takes the first reply. `describe` and `describe_unit` give trace reasons.
  - *Reach:* turns until our unit can shoot (gap within its longest range after its moves) or charge (a roll that
    covers the gap left with P ≥ 0.5), advancing in the turns before; the turns are cut at our turns left. A unit in the
    middle of a move is taken at its move's start, so a replan gets the same plan.
  - *Joint exchange:* `MultiExchange` with the roles turned round, strengths by wounds left in points-weighted shares
    (plain shares on Strike). Three of our volleys from first contact. Fights strike both ways. The target's reply is
    the one worst for us, before contact too, and it closes in each turn it doesn't charge.
  - *Greedy:* a group pays when value > 0.05 and gain > 0.05 over the sum of the members' alternatives (max(0, alone on
    the target, alone on their closest enemy unit)). Groups are ranked by gain per risk; a third member joins only if it
    adds 0.05; at most 4 candidates per target, the soonest to arrive. An engaged unit is a member of any group on the
    unit it fights.
  - *No-op:* M55 / M77, fewer than two of our units, no turn left, before the battle.
  - *Changed from the outline:* three volleys instead of two (with two, two Legionaries units 18" from a Screamer-killer
    come out −0.08: its first charge is never repaid); gain per risk instead of joint value per risk, and growth by
    marginal gain instead of the smallest group (prototype surplus per plan, rosters / random armies: 0.233 / 0.142,
    against 0.215 / 0.123 and 0.194 / 0.128).
  - *Changed in review:* the design first made either side's last unit a no-op; it is now only our side's, since ganging
    up on the enemy's last unit is what the plan is for.
- **Measured** (the test fixtures, Release; values in points-weighted shares):

  | Position | Alone | Together | Plan |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | both Legionaries units 12" from a Screamer-killer | −0.30 / −0.90 | +0.48 | a pair |
  | the same at 16" (the test) | −0.70 / −0.53 | +0.22 | a pair |
  | the same at 18" (PLAN.md's wording) | −0.92 / −0.43 | +0.06 | a pair, 0.01 over the margin |
  | Hive Tyrant 14" from a lone Legionaries unit, full strength | Tyrant +1.16, Genestealers −0.37, Termagants −0.37 | Termagants + Tyrant add < 0.05; Genestealers + Tyrant less than the Tyrant alone | no group |
  | the same, the Tyrant at 3 wounds | Tyrant +0.38 | Genestealers + Tyrant +0.88 (apart +0.38) | Genestealers + Tyrant |
  | both Genestealers units 12" from the boltgun Legionaries | −0.42 / −0.42 | below 0 | no group |
  | the chainsword Legionaries engaged with a Screamer-killer, the boltgun unit 10" away | −0.04 / +0.13 | +0.38 (apart +0.09) | a pair, the engaged unit its seed |

  So PLAN.md's two worded cases are tested where they hold firmly. The Screamer-killer pair is tested at 16" rather than
  18", where it rests on a +0.06 margin. "Genestealers, Termagants and the Hive Tyrant pair up" is two tests: a
  full-strength Tyrant wins alone and takes no partners, a wounded one pairs with the Genestealers. In games (Normal vs
  Normal, the plan at every decision) 22% of the rosters' units and 23% of random armies' have partners; the groups seen
  include three monsters on the Chaos Terminators, and the Havocs with a Legionaries unit on a Screamer-killer.
- **Cost** (Release, the plan at every decision of Normal vs Normal games: rosters seeds 1–10, 5,592 calls; random
  armies seeds 1–40, 14,101 calls):

  | | mean | p95 | max (ms) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | rosters | 0.66 | 1.34 | 2.95 |
  | random armies | 0.45 | 1.27 | 3.34 |

  One earlier random-army run had a single 13.2 ms call that didn't recur; a rerun timing every slow call five more times
  found none. Debug, printed by `TheCostIsMeasured`: about 14 ms. Normal's decision p95 is about 9–11 ms, so with the
  plan computed once per decision (M80, M81) it stays within the batch's 15 ms; Hard (M82) must compute it once per
  decision, never per evaluated state.
- **Tests** (12 new, `tests/ai/m79_test.cpp`):
  - `M79_ArmyPlan.TwoLegionariesUnitsTeamUpOnAScreamerKiller`, `AFullStrengthHiveTyrantNeedsNoPartners`,
    `AWoundedHiveTyrantPairsWithGenestealers`, `ALosingGroupIsNotFormed`, `AnEngagedUnitJoinsAsASeed`,
    `AGroupGangsUpOnTheEnemysLastUnit`, `ASideWithOneUnitHasNoPlan`;
  - `TheSameStateGivesTheSamePlan`: repeated calls, a copied state and a fresh cache at four states (one from a played
    rosters game), and the middle of a move;
  - `OneUnitASideIsTheClosestWithNoPartners`, `TheM77SwitchGivesTheClosestWithNoPartners` (M77, M55, and before the
    battle);
  - `StrikeWithoutPointsUsesShares` (no points: the group's risk is 0.7 + 0.3 + 0.8 shares);
  - `TheCostIsMeasured` (printed).

  No existing test changed.
- **Acceptance:**
  - `M79_ArmyPlan` tests on hand-built roster positions: met, with the wording changes above:
    - two Legionaries units form a group on a Screamer-killer and neither does alone (16", not 18");
    - the Genestealers pair with a wounded Hive Tyrant, and a full-strength one needs no partners;
    - repeated calls and a copied state give the same plan;
    - one unit a side gives the closest unit with no partners.
  - The plan's cost is measured: met (the table above). Decision times are unchanged, since nothing calls it yet.
  - Nothing plays differently: met.
    - The identity set: all 30 files match the pre-batch baseline at the current levels, and all 30 at the m77 levels
      (4,740 games each).
    - Every replay regenerates byte-identically (`git status tests/` shows only the new test file).
    - The final Release build's per-game lines for rosters and Strike Normal vs Normal (seeds 1–20) match too.
  - Debug `ctest`: 1327 tests, all pass. Release builds.

## M80: Normal moves as a group ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `xhigh` · **Rules:** none (bot only) · **Decision:** D-083 part 2 (amends D-066, D-077)

> Only for a unit with partners (`move_score.*`, `movement.*`):
> - **The line follows the focus**, not the closest unit. A closer enemy unit joins the others (D-066) like any
>   other.
> - **Support:** the partners' expected volleys on the focus in our next turn are credited on the ScoreLine (new
>   `ScoreLine::allies_hit`, beside `others_hit`). The focus then replies weaker, and our charge meets fewer models.
> - **Shared reply:** the focus's reply counts on this unit only if this unit would be its closest target in the
>   group after the move (D-066's closest-target rule, inside the group).
> - **Synchronisation:** a unit that ends in the focus's charge or shooting reach while no partner can reach the
>   focus by our next turn gets no support credit, so it waits at the edge of reach for its partners.
> - **Approach and cohesion,** small `MoveWeights` terms that are 0 for a unit without partners (Hard's
>   `BotWeights::move` is unchanged for those units). They fix the ±0.00 drift beyond the horizon:
>   - `approach`: x the turns saved towards the focus;
>   - `cohesion`: x max(0, gap to the nearest partner − 6").
> - **Candidates:** directions sorted towards the focus centroid, formations on that line, and a few spots beside the
>   nearest partner.
> - **Activation order:** unchanged (the closest to an enemy first). Later partners plan from where earlier ones
>   ended.
> - The move reason names the focus and partners.

**Acceptance:**
- `M80_Normal` tests:
  - two Legionaries units advance together on a Screamer-killer and end within 6" of each other;
  - a lone Legionaries unit, its partner 20" back, does not step into the Screamer-killer's charge reach;
  - far units close in instead of drifting;
- M56 and M68 tests that pin pre-batch choices run with `Generation::M77` (assertions unchanged), with a new test for
  each new choice;
- the arena's teamwork statistics move towards the batch's targets on the rosters and random armies;
- `normal-m77` matches the pre-batch baseline.

**Results (2026-11-10):** D-083 part 2 records the design, the measurements and the dead ends. Bot only: no rule, core,
`GameState` field, action, event or file format change. The first play change of the batch, on the rosters and custom
battles; V1, both bot scenarios, Strike and every Easy vs Easy game play exactly as before.
- **What changed** (`src/ai/normal/move_score.{hpp,cpp}`, `movement.{hpp,cpp}`, `hard/hard_bot.{hpp,cpp}`):
  - the army plan is computed once per Move Units decision (Normal's and Hard's) and read by every move score of it;
  - a unit **follows its group** when it has partners, the group's gain is at least a fifth of its risk
    (`MoveWeights::follow` 0.2), and (under D-007) it doesn't lose its exchange with the focus; every other unit scores
    exactly as before;
  - while some partner reaches the focus by our next turn the **line follows the focus** (the closest enemy unit joins
    the others), the partners' volleys are credited (`ScoreLine::allies_hit`: the focus replies weaker, our hits are
    capped by what is left of it), and the focus's **reply is shared** 1/k with the synced partners in its reach;
    until then the unit keeps its closest enemy unit's line;
  - `approach` (0.05 x the turns saved towards the focus beyond the horizon) and `cohesion` (0.005 x the inches beyond 6"
    from the nearest partner); candidates on the focus's line and beside the nearest partner; an off-focus charge valued
    by the charge planner; reasons that name the focus and partners;
  - `plan_unit_move`, `decide_move_units` and `continue_unit_move` take the score's `MoveWeights`; the arena's Hard
    `--weights` takes `approach`, `cohesion` and `follow`.
- **Arena** (Release, 200 games a row, 4 runs in parallel; the new Normal against `normal-m77`; A = Chaos Space Marines,
  B = Tyranids; in brackets the pre-batch play on the same seeds):

  | Pairing (seeds) | Wins A / B | Points A / B | Gang-ups A / B | Lone engagements A / B | Never within 12" A / B | First engagement (games without) | Normal p95 / max ms |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | rosters new vs m77 (1–200) | 65 / 135 (60 / 140) | 362 / 483 (352 / 473) | 0.02 / 0.38 | 0.75 / 0.93 | 1.60 / 6.38 | 4.11 (36) | 11.5 / 277 |
  | rosters new vs m77 (201–400) | 63 / 137 (70 / 130) | 362 / 485 (366 / 458) | 0.01 / 0.36 | 0.71 / 0.95 | 1.55 / 6.38 | 4.05 (40) | 11.6 / 528 |
  | rosters m77 vs new (1–200) | 56 / 144 (60 / 140) | 352 / 490 (352 / 473) | 0.04 / 0.42 | 0.70 / 0.64 | 1.65 / 6.35 | 4.32 (35) | 6.7 / 68 |
  | rosters m77 vs new (201–400) | 61 / 139 (70 / 130) | 354 / 487 (366 / 458) | 0.02 / 0.42 | 0.69 / 0.72 | 1.69 / 6.33 | 4.35 (38) | 6.8 / 130 |

## M81: Focus fire and joint charges (Normal) ✅ (done)
**Effort:** `high` · **Rules:** none (bot only) · **Decision:** D-083 part 3

> - **Shooting** (`choose_shooting`):
>   - a plan's value adds a finishing bonus (the target's future attacks removed: its reply rate x the share killed)
>     and a small bonus for the side's focus targets;
>   - a unit splits its weapons over several targets only when that beats concentrating by a margin;
>   - units shooting at a focus target shoot first.
> - **Charges** (`charge.*`):
>   - a partner already engaged with the target is credited: our side's fight includes its melee (`side_melee`), and
>     the target's fight back is split;
>   - a partner that may still charge the target this phase is credited at its charge odds;
>   - the best-odds charger goes first, so the others follow it in.

**Acceptance:**
- `M81_Shooting` / `M81_Charge` tests:
  - the Havocs put every lascannon on one monster rather than three;
  - a second unit charges a Screamer-killer that a partner already fights, where alone it would not;
- the shooting-spread and gang-up statistics improve on M80's;
- not weaker than M80 against `normal-m77`.

**Results (2026-11-10):** D-083 part 3 records the design, the measurements and what was dropped. Bot only: no rule,
core, `GameState` field, action, event or file format change. V1, both bot scenarios and every Easy vs Easy game play
exactly as before; Strike changes on purpose (Normal and Hard), as do the rosters and custom battles.
- **What changed:**
  - **Focus fire** (`src/ai/normal/shooting.{hpp,cpp}`): `choose_shooting` takes the generation and builds a
    `ShootingAim` once per Shoot decision (inactive under M55 / M77 and with one unit a side). A declaration's value adds
    a **finishing bonus**: each target's reply (its next turn's attack on the unit of ours it hurts most, valued as the
    move score values a reply) x the enemy's turns left (at most `kFinishTurns` 3) x its expected models destroyed / its
    models; and a **focus bonus** of 0.1 x the share destroyed of an army-plan group's target. A split declaration must
    beat the best concentrated one (each model's loadouts with the most shots at one target) by `kSplitMargin` 0.02,
    and a unit shooting at a focus target shoots first. Reasons: ", on the focus Screamer-killer 3", ", to finish
    Screamer-killer 7 (77% to destroy it)", ", concentrated on Chaos Lord 2 (a split is worth no more)".
  - **Joint charges** (`src/ai/normal/charge.{hpp,cpp}`): `charge_credit` finds a charge's partners at one target: our
    units engaged with it (weight 1) and, at the Charge decision, the charger's plan partners on it that may still
    declare a charge (weight: their charge odds). `charged_value` then values the fight of the charger and its partners
    (the target's fight back split by weight) less the partners' fight without it. `decide_charge` declares, of the
    units gaining at the best one's target, the best odds first. Reasons: "...; joins Legionaries 1 against
    Screamer-killer 1", "...; Legionaries 2 may follow (92% charge odds); the best odds of the units charging
    Screamer-killer 1, ahead of Legionaries 2".
  - **Hard** plays Normal's shooting in its decisions and its rollouts (`monte_carlo` takes the generation; `hard-m77`
    passes `M77`) and Normal's charges as its default, so it changes too.
- **Tuning** (seeds 1–400 together, every candidate in both seats, D-083 part 3): the finishing bonus is the lever;
  weighting it by the enemy's turns left (at most 3) was best in every seat. The joint charges move the wins by no more
  than the noise but raise the gang-ups. M80's follow gate (0.2) was re-measured at 0.1 and 0 with M81's terms: no
  consistent gain, kept. **Confirmed on seeds 401–800** (not used to tune), the new Normal's side's wins against
  `normal-m77`, M81 / M80: rosters Chaos Space Marines 130 / 102, Tyranids 312 / 281; random armies Chaos Space Marines
  103 / 86, Tyranids 316 / 311.
- **Arena** (Release, 200 games a row, 4 runs in parallel; the new Normal against `normal-m77` unless named; A = Chaos

  seat), by a much smaller margin than before the batch (90% / 73%). Hard's p95 rose (284 ms against 133 for the Chaos

  wounded).

  `AUnitFinishesAWoundedTarget`, `ShootingAtTheFocusGoesFirst`, `OneUnitASideShootsAsBefore`;
  `M81_Charge.ASecondUnitJoinsAFightAPartnerIsIn`, `TheBestOddsChargerGoesFirst`; each checks the M77 switch's choice

    keeps its shooting check (the new game has no fight; the fight check moved to the pre-batch game), as M80 did for
    m68 and m70. `play_strike_bot_game` and `play_custom_bot_game` take a generation.
  - No test of a shooting or charge choice needed `Generation::M77`: none failed (the brief's list is empty).

  win in battle round 4), `m68_rosters_normal_bot_game.json`, `m70_rosters_bot_game.json` (Hard), `m73_custom_bot_game.json`
  (no fight now) and `m77_custom_hand_deploy_bot_game.json`, each ending in a result. Byte-identical: every core replay,
  m45, m46, m47, m75 and every fixture scenario.

  of 30 files; Strike's and the rosters' five Normal/Hard files each differ on purpose, Strike now too); at the m77
  levels all 30 files match (4,740 games). `M78_Regression.TheM77LevelsMatchThePreBatchHashes` passes unchanged.
- **Acceptance:**
  - `M81_Shooting` / `M81_Charge` tests: met. The Havocs put every lascannon (and the plasma gun) on one monster where
    the pre-batch Normal splits them over three (a hand-built position: three monsters 14" away; in the traced game seed
    3 the current Normal still splits them over two in battle round 2, worth more there by the margin); a second unit
    charges a Screamer-killer its partner fights, where alone it doesn't (and doesn't with the partner 20" back).
  - Shooting spread and gang-ups improve on M80's: shooting spread met everywhere (the Chaos Space Marines' targets per
    Shoot 1.14 against 1.50–1.53 on the rosters, 1.28–1.32 against 1.59–1.62 on random armies; the top-target shares
    up). Gang-ups partly. The new Normal's side against `normal-m77`, up or level in every seat: rosters Chaos Space
    Marines 0.09–0.12 a game (M80 0.01–0.02; the batch's target 0.08 met), Tyranids 0.48–0.54 (0.42); random armies Chaos
    Space Marines 0.03 (0.02–0.04), Tyranids 0.17–0.18 (0.14–0.17). New against new, the Chaos Space Marines' up (rosters
    0.12 against 0.05) and the Tyranids' down (rosters 0.36 against 0.51, random armies 0.15 against 0.26): the Chaos
    Space Marines now shoot the monsters down before they gang up. Open for M82–M83: the Tyranids' gang-ups against the
    new Normal, lone engagements (the new side's rosters 0.67–0.71, not halved) and units never within 12" (the rosters'
    Chaos Space Marines 1.76–1.93 against M80's 1.55–1.60; the Tyranids 6.24–6.39, level).
  - Not weaker than M80 against `normal-m77`: met, in every seat (seeds 1–400: +18, +20, +10, 0; seeds 401–800: +28,
    +31, +17, +5). The batch's 55% / 50% targets stay open (the rosters' Chaos Space Marines win 37%, the random armies'
    21%).
  - Normal decision p95 at most 15 ms and max under 1 s: met (rosters 13.1 ms / 451 ms, random armies 6.6 ms / 319 ms).
  - `normal-m77` matches the pre-batch baseline: met (all 30 files, `hard-m77` too).
  - Debug `ctest`: 1346 tests, all pass. Release builds.
